## XXII A Good Finish / 二十二 完美的收场

===Original===
“Final, Lightweights,” shouted the referee.
A murmur of interest from the ringside chairs.
“R. D. Sheen, Wrykyn College.”
Sheen got his full measure of applause this time. His victories in the preliminary bouts had won him favour with the spectators.
“J. Peteiro, Ripton School.”
“Go it, Ripton!” cried a voice from near the door. The referee frowned in the direction of this audacious partisan, and expressed a hope that the audience would kindly refrain from comment during the rounds.
===Chinese===
“轻量级决赛。”裁判喊道。
拳台边的座位上响起一阵饶有兴味的低语。
“R. D. 希恩，雷金学院。”
这一次，希恩赢得了满场喝彩。他在预赛中的几场胜利已经博得了观众的好感。
“J. 佩泰罗，里普顿中学。”
“里普顿，加油！”门口附近有人喊道。裁判朝这胆大包天的偏心看客皱了皱眉，并表示希望各位观众在回合进行期间好自为之，勿加评论。

===Original===
Then he turned to the ring again, and announced the names a second time.
“Sheen﻿–﻿Peteiro.”
The Ripton man was sitting with a hand on each knee, listening to the advice of his school instructor, who had thrust head and shoulders through the ropes, and was busy impressing some point upon him. Sheen found himself noticing the most trivial things with extraordinary clearness. In the front row of the spectators sat a man with a parti-coloured tie. He wondered idly what tie it was. It was rather like one worn by members of Templar’s house at Wrykyn. Why were the ropes of the ring red? He rather liked the colour. There was a man lighting a pipe. Would he blow out the match or extinguish it with a wave of the hand? What a beast Peteiro looked. He really was a nigger. He must look out for that right of his. The straight left. Push it out. Straight left ruled the boxing world. Where was Joe? He must have missed the train. Or perhaps he hadn’t been able to get away. Why did he want to yawn, he wondered.
“Time!”
The Ripton man became suddenly active. He almost ran across the ring. A brief handshake, and he had penned Sheen up in his corner before he had time to leave it. It was evident what advice his instructor had been giving him. He meant to force the pace from the start.
The suddenness of it threw Sheen momentarily off his balance. He seemed to be in a whirl of blows. A sharp shock from behind. He had run up against the post. Despite everything, he remembered to keep his guard up, and stopped a lashing hit from his antagonist’s left. But he was too late to keep out his right. In it came, full on the weakest spot on his left side. The pain of it caused him to double up for an instant, and as he did so his opponent uppercut him. There was no rest for him. Nothing that he had ever experienced with the gloves on approached this. If only he could get out of this corner.
===Chinese===
随后他转向拳台，第二次报出两人的名字。
“希恩——佩泰罗。”
那个里普顿选手双手分按两膝坐着，听本校教练的指点；教练头和肩膀从围绳间探了进来，正忙着向他叮嘱某个要点。希恩发觉自己正以异乎寻常的清晰注意到种种最琐屑的事情。前排观众里坐着个系杂色领带的人。他心不在焉地想，那是什么领带呢？挺像雷金学院坦普尔宿舍（Templar’s house）的人系的那种。拳台的围绳为什么是红色的？他还挺喜欢这颜色。有个人正在点烟斗。他是会把火柴吹灭，还是一挥手甩灭呢？佩泰罗这副模样真凶。他真是个黑家伙。得当心他那只右手。直刺左拳。把左拳直直送出去。直刺左拳统治着拳坛。乔在哪儿？准是误了火车。也可能是脱不开身。他纳闷自己为什么直想打哈欠。
“开始！”
那里普顿选手陡然活跃起来。他几乎是跑着穿过拳台。匆匆一握手上，希恩还没来得及离开自己的角落，就被他堵在了里面。方才教练叮嘱的究竟是什么，这下昭然若揭——他要从一开局就强攻快打。
这突如其来的攻势一时间打得希恩阵脚大乱。他仿佛置身于拳风的旋涡之中。背后猛地一震——他撞上了立柱。尽管晕头转向，他仍记得抬手护住自己，挡住了对手左手抽来的一记狠拳。可对方的右手他却来不及招架。那一拳直捣进来，结结实实砸在他左侧最不禁打的部位。剧痛使他霎时弯下腰去，对手趁势又是一记上钩拳。他得不到片刻喘息。他戴着拳套经历过的种种，没有一样及得上这个。要是能冲出这个角落就好了。

===Original===
Then, almost unconsciously, he recalled Joe Bevan’s advice.
“If a man’s got you in a corner,” Joe had said, “fall on him.”
Peteiro made another savage swing. Sheen dodged it and hurled himself forward.
“Break away,” said a dispassionate official voice.
Sheen broke away, but now he was out of the corner with the whole good, open ring to manoeuvre in.
He could just see the Ripton instructor signalling violently to his opponent, and, in reply to the signals, Peteiro came on again with another fierce rush.
===Chinese===
就在这时，他几乎是无意识地想起了乔·贝文的忠告。
“要是有人把你逼进了角落，”乔曾说过，“就整个儿扑上去缠住他。”
佩泰罗又是一记凶狠的挥拳。希恩闪身躲过，猛地扑了上去。
“分开。”一个公事公办、不带感情的声音说道。
希恩撤开了身，而此刻他已脱出角落，整片开阔的好场子任他周旋。
他瞥见里普顿教练正朝他的对手拼命打手势，而佩泰罗应着手势，又凶猛地冲杀过来。

===Original===
But Sheen in the open was a different person from Sheen cooped up in a corner. Francis Hunt had taught him to use his feet. He sidestepped, and, turning quickly, found his man staggering past him, overbalanced by the force of his wasted blow. And now it was Sheen who attacked, and Peteiro who tried to escape. Two swift hits he got in before his opponent could face round, and another as he turned and rushed. Then for a while the battle raged without science all over the ring. Gradually, with a cold feeling of dismay, Sheen realised that his strength was going. The pace was too hot. He could not keep it up. His left counters were losing their force. Now he was merely pushing his glove into the Ripton man’s face. It was not enough. The other was getting to close quarters, and that right of his seemed stronger than ever.
He was against the ropes now, gasping for breath, and Peteiro’s right was thudding against his ribs. It could not last. He gathered all his strength and put it into a straight left. It took the Ripton man in the throat, and drove him back a step. He came on again. Again Sheen stopped him.
It was his last effort. He could do no more. Everything seemed black to him. He leaned against the ropes and drank in the air in great gulps.
“Time!” said the referee.
The word was lost in the shouts that rose from the packed seats.
Sheen tottered to his corner and sat down.
===Chinese===
可开阔地里的希恩，与困守一隅的希恩判若两人。弗朗西斯·亨特（Francis Hunt）教过他如何运用脚步。他向旁一闪，迅速转身，只见对手收势不住，被那记打空的重拳带得踉踉跄跄冲过了头。此刻攻的是希恩，逃的是佩泰罗。趁对手回身不及，他先得了两记快拳；待对方转身冲来，又补上一记。接下来有一阵子，一场毫无章法的混战席卷了整个拳台。渐渐地，希恩带着一股寒意惶然意识到，自己的力气正在流失。节奏太猛了，他撑不住了。他左手反击的力道正在减弱，如今不过是把拳套往里普顿人的脸上推搡而已。这可不够。对方已逼进近身距离，而那只右手似乎比先前更凶了。
此刻他背靠围绳，大口喘着气，佩泰罗的右拳正一下下擂在他的肋上。这样撑不了多久了。他鼓足全身力气，凝成一记左直拳。这一拳打在里普顿人的喉咙上，把他逼退了一步。对方又冲上来，希恩又是一拳截住了他。
这是他最后的力气了。他再也使不出什么来。眼前一片发黑。他倚着围绳，大口大口地吞咽着空气。
“时间到！”裁判喊道。
这一声淹没在看台满座观众腾起的欢呼里。
希恩摇摇晃晃地走回角落，坐了下来。

===Original===
“Keep it up, sir, keep it up,” said a voice. “Bear’t that the opposed may beware of thee. Don’t forget the guard. And the straight left beats the world.”
It was Joe﻿—at the eleventh hour.
With a delicious feeling of content Sheen leaned back in his chair. It would be all right now. He felt that the matter had been taken out of his hands. A more experienced brain than his would look after the generalship of the fight.
As the moments of the half-minute’s rest slid away he discovered the truth of Joe’s remarks on the value of a good second. In his other fights the napping of the towel had hardly stirred the hair on his forehead. Joe’s energetic arms set a perfect gale blowing. The cool air revived him. He opened his mouth and drank it in. A spongeful of cold water completed the cure. Long before the call of Time he was ready for the next round.
“Keep away from him, sir,” said Joe, “and score with that left of yours. Don’t try the right yet. Keep it for guarding. Box clever. Don’t let him corner you. Slip him when he rushes. Cool and steady does it. Don’t aim at his face too much. Go down below. That’s the *de*-partment. And use your feet. Get about quick, and you’ll find he don’t like that. Hullo, says he, I can’t touch him. Then, when he’s tired, go in.”
The pupil nodded with closed eyes.
===Chinese===
“撑住，先生，撑住。”一个声音说道，“稳住阵脚，好教对手见了你先自胆寒（莎士比亚语）。别忘了抬手护面。直刺左拳打遍天下。”
是乔——在最后关头赶到了。
希恩心头一股妙不可言的安适，向后靠进椅子里。这下不用愁了。他觉得此事已不必自己操心，自有一颗比他老练得多的头脑来照管这场战斗的方略。
随着半分钟休息一分一秒地滑过，他体会到了乔关于一位好场边指导有何价值的议论何等真确。在先前那几场里，毛巾的扇动几乎吹不动他额前的头发；乔那双有力的胳膊却扇起了一阵十足的大风。清凉的空气使他缓过劲来。他张开嘴，大口吸着。一海绵的凉水浇下去，这场救治便大功告成。远在“开始”的口令响起之前，他已备好迎接下一个回合。
“跟他保持距离，先生，”乔说，“用你那只左手拿分。先别动右手，留着它防守。打得聪明点。别让他把你逼进角落。他冲上来就侧身滑开。又冷又稳，便无往不利。别老瞄着他的脸。往下打，那才叫‘对路’。还要用上你的脚步。快速挪动，你会发现他就没了辙。嘿，他说，我怎么碰不着他。等他乏了，再压上去。”
学生阖着眼睛点了点头。

===Original===
While these words of wisdom were proceeding from the mouth of Mr. Bevan, another conversation was taking place which would have interested Sheen if he could have heard it. Mr. Spence and the school instructor were watching the final from the seats under the side windows.
“It’s extraordinary,” said Mr. Spence. “The boy’s wonderfully good for the short time he has been learning. You ought to be proud of your pupil.”
“Sir?”
“I was saying that Sheen does you credit.”
“Not me, sir.”
“What! He told me he had been taking lessons. Didn’t you teach him?”
===Chinese===
正当这些金玉良言从贝文先生口中源源而出时，另一场谈话也在进行——希恩若能听见，定会大感兴趣。斯彭斯先生（Mr. Spence）与那位学校教练正坐在侧窗下的座位上观看这场决赛。
“真是了不得，”斯彭斯先生说，“这孩子学拳的日子这么短，竟打得这么出色。你该为你的学生骄傲才是。”
“先生，您说什么？”
“我是说，希恩给你争了光。”
“不是我，先生。”
“什么！他跟我说过，他一直在上课学拳。难道不是你教的？”

===Original===
“Never set eyes on him, till this moment. Wish I had, sir. He’s the sort of pupil I could wish for.”
Mr. Spence bent forward and scanned the features of the man who was attending the Wrykinian.
“Why,” he said, “surely that’s Bevan﻿—Joe Bevan! I knew him at Cambridge.”
“Yes, sir, that’s Bevan,” replied the instructor. “He teaches boxing at Wrykyn now, sir.”
“At Wrykyn﻿—where?”
“Up the river﻿—at the ‘Blue Boar,’ sir,” said the instructor, quite innocently﻿—for it did not occur to him that this simple little bit of information was just so much incriminating evidence against Sheen.
===Chinese===
“直到这一刻，我从没见过这孩子。倒真希望是我教的，先生。他就是我求之不得的那种学生。”
斯彭斯先生俯身向前，端详那位正照应雷金选手之人的相貌。
“咦，”他说，“那不是贝文嘛——乔·贝文！我在剑桥就认识他了。”
“是的，先生，那是贝文，”教练答道，“他如今在雷金教拳击，先生。”
“在雷金——哪儿？”
“河的上游——‘蓝野猪’（‘Blue Boar’）那儿，先生，”教练答道，说得全无他意——他压根没想到，这条小小的情况，恰恰全是指证希恩越界外出的罪证。

===Original===
Mr. Spence said nothing, but he opened his eyes very wide. Recalling his recent conversation with Sheen, he remembered that the boy had told him he had been taking lessons, and also that Joe Bevan, the ex-pugilist, had expressed a high opinion of his work. Mr. Spence had imagined that Bevan had been a chance spectator of the boy’s skill; but it would now seem that Bevan himself had taught Sheen. This matter, decided Mr. Spence, must be looked into, for it was palpable that Sheen had broken bounds in order to attend Bevan’s boxing-saloon up the river.
For the present, however, Mr. Spence was content to say nothing.
---
Sheen came up for the second round fresh and confident. His head was clear, and his breath no longer came in gasps. There was to be no rallying this time. He had had the worst of the first round, and meant to make up his lost points.
Peteiro, losing no time, dashed in. Sheen met him with a left in the face, and gave way a foot. Again Peteiro rushed, and again he was stopped. As he bored in for the third time Sheen slipped him. The Ripton man paused, and dropped his guard for a moment.
Sheen’s left shot out once more, and found its mark. Peteiro swung his right viciously, but without effect. Another swift counter added one more point to Sheen’s score.
===Chinese===
斯彭斯先生一言不发，眼睛却睁得老大。回想起方才与希恩的谈话，他记起这孩子说过自己一直在学拳，还说过前拳师乔·贝文对他的练拳成绩如何激赏。斯彭斯先生原以为贝文不过是碰巧见识了这孩子的身手；可如今看来，教希恩的正是贝文本人。斯彭斯先生拿定主意，此事非查明不可，因为希恩分明是为了去河上游贝文的拳馆练拳，才越了界的。
不过眼下，斯彭斯先生暂且满足于一声不吭。
---
第二回合，希恩上场时精神抖擞、信心十足。他头脑清明，呼吸也不再一口一口地喘。这一次再不用苦苦支撑了。上一回合他吃了亏，正要把丢掉的分数补回来。
佩泰罗分秒不让，直冲进来。希恩一记左拳打在他脸上，随即退让一步。佩泰罗再度猛冲，再度被截住。待他第三次硬挤进来，希恩一侧身让过。那里普顿人顿了一顿，防守霎时放低了。
希恩的左拳再次疾刺而出，正中目标。佩泰罗恶狠狠地抡起右拳，却全无成效。又一记迅捷的反击，给希恩的积分再添一分。

===Original===
Sheen nearly chuckled. It was all so beautifully simple. What a fool he had been to mix it up in the first round. If he only kept his head and stuck to outfighting he could win with ease. The man couldn’t box. He was nothing more than a slogger. Here he came, as usual, with the old familiar rush. Out went his left. But it missed its billet. Peteiro had checked his rush after the first movement, and now he came in with both hands. It was the first time during the round that he had got to close quarters, and he made the most of it. Sheen’s blows were as frequent, but his were harder. He drove at the body, right and left; and once again the call of Time extricated Sheen from an awkward position. As far as points were concerned he had had the best of the round, but he was very sore and bruised. His left side was one dull ache.
“Keep away from him, sir,” said Joe Bevan. “You were ahead on that round. Keep away all the time unless he gets tired. But if you see me signalling, then go in all you can and have a fight.”
There was a suspicion of weariness about the look of the Ripton champion as he shook hands for the last round. He was beginning to feel the effects of his hurricane fighting in the opening rounds. He began quietly, sparring for an opening. Sheen led with his left. Peteiro was too late with his guard. Sheen tried again﻿—a double lead. His opponent guarded the first blow, but the second went home heavily on the body, and he gave way a step.
Then from the corner of his eye Sheen saw Bevan gesticulating wildly, so, taking his life in his hands, he abandoned his waiting game, dropped his guard, and dashed in to fight. Peteiro met him doggedly. For a few moments the exchanges were even. Then suddenly the Riptonian’s blows began to weaken. He got home his right on the head, and Sheen hardly felt it. And in a flash there came to him the glorious certainty that the game was his.
He was winning﻿—winning﻿—winning.
---
===Chinese===
希恩差点笑出声来。这一切顺当得简直美妙。第一回合缠上去混战，自己多傻啊。只要保持头脑冷静，坚持远距离打法，便可稳操胜券。这人不会拳法，不过是个一味蛮打的莽夫。瞧，他又照老样子，使出那套熟悉的猛冲过来了。希恩左拳递出——却打了个空。佩泰罗在第一步冲出后便收住了势头，此刻双拳齐出，逼进身来。这是本回合里他头一回打进近身距离，自然要大加利用。希恩出拳一样频繁，可对方的拳头更沉。他左右开弓，直捣躯干；又是“时间到”的口令，才把希恩从窘境中解救出来。论分数，这一回合他占了上风，可他浑身青一阵痛一阵，左肋整片钝钝地疼。
“跟他保持距离，先生，”乔·贝文说，“那回合你是领先的。一直保持距离，除非他乏了。可要是你看见我打手势，那就使出浑身解数压上去，跟他大干一场。”
最后一个回合握手时，那位里普顿冠军的神色里隐隐透着疲惫。开头几回合疾风骤雨般的打法，其后果他开始尝到了。他不动声色地开局，试探着寻找空当。希恩以左拳引击。佩泰罗抬手防守慢了半拍。希恩再来——一连两记引拳。对手挡住了第一拳，第二拳却重重落在他身上，打得他退了一步。
就在这时，希恩眼角瞥见贝文正拼命挥手示意，于是他把性命豁出去，弃了那套相机而动的打法，放下防守，一头冲进去对攻。佩泰罗顽强接战。有那么几个照面，双方你来我往，旗鼓相当。忽然间，那位里普顿人的拳头开始软了。他一记右拳正中头部，希恩却几乎没觉出疼来。刹那间，一个辉煌的确信涌上他心头：胜局已在他手。
他赢了——赢了——赢了。
---

===Original===
“That’s enough,” said the referee.
The Ripton man was leaning against the ropes, utterly spent, at almost the same spot where Sheen had leaned at the end of the first round. The last attack had finished him. His seconds helped him to his corner.
The referee waved his hand.
“Sheen wins,” he said.
And that was the greatest moment of his life.
===Chinese===
“够了。”裁判说道。
那里普顿人倚着围绳，气力全消，那位置几乎正是第一回合结束时希恩倚靠过的地方。最后一阵攻势把他彻底打垮了。他的助手们把他搀回了角落。
裁判挥了挥手。
“希恩胜。”他说。
那是他一生中最伟大的时刻。

## XXI A Good Start / 二十一 开门红

===Original===
It was all over in half a minute.
The Tonbridgian was a two-handed fighter of the rushing type almost immediately after he had shaken hands. Sheen found himself against the ropes, blinking from a heavy hit between the eyes. Through the mist he saw his opponent sparring up to him, and as he hit he sidestepped. The next moment he was out in the middle again, with his man pressing him hard. There was a quick rally, and then Sheen swung his right at a venture. The blow had no conscious aim. It was purely speculative. But it succeeded. The Tonbridgian fell with a thud.
Sheen drew back. The thing seemed pathetic. He had braced himself up for a long fight, and it had ended in half a minute. His sensations were mixed. The fighting half of him was praying that his man would get up and start again. The prudent half realised that it was best that he should stay down. He had other fights before him before he could call that silver medal his own, and this would give him an invaluable start in the race. His rivals had all had to battle hard in their opening bouts.
The Tonbridgian’s rigidity had given place to spasmodic efforts to rise. He got on one knee, and his gloved hand roamed feebly about in search of a hold. It was plain that he had shot his bolt. The referee signed to his seconds, who ducked into the ring and carried him to his corner. Sheen walked back to his own corner, and sat down. Presently the referee called out his name as the winner, and he went across the ring and shook hands with his opponent, who was now himself again.
He overheard snatches of conversation as he made his way through the crowd to the dressing-room.
===Chinese===
前后不过半分钟，一切就都结束了。
那位汤布里奇（Tonbridge）选手握手刚毕，几乎立刻就变成了一名猛冲型的双手拳手。希恩发现自己已被逼到围绳边，两眼之间挨了重重一击，直眨眼睛。透过眼前的一片雾蒙，他看见对手步步进逼过来，便趁对方出拳时侧身闪开。下一刻他又回到了场地中央，被对手穷追猛压。两人快速地过了一轮拳，接着希恩便孤注一掷地抡出右拳。这一拳并无自觉的瞄准，纯属投机一试。但它奏效了。汤布里奇选手砰的一声栽倒在地。
希恩退后了一步。这情形看着有些可怜。他原本憋足了劲准备打一场持久战，结果半分钟便告收场。他的心情颇为复杂：体内好斗的那一半巴望着对手能爬起来再战，审慎的那一半则明白，对方还是躺着不起为好。要把那枚银牌真正称作自己的，他前面还有几仗要打，而这一场速胜将为这场竞逐赢得无比宝贵的先机。他的对手们在各自的开场赛里可都是苦战过来的。
汤布里奇选手的僵直已经变成了阵阵挣扎着想起身的努力。他撑起一条腿跪在地上，戴着拳套的手有气无力地四下摸索，想找个抓扶之处。显然他已使完了全身的解数。裁判向他的副手们示意，两人钻进围绳，把他抬回了他那一角。希恩走回自己的角落坐下。片刻之后，裁判高声报出他的名字，宣布他为胜者，他便穿过拳台，与此时已缓过劲来的对手握了握手。
他穿过人群走向更衣室时，断断续续听到了几句议论。

===Original===
“Useful boxer, that Wrykyn boy.”
“Shortest fight I’ve seen here since Hopley won the Heavyweights.”
“Fluke, do you think?”
“Don’t know. Came to the same thing in the end, anyhow. Caught him fair.”
“Hard luck on that Tonbridge man. He’s a good boxer, really. Did well here last year.”
===Chinese===
“那个雷金学院的小子，是块实用的好料。”
“这是自打霍普利（Hopley）拿下重量级以来，我在这里见过的最短的一场比赛。”
“你觉得是不是碰巧？”
“说不准。反正到头来结果都一样。结结实实打中了他。”
“那名汤布里奇选手真倒霉。其实他拳打得不错，去年在这里表现挺好。”

===Original===
Then an outburst of handclaps drowned the speakers’ voices. A swarthy youth with the Ripton pink and green on his vest had pushed past him and was entering the ring. As he entered the dressing-room he heard the referee announcing the names. So that was the famous Peteiro! Sheen admitted to himself that he looked tough, and hurried into his coat and out of the dressing-room again so as to be in time to see how the Ripton terror shaped.
It was plainly not a one-sided encounter. Peteiro’s opponent hailed from St. Paul’s, a school that has a habit of turning out boxers. At the end of the first round it seemed that honours were even. The great Peteiro had taken as much as he had given, and once had been uncompromisingly floored by the Pauline’s left. But in the second round he began to gain points. For a boy of his weight he had a terrific hit with the right, and three applications of this to the ribs early in the round took much of the sting out of the Pauline’s blows. He fought on with undiminished pluck, but the Riptonian was too strong for him, and the third round was a rout. To quote the *Sportsman* of the following day, “Peteiro crowded in a lot of work with both hands, and scored a popular victory.”
Sheen looked thoughtful at the conclusion of the fight. There was no doubt that Drummond’s antagonist of the previous year was formidable. Yet Sheen believed himself to be the cleverer of the two. At any rate, Peteiro had given no signs of possessing much cunning. To all appearances he was a tough, go-ahead fighter, with a right which would drill a hole in a steel plate. Had he sufficient skill to baffle his (Sheen’s) strong tactics? If only Joe Bevan would come! With Joe in his corner to direct him, he would feel safe.
But of Joe up to the present there were no signs.
Mr. Spence came and sat down beside him.
===Chinese===
这时一阵掌声骤然响起，淹没了说话人的声音。一个背心上缀着里普顿粉绿两色标志、肤色黝黑的青年从他身边挤了过去，正登台入圈。他走进更衣室时，听见裁判正在报出双方姓名。原来这位就是大名鼎鼎的佩泰罗（Peteiro）！希恩暗自承认这家伙看上去不好惹，便急急忙忙穿上外套，又走出更衣室，好赶得上去看看这位里普顿煞星成色如何。
这显然不是一场一边倒的较量。佩泰罗的对手出身于圣保罗公学（St. Paul's）——那是一所素有出产拳手习惯的学校。第一回合结束时，双方似可谓旗鼓相当：伟大的佩泰罗挨的拳不比出的拳少，还曾被那位圣保罗选手（Pauline）的左拳毫不通融地击倒过一次。但到了第二回合，他开始得分了。以他这个体重的少年而论，他的右拳力道骇人，回合伊始便接连三记砸在对方肋部，把圣保罗选手出拳的锐气磨掉了大半。那孩子依旧勇气不减地苦撑，但这位里普顿人力气实在太大，第三回合成了一场溃败。用第二天《体育人报》（Sportsman）的话来说：“佩泰罗双手连环抢攻，大做文章，赢得了一场众望所归的胜利。”
比赛结束时，希恩面色凝重。毫无疑问，德拉蒙德去年遇上的这个对手绝非易与之辈。然而希恩相信，两人之中更为机敏的是自己。至少，佩泰罗没有显出多少心机。单凭外表看，他是个狠辣猛冲的拳手，一记右拳能在钢板上钻出个洞来。他可有足够的技巧，能破解得了自己（希恩）的稳扎稳打？要是乔·贝文（Joe Bevan）能来就好了！有乔在场边指点，他心里就踏实了。
可是直到眼下，乔那边毫无音讯。
斯彭斯先生（Mr. Spence）走过来，在他身旁坐下。

===Original===
“Well, Sheen,” he said, “so you won your first fight. Keep it up.”
“I’ll try, sir,” said Sheen.
“What do you think of Peteiro?”
“I was just wondering, sir. He hits very hard.”
“Very hard indeed.”
===Chinese===
“好啊，希恩，”他说，“第一场打赢了。保持下去。”
“我尽力，先生。”希恩说。
“你觉得佩泰罗如何？”
“我正琢磨呢，先生。他出拳很重。”
“确实重得很。”

===Original===
“But he doesn’t look as if he was very clever.”
“Not a bit. Just a plain slogger. That’s all. That’s why Drummond beat him last year in the Featherweights. In strength there was no comparison, but Drummond was just too clever for him. You will be the same, Sheen.”
“I hope so, sir,” said Sheen.
---
After lunch the second act of the performance began. Sheen had to meet a boxer from Harrow who had drawn a bye in the first round of the competition. This proved a harder fight than his first encounter, but by virtue of a stout heart and a straight left he came through it successfully, and there was no doubt as to what the decision would be. Both judges voted for him.
===Chinese===
“不过他看上去不像有多少心眼的样子。”
“一点儿也没有。就是个光凭蛮力的莽拳手，仅此而已。正因如此，德拉蒙德去年才能在羽量级上击败他。论力气，两人根本没得比，可德拉蒙德就是比他机敏。你也一样能行，希恩。”
“但愿如此，先生。”希恩说。
---
午饭后，这场演出的第二幕开始了。希恩要面对一名来自哈罗公学（Harrow）的拳手，此人在比赛第一轮抽到了轮空。这场较量比他的首战更为艰苦，但凭着一颗坚定的心和一手笔直的左拳，他顺利闯了过去，裁决结果如何已毫无悬念。两位裁判都把票投给了他。

===Original===
Peteiro demolished a Radleian in his next fight.
By the middle of the afternoon there were three lightweights in the running﻿—Sheen, Peteiro, and a boy from Clifton. Sheen drew the bye, and sparred in an outer room with a soldier, who was inclined to take the thing easily. Sheen, with the thought of the final in his mind, was only too ready to oblige him. They sparred an innocuous three rounds, and the man of war was kind enough to whisper in his ear as they left the room that he hoped he would win the final, and that he himself had a matter of one-and-sixpence with Old Spud Smith on his success.
“For I’m a man,” said the amiable warrior confidentially, “as knows Class when he sees it. You’re Class, sir, that’s what you are.”
This, taken in conjunction with the fact that if the worst came to the worst he had, at any rate, won a medal by having got into the final, cheered Sheen. If only Joe Bevan had appeared he would have been perfectly contented.
But there were no signs of Joe.
===Chinese===
佩泰罗在下一场里把一名拉德利公学（Radley）选手打得落花流水。
到下午中段，还在竞争行列中的轻量级选手只剩三人——希恩、佩泰罗，还有一名克利夫顿公学（Clifton）的少年。希恩抽到了轮空，便到外面一间屋子里与一名士兵练拳，那位老兄乐得敷衍了事。希恩心里惦记着决赛，正求之不得。两人不痛不痒地过了三个回合，出屋时，这位军人好心地凑到他耳边说，他盼着他能赢下决赛，还说自己在“老土豆”史密斯（Old Spud Smith）那里押了一先令六便士，赌的就是他成功。
“因为我这人哪，”这位和气的军人推心置腹地说，“一眼就认得上等的拳艺。您就是上等拳艺，先生，就是这么回事。”
这句话，再想到事到最坏、他无论如何也已打进决赛、稳稳到手一枚奖牌，希恩为之一振。只要乔·贝文能露面，他就心满意足了。
可是始终不见乔的影子。

## VIII A Naval Battle and Its Consequences / 八 一场水战及其后果

===Original===
What a go is life!
Let us examine the case of Jackson, of Dexter’s. O’Hara, who had left Dexter’s at the end of the summer term, had once complained to Clowes of the manner in which his housemaster treated him, and Clowes had remarked in his melancholy way that it was nothing less than a breach of the law that Dexter should persist in leading a fellow a dog’s life without a dog licence for him.
That was precisely how Jackson felt on the subject.
Things became definitely unbearable on the day after Sheen’s interview with Mr. Joe Bevan.
’Twas morn﻿—to begin at the beginning﻿—and Jackson sprang from his little cot to embark on the labours of the day. Unfortunately, he sprang ten minutes too late, and came down to breakfast about the time of the second slice of bread and marmalade. Result, a hundred lines. Proceeding to school, he had again fallen foul of his housemaster﻿—in whose form he was﻿—over a matter of unprepared Livy. As a matter of fact, Jackson *had* prepared the Livy. Or, rather, he had not absolutely *prepared* it; but he had meant to. But it was Mr. Templar’s preparation, and Mr. Templar was shortsighted. Anyone will understand, therefore, that it would have been simply chucking away the gifts of Providence if he had not gone on with the novel which he had been reading up till the last moment before prep time, and had brought along with him accidentally, as it were. It was a book called *A Spoiler of Men*, by Richard Marsh, and there was a repulsive crime on nearly every page. It was Hot Stuff. Much better than Livy.﻿ ﻿…
Lunch Score﻿—Two hundred lines.
===Chinese===
生活这回事，真是难以预料！
我们不妨考察一下德克斯特（Dexter）宿舍的杰克逊（Jackson）这个案例。奥哈拉（O'Hara）已于夏季学期末离开德克斯特宿舍，他曾向克洛斯（Clowes）抱怨过舍监待他的方式；克洛斯以他那一贯忧郁的口吻评论道：德克斯特先生执意让人过狗一样的日子，却不给他领一张养狗执照，这简直是在公然践踏法律。
在这个问题上，杰克逊的感受恰恰正是如此。
到希恩与乔·贝文先生会面之后的第二天，局面终于发展到了忍无可忍的地步。
那是一个清晨——且让我们从头说起——杰克逊从他那小床上蹿了起来，开始一天的劳作。不巧的是，他这一蹿晚了十分钟，下楼吃早餐时，正赶上人家吃到第二片面包抹果酱的时候。结果：罚抄一百行。接着去上课，他又因为李维（Livy）课文没有准备的事，跟他那位舍监——他正编在舍监的班里——闹了一场冲突。说实话，李维的课文杰克逊*确实*准备过。或者说，他并没有完全*准备*过，可他本来是打算准备的。不过那节预习课归坦普尔（Templar）先生坐镇，而坦普尔先生是个近视眼。因此谁都明白，那部小说他一直读到预习课前的最后一刻，又仿佛是无意之间顺手带了来，要是他不接着读下去，那简直就是把上天的恩赐白白糟蹋了。那本书名叫《人类的戕害者》（*A Spoiler of Men*），作者是理查德·马什（Richard Marsh），几乎每一页上都有一桩叫人毛骨悚然的罪行。这书可真够劲，比李维强多了……
午餐时比分——两百行。

===Original===
During lunch he had the misfortune to upset a glass of water. Pure accident, of course, but there it was, don’t you know, all over the table.
Mr. Dexter had called him﻿—
1. clumsy;
2. a pig;
===Chinese===
午餐时他不幸打翻了一杯水。当然纯属意外，可是你瞧，弄得到处都是，洒了一桌子。
德克斯特先生骂他是——
1. 笨手笨脚；
2. 一头猪；

===Original===
and had given him
1. Advice﻿—“You had better be careful, Jackson.”
2. A present﻿—“Two hundred lines, Jackson.”
===Chinese===
接着又赏了他
1. 一句忠告——“你最好当心点儿，杰克逊。”
2. 一份礼物——“罚抄两百行，杰克逊。”

===Original===
On the match being resumed at two o’clock, with four hundred lines on the score sheet, he had played a fine, free game during afternoon school, and Mr. Dexter, who objected to fine, free games﻿—or, indeed, any games﻿—during school hours, had increased the total to six hundred, when stumps were drawn for the day.
So on a bright sunny Saturday afternoon, when he should have been out in the field cheering the house-team on to victory against the School House, Jackson sat in the junior day-room at Dexter’s copying out portions of Virgil, Aeneid Two.
To him, later on in the afternoon, when he had finished half his task, entered Painter, with the news that Dexter’s had taken thirty points off the School House just after halftime.
“Mopped them up,” said the terse and epigrammatic Painter. “Made rings round them. Haven’t you finished yet? Well, chuck it, and come out.”
“What’s on?” asked Jackson.
===Chinese===
两点钟比赛重新开张时，记分牌上已经积了四百行。下午上课期间，他打了一场漂亮潇洒的比赛，而德克斯特先生最反对在上课时间打漂亮潇洒的比赛——其实什么比赛都反对——于是到这一天鸣金收兵时，他把总分追加到了六百。
于是，在一个阳光灿烂的星期六下午，本该到运动场上为宿舍队助威、看他们击败学校宿舍（School House）的杰克逊，却坐在德克斯特宿舍的低年级自习室里，抄写维吉尔（Virgil）《埃涅阿斯纪》第二卷的选段。
下午晚些时候，任务刚完成一半，佩因特（Painter）进来找他，带来消息说，中场休息刚过，德克斯特宿舍就从学校宿舍身上砍下了三十分。
“把他们收拾得干干净净，”言简意赅、妙语如珠的佩因特说。“耍得他们团团转。你还没抄完？行了，撂下别管，出来吧。”
“有什么热闹？”杰克逊问。

===Original===
“We’re going to have a boat race.”
“Pile it on.”
“We are, really. Fact. Some of these School House kids are awfully sick about the match, and challenged us. That chap Tomlin thinks he can row.
“He can’t row for nuts,” said Jackson. “He doesn’t know which end of the oar to shove into the water. I’ve seen cats that could row better than Tomlin.”
“That’s what I told him. At least, I said he couldn’t row for toffee, so he said all right, I bet I can lick you, and I said I betted he couldn’t, and he said all right, then, let’s try, and then the other chaps wanted to join in, so we made an inter-house thing of it. And I want you to come and stroke us.”
===Chinese===
“我们要赛一场船。”
“你就吹吧。”
“是真的，不骗你。千真万确。学校宿舍那帮小子对这场比赛气得要命，就来挑战我们。那个叫汤姆林（Tomlin）的家伙自以为会划船。
“他划船的本事一文不值，”杰克逊说。“他连桨该把哪一头插进水里都分不清。我见过的猫里头，随便哪只都比汤姆林划得好。”
“我就是这么跟他说的。至少，我说他连划船的半点门道都没有，于是他说，好啊，我打赌我能揍赢你；我说我打赌你赢不了；他说那好，试试就试试；接着其余的家伙也想掺和进来，我们就把它办成了一场宿舍对抗赛。我想请你来给我们当领桨手。”

===Original===
Jackson hesitated. Mr. Dexter, setting the lines on Friday, had certainly said that they were to be shown up “tomorrow evening.” He had said it very loud and clear. Still, in a case like this.﻿ ﻿… After all, by helping to beat the School House on the river he would be giving Dexter’s a leg up. And what more could the man want?
“Right ho,” said Jackson.
Down at the School boathouse the enemy were already afloat when Painter and Jackson arrived.
“Buck up,” cried the School House crew.
Dexter’s embarked, five strong. There was room for two on each seat. Jackson shared the post of stroke with Painter. Crowle steered.
===Chinese===
杰克逊犹豫了。德克斯特先生星期五布置罚抄时，确实说过“明天晚上”交上来。他说得又响又清楚。可是，眼下这种局面嘛……话说回来，若能在河上助一臂之力、打败学校宿舍，那等于是给德克斯特宿舍争光。这家伙还有什么可挑剔的呢？
“好嘞，”杰克逊说。
佩因特和杰克逊赶到学校船屋时，敌方已经下水了。
“抓紧点儿，”学校宿舍的船员们嚷道。
德克斯特宿舍一行五人登船。每张座板上能坐两人。杰克逊与佩因特同掌领桨之职。克劳尔（Crowle）当舵手。

===Original===
“Ready?” asked Tomlin from the other boat.
“Half a sec.,” said Jackson. “What’s the course?”
“Oh, don’t you know *that* yet? Up to the town, round the island just below the bridge﻿—the island with the croquet ground on it, *you* know﻿—and back again here. Ready?”
“In a jiffy. Look here, Crowle, remember about steering. You pull the right line if you want to go to the right and the other if you want to go to the left.”
“All right,” said the injured Crowle. “As if I didn’t know that.”
===Chinese===
“准备好了吗？”汤姆林在另一条船上问。
“稍等片刻，”杰克逊说。“赛程怎么走？”
“哦，这个你还不知道？划到镇子那边，绕桥正下方那座小岛一圈——就是岛上有槌球场的那个，*你*知道的——再划回这里。准备好了吗？”
“马上就好。听好，克劳尔，掌舵的诀窍记住了：想往右去，就拉右边那根绳；想往左去，就拉另一根。”
“行了吧，”受了委屈的克劳尔说。“说得好像我不知道似的。”

===Original===
“Thought I’d mention it. It’s your fault. Nobody could tell by looking at you that you knew anything except how to eat. Ready, you chaps?”
“When I say ‘Three,’ ” said Tomlin.
It was a subject of heated discussion between the crews for weeks afterwards whether Dexter’s boat did or did not go off at the word “Two.” Opinions were divided on the topic. But it was certain that Jackson and his men led from the start. Pulling a good, splashing stroke which had drenched Crowle to the skin in the first thirty yards, Dexter’s boat crept slowly ahead. By the time the island was reached, it led by a length. Encouraged by success, the leaders redoubled their already energetic efforts. Crowle sat in a shower-bath. He was even moved to speech about it.
“When you’ve finished,” said Crowle.
Jackson, intent upon repartee, caught a crab, and the School House drew level again. The two boats passed the island abreast.
===Chinese===
“我不过是提个醒。怪只怪你自己。谁瞧你那副模样，也只能看出你除了吃之外一无所长。伙计们，准备好了吗？”
“等我喊‘三’的时候，”汤姆林说。
此后好几个星期，两船的人一直在激烈争论：德克斯特那条船究竟是不是在“二”字令下就冲了出去。大家对此各执一词。但有一点确凿无疑：杰克逊和他的人从一开始就领先。他们一桨接一桨划得又猛又溅水，头三十码就把克劳尔浇了个透心凉，德克斯特的船缓缓抢到前头。抵达小岛时，已领先一个船身。首战告捷，领先的人把本来就使足了力气的动作又加了一倍。克劳尔如同坐在淋浴喷头底下，甚至忍不住为此发表了讲话。
“等你们划够了再说，”克劳尔说。
杰克逊正一门心思琢磨俏皮话，冷不防一桨失手划了空，学校宿舍的船又追成了平手。两条船并排驶过小岛。

===Original===
Just here occurred one of those unfortunate incidents. Both crews had quickened their stroke until the boats had practically been converted into submarines, and the rival coxswains were observing bitterly to space that this was jolly well the last time they ever let themselves in for this sort of thing, when round the island there hove in sight a flotilla of boats, directly in the path of the racers.
There were three of them, and not even the spray which played over him like a fountain could prevent Crowle from seeing that they were manned by Judies. Even on the river these outcasts wore their mortarboards.
“Look out!” shrieked Crowle, pulling hard on his right line. “Stop rowing, you chaps. We shall be into them.”
At the same moment the School House oarsmen ceased pulling. The two boats came to a halt a few yards from the enemy.
“What’s up?” panted Jackson, crimson from his exertions. “Hullo, it’s the Judies!”
===Chinese===
偏偏就在这当口，出了一件不走运的事。两队人马都把桨频提到了极限，两条船差不多已经改装成了潜水艇，两位互相较劲的舵手正对着空旷的河面恨恨地申明，这回绝对、绝对是他们最后一次自讨这种苦吃——就在这时，小岛另一侧驶出一小队船只，正好横在赛道的必经之路上。
那船队一共三条。浪花像喷泉似的朝克劳尔兜头浇下，可他仍旧看清了船上满载的全是朱迪们（Judies，镇立中学的学生）。这帮被社会放逐的家伙即使到了河上，头上也还端端正正扣着他们的方帽。
“当心！”克劳尔尖声大叫，死命拉住右边的舵绳。“别划了，伙计们。要撞上他们了。”
同一瞬间，学校宿舍的桨手们也停了桨。两条船在离敌人几码远的地方停了下来。
“出什么事了？”杰克逊喘着粗气问，一通猛划把他挣得满脸通红。“哟，是朱迪们！”

===Original===
Tomlin was parleying with the foe.
“Why the dickens can’t you keep out of the way? Spoiling our race. Wait till we get ashore.”
But the Judies, it seemed, were not prepared to wait even for that short space of time. A miscreant, larger than the common run of Judy, made a brief, but popular, address to his men.
“Splash them!” he said.
Instantly, amid shrieks of approval, oars began to strike the water, and the water began to fly over the Wrykyn boats, which were now surrounded. The latter were not slow to join battle with the same weapons. Homeric laughter came from the bridge above. The town bridge was a sort of loafers’ club, to which the entrance fee was a screw of tobacco, and the subscription an occasional remark upon the weather. Here gathered together day by day that section of the populace which resented it when they “asked for employment, and only got work instead.” From morn till eve they lounged against the balustrades, surveying nature, and hoping it would be kind enough to give them some excitement that day. An occasional dogfight found in them an eager audience. No runaway horse ever bored them. A broken-down motorcar was meat and drink to them. They had an appetite for every spectacle.
===Chinese===
汤姆林正在与敌方交涉。
“你们就不能让让路吗？搅了我们的比赛。等着，等上了岸再跟你们算账。”
可朱迪们似乎连这么短的工夫都等不得。一个块头比寻常朱迪大出一号的小恶棍，向他的人马发表了一篇简短而大得人心的演说。
“泼他们！”他说。
说时迟那时快，在一阵阵喝彩的尖叫声中，船桨纷纷拍向水面，河水开始朝雷金学院的船泼去——此刻它们已被团团围住。后者也不怠慢，立刻操起同样的武器应战。桥的上空腾起荷马式的大笑。镇上这座桥俨然是个懒汉俱乐部：入会费是一小撮烟草，会费则是偶尔就天气发表几句高见。天天聚集在这里的，正是市民中那一批心怀怨愤的人——他们抱怨“想找份差事（employment），到头来只捞到苦活（work）干”。从早到晚，他们倚着栏杆消磨时光，饱览自然风光，巴望着老天爷开恩，当天能赏点热闹。偶尔一场斗狗，便能让观众里三层外三层围个水泄不通。脱缰的野马从来不会让他们感到无聊。趴了窝的汽车对他们来说更是美酒佳肴。凡有奇观，他们无不胃口大开。

===Original===
When, therefore, the water began to fly from boat to boat, kindhearted men fetched their friends from neighbouring public houses and craned with them over the parapet, observing the sport and commenting thereon. It was these comments that attracted Mr. Dexter’s attention. When, cycling across the bridge, he found the south side of it entirely congested, and heard raucous voices urging certain unseen “little ’uns” now to “go it” and anon to “vote for Pedder,” his curiosity was aroused. He dismounted and pushed his way through the crowd until he got a clear view of what was happening below.
He was just in time to see the most stirring incident of the fight. The biggest of the Judy boats had been propelled by the current nearer and nearer to the Dexter Argo. No sooner was it within distance than Jackson, dropping his oar, grasped the side and pulled it towards him. The two boats crashed together and rocked violently as the crews rose from their seats and grappled with one another. A hurricane of laughter and applause went up from the crowd upon the bridge.
The next moment both boats were bottom upwards and drifting sluggishly down towards the island, while the crews swam like rats for the other boats.
Every Wrykinian had to learn to swim before he was allowed on the river; so that the peril of Jackson and his crew was not extreme: and it was soon speedily evident that swimming was also part of the Judy curriculum, for the shipwrecked ones were soon climbing drippingly on board the surviving ships, where they sat and made puddles, and shrieked defiance at their antagonists.
This was accepted by both sides as the end of the fight, and the combatants parted without further hostilities, each fleet believing that the victory was with them.
===Chinese===
于是，等到水花开始在船与船之间穿梭飞舞，那些好心人便从附近酒馆里唤来朋友，同他们一起扒着桥栏探头张望，一面观战一面评点。正是这些评点引来了德克斯特先生的注意。他骑车过桥，发觉桥南侧堵得水泄不通，又听见嘶哑的嗓音在给一群看不见的“小家伙”打气，一会儿喊“加油”，一会儿喊“投佩德（Pedder）一票”，不由得好奇心大起。他下了车，从人堆里挤过去，直到把桥下正在发生的一切看得清清楚楚。
他来得恰是时候，赶上了这场水战中最激动人心的一幕。朱迪船队里最大的一条，被水流推得离德克斯特这艘“阿尔戈号”（Argo）越来越近。它刚一进入够得着的距离，杰克逊便丢下船桨，一把抓住对方的船舷，朝自己这边猛拖。两条船轰然相撞，剧烈摇晃，两队的船员纷纷从座位上跃起，扭作一团。桥上的观众顿时爆发出飓风般的笑声和喝彩。
紧接着，两条船双双船底朝天，慢悠悠地朝小岛漂去，船员们则如落水的老鼠一般，朝其余的船只逃窜而去。
凡雷金学院的学生，未学会游泳不得下河；因此杰克逊一行人并无大险。而且很快就明摆着，游泳同样是朱迪们课程表上的一门——那批遭了海难的家伙很快便浑身滴水地爬上幸存的船只，坐在船上积出一汪汪水洼，朝着对手尖声叫骂不休。
双方都把这当作战斗的终局，交战者就此别过，未再大动干戈，而且各自的船队都坚信胜利握在自己手里。

===Original===
And Mr. Dexter, mounting his bicycle again, rode home to tell the headmaster.
That evening, after preparation, the headmaster held a reception. Among distinguished visitors were Jackson, Painter, Tomlin, Crowle, and six others.
On the Monday morning the headmaster issued a manifesto to the school after prayers. He had, he said, for some time entertained the idea of placing the town out of bounds. He would do so now. No boy, unless he was a prefect, would be allowed till further notice to cross the town bridge. As regarded the river, for the future boating Wrykinians must confine their attentions to the lower river. Nobody must take a boat upstream. The school boatman would have strict orders to see that this rule was rigidly enforced. Any breach of these bounds would, he concluded, be punished with the utmost severity.
The headmaster of Wrykyn was not a hasty man. He thought before he put his foot down. But when he did, he put it down heavily.
===Chinese===
而德克斯特先生重新跨上自行车，赶回家向校长大人报告去了。
当天晚上，晚自习过后，校长举行了一场招待会。莅临的贵宾当中有：杰克逊、佩因特、汤姆林、克劳尔，以及另外六位。
星期一早晨，晨祷过后，校长向全校发布了一项宣言。他说，一段时间以来，他早有意将镇子划为禁区，如今便要付诸实施。即日起，除非身为级长，任何学生一律不得通过镇桥，直至另行通知。至于河上，今后雷金学院的划船活动仅限于下游河段，任何人不得驾船上行。学校船夫将奉有严令，务必不折不扣地执行这一规矩。他最后总结道，凡越此界者，必将受到最严厉的惩处。
雷金学院的校长不是个草率行事的人。他把脚踏下去之前，总要先琢磨琢磨。可一旦踏了下去，那就是踏得地动山摇。

===Original===
Sheen heard the ultimatum with dismay. He was a law-abiding person, and here he was, faced with a dilemma that made it necessary for him to choose between breaking school rules of the most important kind, or pulling down all the castles he had built in the air before the mortar had had time to harden between their stones.
He wished he could talk it over with somebody. But he had nobody with whom he could talk over anything. He must think it out for himself.
He spent the rest of the day thinking it out, and by nightfall he had come to his decision.
Even at the expense of breaking bounds and the risk of being caught at it, he must keep his appointment with Joe Bevan. It would mean going to the town landing-stage for a boat, thereby breaking bounds twice over.
But it would have to be done.
===Chinese===
希恩听着这道最后通牒，心中一片惶恐。他本是个安分守己的人，如今却被推上了一个两难境地：要么违犯最要紧的校规，要么亲手推倒自己盖起来的空中楼阁——那些楼阁的砖缝里，灰浆还没来得及凝固呢。
他真希望能找个人商量商量。可他连一个能商量任何事的人都没有。他只能自己想明白。
这一天剩下的时间，他都在琢磨这件事，到天黑时，他拿定了主意。
哪怕越界、哪怕冒着被当场拿获的风险，他也必须赴乔·贝文的约。这就意味着要去镇上的浮码头雇一条船，如此一来，便是两重越界了。
可这事非做不可。

## XIV A Skirmish / 十四 一场小冲突

===Original===
Upon consideration Sheen determined to see Linton about that small matter of the boat without delay. After prayers that night he went to his study.
“Can I speak to you for a minute, Linton?” he said.
Linton was surprised. He disapproved of this intrusion. When a fellow is being cut by the house, he ought, by all the laws of school etiquette, to behave as such, and not speak till he is spoken to.
“What do you want?” asked Linton.
“I shan’t keep you long. Do you think you could put away that book for a minute, and listen?”
===Chinese===
经过考虑，希恩决定毫不拖延地去和林顿谈谈船的那件小事。当晚晚祷之后，他便去了林顿的书房。
“能和你说句话吗，林顿？”他说。
林顿吃了一惊。他不赞成这种冒昧打扰。一个人既然被全宿舍冷落，按照校中礼数的全部律条，就该拿出被冷落的样儿来，别人不开口，自己便不该先说话。
“你有什么事？”林顿问。
“不会耽误你太久。能不能请你把那本书放一放，听我说？”

===Original===
Linton hesitated, then shut the book.
“Hurry up, then,” he said.
“I was going to,” said Sheen. “I simply came in to tell you that I know perfectly well who sunk my boat this afternoon.”
He felt at once that he had now got Linton’s undivided attention.
“Your boat!” said Linton. “You don’t mean to say that was yours! What on earth were you doing at the place?”
===Chinese===
林顿犹豫了一下，随即合上了书。
“那就快点说。”他说。
“我本就打算快点，”希恩说，“我进来只是要告诉你，今天下午是谁弄沉了我的船，我一清二楚。”
他立刻感到，此刻林顿的注意力已全在他身上了。
“你的船！”林顿说，“你该不是说那条船是你的吧！你到底在那地方干什么？”

===Original===
“I don’t think that’s any business of yours, is it, Linton?”
“How did you get back?”
“I don’t think that’s any business of yours, either. I daresay you’re disappointed, but I did manage to get back. In time for lockup, too.”
“But I don’t understand. Do you mean to say that that was your boat we took?”
“Sunk,” corrected Sheen.
===Chinese===
“我看这就轮不到你过问了吧，林顿？”
“那你是怎么回来的？”
“这也轮不到你过问。想必你很失望，不过我确实想办法回来了，还赶上了锁门。”
“可我不明白。你是说我们划走的那条船是你的？”
“是弄沉，”希恩纠正道。

===Original===
“Don’t be a fool, Sheen. What the dickens should we want to sink your boat for? What happened was this. Albert﻿—you remember Albert?﻿—followed us up to the inn, and smashed our boat while we were having tea. When we got out and found it sunk, we bagged the only other one we could see. We hadn’t a notion it was yours. We thought it belonged to some fisherman chap.”
“Then you didn’t sink my boat?”
“Of course we didn’t. What do you take us for?”
“Sorry,” said Sheen. “I thought it was a queer thing for you to have done. I’m glad it wasn’t you. Good night.”
“But look here,” said Linton, “don’t go. It must have landed you in a frightful hole, didn’t it?”
===Chinese===
“别犯傻了，希恩。我们干嘛要弄沉你的船？事情是这样的：阿尔伯特——你记得阿尔伯特吧？——跟着我们到了那家酒店，趁我们喝茶的工夫把我们的船砸了。我们出来一看船沉了，就把能看见的唯一另一条船占了下来。压根儿没想到那是你的船，还当是哪个打鱼人的呢。”
“那么你们没有弄沉我的船？”
“当然没有。你把我们当什么人了？”
“对不起，”希恩说，“我本来也觉得这事出在你们身上未免蹊跷。很高兴不是你们干的。晚安。”
“哎，别急着走，”林顿说，“那肯定让你陷进了可怕的窘境吧？”

===Original===
“A little. But it doesn’t matter. Good night.”
“But half a second, Sheen﻿—”
Sheen had disappeared.
Linton sat on till lights were turned off, ruminating. He had a very tender conscience where other members of the school were concerned, though it was tougher as regarded masters; and he was full of remorse at the thought of how nearly he had got Sheen into trouble by borrowing his boat that afternoon. It seemed to him that it was his duty to make it up to him in some way.
It was characteristic of Linton that the episode did not, in any way, alter his attitude towards Sheen. Another boy in a similar position might have become effusively friendly. Linton looked on the affair in a calm, judicial spirit. He had done Sheen a bad turn, but that was no reason why he should fling himself on his neck and swear eternal friendship. His demeanour on the occasions when they came in contact with each other remained the same. He did not speak to him, and he did not seem to see him. But all the while he was remembering that somehow or other he must do him a good turn of some sort, by way of levelling things up again. When that good turn had been done, he might dismiss him from his thoughts altogether.
===Chinese===
“有一点儿。不过不要紧。晚安。”
“等等，希恩——”
希恩已经不见了。
林顿一直坐到熄灯，反复思量着。对学校里的其他同学，他的良心极为柔软——虽然在对待教师方面，那良心要皮实得多；一想到那天下午自己借船差点害希恩闯下大祸，他就满怀愧疚。他觉得，自己有义务想法子补偿他。
这件事丝毫没有改变林顿对希恩的态度，这正是他的本色。换作别的男孩处在类似境地，也许会变得热情无比。林顿却以冷静、近乎法官的眼光看待此事：他坑了希恩一把，但这并不构成理由，让他扑上去搂住对方的脖子、赌咒发誓结成永远的朋友。两人打照面的时候，他的做派一如既往：不和他说话，也仿佛看不见他。但自始至终，他都记着必须想个法子帮希恩一个忙，好把账重新扯平。等这个忙帮完了，他便可以把希恩从心里彻底放下。

===Original===
Sheen, for his part, made no attempt to trade on the matter of the boat. He seemed as little anxious to be friendly with Linton as Linton was to be friendly with him. For this Linton was grateful, and continued to keep his eyes open in the hope of finding some opportunity of squaring up matters between them.
His chance was not long in coming. The feeling in the house against Sheen, caused by the story of his encounter with Attell, had not diminished. Stanning had fostered it in various little ways. It was not difficult. When a house of the standing in the school which Seymour’s possessed exhibits a weak spot, the rest of the school do not require a great deal of encouragement to go on prodding that weak spot. In short, the school rotted Seymour’s about Sheen, and Seymour’s raged impotently. Fags of other houses expended much crude satire on Seymour’s fags, and even the seniors of the house came in for their share of the baiting. Most of the houses at Wrykyn were jealous of Seymour’s, and this struck them as an admirable opportunity of getting something of their own back.
One afternoon, not long after Sheen’s conversation with Linton, Stanning came into Seymour’s senior day-room and sat down on the table. The senior day-room objected to members of other houses coming and sitting on their table as if they had bought that rickety piece of furniture; but Stanning’s reputation as a bruiser kept their resentment within bounds.
“Hullo, you chaps,” said Stanning.
The members of the senior day-room made no reply, but continued, as Mr. Kipling has it, to persecute their vocations. Most of them were brewing. They went on brewing with the earnest concentration of chefs.
===Chinese===
希恩这一边呢，也丝毫不想借船的事讨什么好处。他看上去和林顿一样无意交好。林顿对此心怀感激，继续留心张望，指望能找到什么机会，把两人之间的账清算平整。
他的机会不久便来了。由于他与阿特尔（Attell）冲突一事的传闻，宿舍里对希恩的敌意并未消减。斯坦宁（Stanning）又用各种小手段推波助澜。这并不费事。像西摩（Seymour）宿舍这样在全校有头有脸的宿舍一旦露出了软肋，全校其余的人用不着多少怂恿，就会一个劲儿去戳那处软肋。简而言之，全校拿希恩这事把西摩宿舍糟践了个够，西摩宿舍的人干瞪眼没辙。别家宿舍的跑腿小子们对西摩宿舍的小子们大肆冷嘲热讽，连宿舍的高年级生也挨上了自己那一份奚落。雷金学院的大多数宿舍都嫉妒西摩宿舍，眼下这桩事在他们看来，正是讨还旧账的天赐良机。
一天下午，就在希恩与林顿谈话后不久，斯坦宁走进西摩宿舍的高年级活动室，一屁股坐在桌子上。高年级活动室对别家宿舍的人跑来坐在他们的桌子上、仿佛买下了那件摇摇晃晃的家什一事颇有异议；但斯坦宁打架能打的名声摆在那里，他们只好把怨气按在限度之内。
“喂，各位，”斯坦宁说。
高年级活动室的众人没有搭腔，只是照吉卜林（Kipling）先生的说法，继续“迫害”各自的正业。他们多数在煮茶，便带着大厨般的专注继续煮茶。

===Original===
“You’re a cheery lot,” said Stanning. “But I don’t wonder you’ve got the hump. I should be a bit sick if we’d got a skunk like that in our house. Heard the latest?”
Some lunatic said, “No. What?” thereby delivering the day-room bound into the hands of the enemy.
“Sheen’s apologised to Attell.”
There was a sensation in the senior day-room, as Stanning had expected. He knew his men. He was perfectly aware that any story which centred round Sheen’s cowardice would be believed by them, so he had not troubled to invent a lie which it would be difficult to disprove. He knew that in the present state of feeling in the house Sheen would not be given a hearing.
“No!” shouted the senior day-room.
===Chinese===
“你们这帮人可真够快活的，”斯坦宁说，“不过我难怪你们闷闷不乐。要是我们宿舍也有那么个懦夫，我心里也得堵得慌。听说最新消息了吗？”
有个没脑子的应了一句：“没有。什么消息？”就这样把整个活动室拱手送到了敌人手里。
“希恩向阿特尔赔罪了。”
活动室里一片哗然，正如斯坦宁所料。他摸得透这帮人。他完全清楚，凡是围着希恩胆怯做文章的传闻，他们一概都会相信，所以他也就犯不着费事编造一个难以拆穿的谎。他知道，以眼下宿舍里的情绪，是不会有人肯听希恩分辩的。
“不会吧！”高年级活动室齐声嚷道。

===Original===
This was the last straw. The fellow seemed to go out of his way to lower the prestige of the house.
“Fact,” said Stanning. “I thought you knew.”
He continued to sit on the table, swinging his legs, while the full horror of his story sunk into the senior day-room mind.
“I wonder you don’t do something about it. Why don’t you touch him up? He’s not a prefect.”
But they were not prepared to go to that length. The senior day-room had a great respect both for Drummond’s word and his skill with his hands. He had said he would slay anyone who touched Sheen, and they were of opinion that he would do it.
===Chinese===
这真是忍无可忍了。这家伙似乎一门心思要把宿舍的名望往低处拖。
“千真万确，”斯坦宁说，“我还当你们都知道了。”
他继续坐在桌子上晃着两条腿，任他那故事的可怖之处渗进高年级活动室众人的心里。
“怪的是你们居然无所作为。怎么不去教训他一顿？他又不是级长。”
但他们不打算做到那个地步。对德拉蒙德的言出必行和手上功夫，高年级活动室的人是深怀敬意的。他说过谁碰希恩就要谁的命，他们认定他说到做到。

===Original===
“He isn’t in,” said one of the brewers, looking up from his toasting-fork. “His study door was open when I passed.”
“I say, why not rag his study?” suggested another thickly, through a mouthful of toast.
Stanning smiled.
“Good idea,” he said.
It struck him that some small upheaval of Sheen’s study furniture, coupled with the burning of one or two books, might check to some extent that student’s work for the Gotford. And if Sheen could be stopped working for the Gotford, he, Stanning, would romp home. In the matter of brilliance there was no comparison between them. It was Sheen’s painful habit of work which made him dangerous.
===Chinese===
“他不在，”一个煮茶的说，一面从烤面包叉上抬起头来，“我路过时他书房门开着。”
“我说，干脆去把他书房砸了怎么样？”另一个含着满嘴吐司、口齿不清地提议。
斯坦宁笑了。
“好主意。”他说。
他想到，把希恩书房的家什小小翻搅一番，再烧掉一两本书，也许能在一定程度上妨害那位学子备考戈特福德（Gotford）奖学金。只要希恩没法备考戈特福德，他斯坦宁便可轻骑夺魁。论天资聪颖，两人根本没得可比；正是希恩那要命的发奋用功，才让他成了威胁。

===Original===
Linton had been listening to this conversation in silence. He had come to the senior day-room to borrow a book. He now slipped out, and made his way to Drummond’s study.
Drummond was in. Linton proceeded to business.
“I say, Drummond.”
“Hullo?”
“That man Stanning has come in. He’s getting the senior day-room to rag Sheen’s study.”
===Chinese===
林顿一直默不作声地听着这场谈话。他本是来高年级活动室借书的，此刻便溜了出去，直奔德拉蒙德的书房。
德拉蒙德在。林顿直奔正题。
“喂，德拉蒙德。”
“怎么？”
“斯坦宁那家伙来了。他正撺掇高年级活动室去砸希恩的书房。”

===Original===
“What!”
Linton repeated his statement.
“Does the man think he owns the house?” said Drummond. “Where is he?”
“Coming up now. I hear them. What are you going to do? Stop them?”
“What do you think? Of course I am. I’m not going to have any of Appleby’s crew coming into Seymour’s and ragging studies.”
===Chinese===
“什么！”
林顿把话又说了一遍。
“这家伙当这宿舍是他家的吗？”德拉蒙德说，“他在哪儿？”
“这就上来了。我听见他们了。你打算怎么办？拦住他们？”
“你说呢？当然要拦。我可不打算让阿普尔比（Appleby）宿舍那帮人跑进西摩宿舍来砸书房。”

===Original===
“This ought to be worth seeing,” said Linton. “Look on me as ‘Charles, his friend.’ I’ll help if you want me, but it’s your scene.”
Drummond opened his door just as Stanning and his myrmidons were passing it.
“Hullo, Stanning,” he said.
Stanning turned. The punitive expedition stopped.
“Do you want anything?” inquired Drummond politely.
===Chinese===
“这可有得好看了，”林顿说，“就把我当成戏里的‘查尔斯，他的朋友’吧。用得着我我就搭把手，不过这场戏是你的。”
德拉蒙德打开房门，恰逢斯坦宁正带着他那一帮喽啰路过。
“喂，斯坦宁。”他说。
斯坦宁转过身来。讨伐队停住了。
“有何贵干？”德拉蒙德客气地问道。

===Original===
The members of the senior day-room who were with Stanning rallied round, silent and interested. This dramatic situation appealed to them. They had a passion for rows, and this looked distinctly promising.
There was a pause. Stanning looked carefully at Drummond. Drummond looked carefully at Stanning.
“I was going to see Sheen,” said Stanning at length.
“He isn’t in.”
“Oh!”
===Chinese===
跟斯坦宁一道的高年级活动室成员围拢过来，不出声，兴致勃勃。这戏剧性的局面正对他们胃口。他们对吵架斗殴素来热心，而眼前这一出显然大有看头。
双方静了片刻。斯坦宁仔细打量德拉蒙德，德拉蒙德仔细打量斯坦宁。
“我本来要去找希恩。”斯坦宁终于开口。
“他不在。”
“哦！”

===Original===
Another pause.
“Was it anything special?” inquired Drummond pleasantly.
The expedition edged a little forward.
“No. Oh, no. Nothing special,” said Stanning.
The expedition looked disappointed.
===Chinese===
又是一阵沉默。
“有什么特别的事吗？”德拉蒙德和颜悦色地问。
讨伐队悄悄往前挪了一点。
“没有。哦，没有。没什么特别的事。”斯坦宁说。
讨伐队一脸失望。

===Original===
“Any message I can give him?” asked Drummond.
“No, thanks,” said Stanning.
“Sure?”
“Quite, thanks.”
“I don’t think it’s worthwhile your waiting. He may not be in for some time.”
===Chinese===
“有什么话要我转达吗？”德拉蒙德问。
“没有，谢了。”斯坦宁说。
“确定？”
“十分确定，谢谢。”
“我看你等也不值当。他一时半会儿可能都回不来。”

===Original===
“No, perhaps not. Thanks. So long.”
“So long.”
Stanning turned on his heel, and walked away down the passage. Drummond went back into his study, and shut the door.
The expedition, deprived of its commander-in-chief, paused irresolutely outside. Then it followed its leader’s example.
There was peace in the passage.
===Chinese===
“是，也许不值。谢了。回见。”
“回见。”
斯坦宁转身就走，顺着走廊走远了。德拉蒙德回到书房里，关上了门。
讨伐队没了总司令，在门外踌躇地停了片刻，随即效法起了首领的榜样。
走廊里恢复了太平。

## XI A Small Incident / 十一 一桩小事

===Original===
Failing a gentleman friend, Mr. Bevan was obliged to do what he could by means of local talent. On Sheen’s next visit he was introduced to a burly youth of his own age, very taciturn, and apparently ferocious. He, it seemed, was the knife and boot boy at the “Blue Boar,” “did a bit” with the gloves, and was willing to spar with Sheen provided Mr. Bevan made it all right with the guv’nor; saw, that is so say, that he did not get into trouble for passing in unprofessional frivolity moments which should have been sacred to knives and boots. These terms having been agreed to, he put on the gloves.
For the first time since he had begun his lessons, Sheen experienced an attack of his old shyness and dislike of hurting other people’s feelings. He could not resist the thought that he had no grudge against the warden of the knives and boots. He hardly liked to hit him.
The other, however, did not share this prejudice. He rushed at Sheen with such determination, that almost the first warning the latter had that the contest had begun was the collision of the back of his head with the wall. Out in the middle of the room he did better, and was beginning to hold his own, in spite of a rousing thump on his left eye, when Joe Bevan called “Time!” A second round went off in much the same way. His guard was more often in the right place, and his leads less wild. At the conclusion of the round, pressure of business forced his opponent to depart, and Sheen wound up his lesson with a couple of minutes at the punching-ball. On the whole, he was pleased with his first spar with someone who was really doing his best and trying to hurt him. With Joe Bevan and Francis there was always the feeling that they were playing down to him. Joe Bevan’s gentle taps, in particular, were a little humiliating. But with his late opponent all had been serious. It had been a real test, and he had come through it very fairly. On the whole, he had taken more than he had given﻿—his eye would look curious tomorrow﻿—but already he had thought out a way of foiling the burly youth’s rushes. Next time he would really show his true form.
The morrow, on which Sheen expected his eye to look curious, was the day he had promised to play fives with Mr. Spence. He hoped that at the early hour at which they had arranged to play it would not have reached its worst stage; but when he looked in the glass at a quarter to seven, he beheld a small ridge of purple beneath it. It was not large, nor did it interfere with his sight, but it was very visible. Mr. Spence, however, was a sportsman, and had boxed himself in his time, so there was a chance that nothing would be said.
It was a raw, drizzly morning. There would probably be few fives-players before breakfast, and the capture of the second court should be easy. So it turned out. Nobody was about when Sheen arrived. He pinned his slip of paper to the door, and, after waiting for a short while for Mr. Spence and finding the process chilly, went for a trot round the gymnasium to pass the time.
===Chinese===
一时请不到绅士朋友来陪练，贝文先生只好就近取材，有什么人用什么人。希恩第二次去时，被介绍给一个和自己年纪相仿的壮实青年，此人沉默寡言，看上去颇为凶悍。据说他是“蓝野猪”（Blue Boar）酒馆里磨刀擦靴的小伙计，“戴拳套也来两手”，愿意陪希恩过招，条件是贝文先生得跟老板把话说妥；也就是说，得保证他不至于因为把本该献给刀子和靴子的神圣时光，挪去干这不务正业的营生而惹祸上身。这些条件谈妥之后，他戴上了拳套。
自开始学拳以来，希恩头一回旧病发作，又害起了羞怯，不忍伤害别人的感情。他禁不住想：自己跟这位刀靴看守人无冤无仇。他有点下不去手。
对方却全无这种顾忌。他一股劲朝希恩猛扑过来，比赛已经开始，希恩得到的头一个通知，差不多就是自己的后脑勺撞上了墙。到了屋子中央，他打得好了些，虽然左眼上结结实实挨了一记，也渐渐能跟对方分庭抗礼了，这时乔·贝文喊了声“时间到！”第二回合的进行也大同小异。他的防守更多时候摆对了位置，出拳也不那么乱放了。这一回合结束时，伙计因公务缠身不得不告辞，希恩便在吊袋上打了几分钟，结束了这一课。总的来说，跟一个真正全力以赴、存心要伤他的人第一次过招，他是满意的。同乔·贝文和弗朗西斯（Francis）练的时候，总觉得他们在让自己。尤其是乔·贝文那轻轻的点触，多少叫人有点丢脸。可跟方才这位对手，一切都是动真格的。那是一次真正的考验，而他相当体面地经受住了。总的说来，他挨的拳多于出的拳——明天他的眼睛会很好看——但他已经想出了破那壮实青年猛冲的招数。下一回，他要真正露一手了。
希恩预计眼睛会变得好看的那第二天，正是他答应了跟斯彭斯（Spence）先生打墙手球的日子。他指望约的时辰那么早，乌青还不至于发展到最坏的地步；可是六点三刻他对着镜子一瞧，眼眶底下已经鼓起一道紫痕。面积不大，也不碍视力，却非常显眼。不过斯彭斯先生是个运动家出身，年轻时自己也打过拳，所以这事也许一个字都不会被提起。
那是个阴冷下着毛毛雨的早晨。早餐之前打墙手球的人多半不会多，占下第二球场应当不难。事实也果然如此。希恩到场时四下无人。他把纸条钉在门上，等了斯彭斯先生一小会儿，越等越冷，便绕着体育馆小跑起来消磨时间。

===Original===
Mr. Spence had not arrived during his absence, but somebody else had. At the door of the second court, gleaming in first-fifteen blazer, sweater, stockings, and honour-cap, stood Attell.
Sheen looked at Attell, and Attell looked through Sheen.
It was curious, thought Sheen, that Attell should be standing in the very doorway of court two. It seemed to suggest that he claimed some sort of ownership. On the other hand, there was his, Sheen’s, paper on the.﻿ ﻿… His eye happened to light on the cement flooring in front of the court. There was a crumpled ball of paper there.
When he had started for his run, there had been no such ball of paper.
Sheen picked it up and straightened it out. On it was written “R. D. Sheen.”
===Chinese===
他跑步回来，斯彭斯先生还没到，别人却到了一位。第二球场的门口，站着阿特尔（Attell），橄榄球一线队的运动夹克、毛线衣、长袜和荣誉帽，浑身闪闪发亮。
希恩望着阿特尔，阿特尔却把希恩当空气看穿了过去。
希恩心想，怪了，阿特尔偏偏站在二号球场的门口，这架势仿佛宣称他对这球场享有某种所有权似的。可是话又说回来，他希恩的纸条明明钉在……他的目光恰好落在球场前的水泥地上。那儿有一团揉皱的纸。
他动身去跑步的时候，那里还没有这团纸。
希恩把它拾起来展平。上面写着“R. D. 希恩”。

===Original===
He looked up quickly. In addition to the faraway look, Attell’s face now wore a faint smile, as if he had seen something rather funny on the horizon. But he spake no word.
A curiously calm and contented feeling came upon Sheen. Here was something definite at last. He could do nothing, however much he might resent it, when fellows passed him by as if he did not exist; but when it came to removing his landmark.﻿ ﻿…
“Would you mind shifting a bit?” he said very politely. “I want to pin my paper on the door again. It seems to have fallen down.”
Attell’s gaze shifted slowly from the horizon and gradually embraced Sheen.
“I’ve got this court,” he said.
===Chinese===
他猛地抬起头。阿特尔脸上，除了那种望向远方的神情，如今又添了一丝淡淡的微笑，仿佛他在地平线上瞧见了什么相当有趣的东西。但他一言不发。
一种异乎寻常的平静而满足的感觉攫住了希恩。总算有了件实实在在的事。别人当他不存在一般从身边擦过去，哪怕他再愤懑，也无计可施；可是说到拔他的纸条……
“能不能劳驾让一让？”他非常有礼貌地说。“我想把纸条重新钉到门上。它好像掉下来了。”
阿特尔的目光缓缓从地平线上收回来，渐渐罩住了希恩。
“这场子我占了，”他说。

===Original===
“I think not,” said Sheen silkily. “I was here at ten to seven, and there was no paper on the door then. So I put mine up. If you move a little, I’ll put it up again.”
“Go and find another court, if you want to play,” said Attell, “and if you’ve got anybody to play with,” he added with a sneer. “This is mine.”
“I think not,” said Sheen.
Attell resumed his inspection of the horizon.
“Attell,” said Sheen.
===Chinese===
“恐怕不是吧，”希恩细声细气地说。“我七点差十分就到了，那时门上没有纸条，所以我把自己的钉了上去。你要是挪一挪，我就再把它钉上。”
“想打球就另找场子去，”阿特尔说，“要是找得到人陪你打的话，”他冷笑着补了一句。“这是我的。”
“恐怕不是吧，”希恩说。
阿特尔重新考察起地平线来。
“阿特尔，”希恩喊道。

===Original===
Attell did not answer.
Sheen pushed him gently out of the way, and tore down the paper from the door.
Their eyes met. Attell, after a moment’s pause, came forward, half-menacing, half irresolute; and as he came Sheen hit him under the chin in the manner recommended by Mr. Bevan.
“When you uppercut,” Mr. Bevan was wont to say, “don’t make it a swing. Just a half-arm jolt’s all you want.”
It was certainly all Attell wanted. He was more than surprised. He was petrified. The sudden shock of the blow, coming as it did from so unexpected a quarter, deprived him of speech: which was, perhaps, fortunate for him, for what he would have said would hardly have commended itself to Mr. Spence, who came up at this moment.
===Chinese===
阿特尔不答理。
希恩把他轻轻推开，把门上那张纸扯了下来。
两人的目光相遇了。阿特尔顿了片刻，走上前来，一半是威吓，一半是犹豫；他刚一上前，希恩便照贝文先生传授的法门，朝他的下巴击出一拳。
“打上钩拳的时候，”贝文先生惯于这样说，“别抡成大摆拳。短臂一顶，就够了。”
这一拳对阿特尔来说的确是足够了。他不止是吃惊，他整个呆若木鸡。这一下突如其来，又出自如此意想不到的方位，惊得他连话都说不出来——这或许倒是他的运气，因为他要说出口的那些话，恐怕很难让恰在此时赶到的斯彭斯先生听了满意。

===Original===
“Well, Sheen,” said Mr. Spence, “here you are. I hope I haven’t kept you waiting. What a morning! You’ve got the court, I hope?”
“Yes, sir,” said Sheen.
He wondered if the master had seen the little episode which had taken place immediately before his arrival. Then he remembered that it had happened inside the court. It must have been over by the time Mr. Spence had come upon the scene.
“Are you waiting for somebody, Attell?” asked Mr. Spence. “Stanning? He will be here directly. I passed him on the way.”
Attell left the court, and they began their game.
===Chinese===
“你好啊，希恩，”斯彭斯先生说，“你已经来了。希望没让你久等。这鬼天气！球场占到了吧？”
“占到了，先生，”希恩说。
他拿不准老师看见没有自己到达前刚上演的那一出小插曲。随即他想起来，那一幕发生在球场里面。等斯彭斯先生到的时候，想必早已收场了。
“你在等人吗，阿特尔？”斯彭斯先生问。“斯坦宁？他马上就到。我半路上碰见他了。”
阿特尔走出球场，他们开打了。

===Original===
“You’ve hurt your eye, Sheen,” said Mr. Spence, at the end of the first game. “How did that happen?”
“Boxing, sir,” said Sheen.
“Oh,” replied Mr. Spence, and to Sheen’s relief he did not pursue his inquiries.
Attell had wandered out across the gravel to meet Stanning.
“Got that court?” inquired Stanning.
===Chinese===
“你眼睛伤了，希恩，”第一局打完，斯彭斯先生说。“怎么弄的？”
“打拳打的，先生，”希恩说。
“哦，”斯彭斯先生应了一声。叫希恩松了口气的是，他没有再追问下去。
阿特尔已经踱出碎石路去迎斯坦宁（Stanning）。
“场子占到了？”斯坦宁问。

===Original===
“No.”
“You idiot, why on earth didn’t you? It’s the only court worth playing in. Who’s got it?”
“Sheen.”
“Sheen!” Stanning stopped dead. “Do you mean to say you let a fool like Sheen take it from you! Why didn’t you turn him out?”
“I couldn’t,” said Attell. “I was just going to when Spence came up. He’s playing Sheen this morning. I couldn’t very well bag the court when a master wanted it.”
===Chinese===
“没有。”
“你这蠢货，究竟为什么没占到？只有那块场子才值得一打。被谁占了？”
“希恩。”
“希恩！”斯坦宁猛地站住。“你是说，你让希恩那么个蠢货从你手里把场子抢走了！你怎么不把他轰出去？”
“不行啊，”阿特尔说。“我正要动手，斯彭斯来了。他今早要跟希恩打球。老师要用的场子，我总不好硬抢。”

===Original===
“I suppose not,” said Stanning. “What did Sheen say when you told him you wanted the court?”
This was getting near a phase of the subject which Attell was not eager to discuss.
“Oh, he didn’t say much,” he said.
“Did you do anything?” persisted Stanning.
Attell suddenly remembered having noticed that Sheen was wearing a black eye. This was obviously a thing to be turned to account.
===Chinese===
“那倒是，”斯坦宁说。“你说你要场子的时候，希恩说了什么？”
这话问到阿特尔极不想谈的话题上来了。
“哦，他没说什么，”他说。
“那你动手了没有？”斯坦宁追问。
阿特尔忽然记起，他曾注意到希恩眼上带着乌青。这显然是件可以拿来利用的事。

===Original===
“I hit him in the eye,” he said. “I’ll bet it’s coloured by school-time.”
And sure enough, when school-tune arrived, there was Sheen with his face in the condition described, and Stanning hastened to spread abroad this sequel to the story of Sheen’s failings in the town battle. By the end of preparation it had got about the school that Sheen had cheeked Attell, that Attell had hit Sheen, and that Sheen had been afraid to hit him back. At the precise moment when Sheen was in the middle of a warm two-minute round with Francis at the “Blue Boar,” an indignation meeting was being held in the senior day-room at Seymour’s to discuss this latest disgrace to the house.
“This is getting a bit too thick,” was the general opinion. Moreover, it was universally agreed that something ought to be done. The feeling in the house against Sheen had been stirred to a dangerous pitch by this last episode. Seymour’s thought more of their reputation than any house in the school. For years past the house had led on the cricket and football field and off it. Sometimes other houses would actually win one of the cups, but, when this happened, Seymour’s was always their most dangerous rival. Other houses had their ups and downs, were very good one year and very bad the next; but Seymour’s had always managed to maintain a steady level of excellence. It always had a man or two in the School eleven and fifteen, generally supplied one of the School Racquets pair for Queen’s Club in the Easter vac., and when this did not happen always had one of two of the Gym. Six or Shooting Eight, or a few men who had won scholarships at the ’Varsities. The pride of a house is almost keener than the pride of a school. From the first minute he entered the house a new boy was made to feel that, in coming to Seymour’s, he had accepted a responsibility that his reputation was not his own, but belonged to the house. If he did well, the glory would be Seymour’s glory. If he did badly, he would be sinning against the house.
===Chinese===
“我照他眼睛来了一下，”他说。“我打赌，到上课的时候准会乌出来。”
果然，上课时希恩的脸便是上述那副模样，斯坦宁赶紧把这桩事四处传扬，当作希恩在镇上那场水战里丢丑的续篇。到晚自习结束时，全校已经传遍：希恩冲撞了阿特尔，阿特尔揍了希恩，而希恩不敢还手。就在希恩正在“蓝野猪”跟弗朗西斯热汗涔涔地打两分钟一回合的那个当口，西摩宿舍的高年级走读生自习室里正开着一场义愤大会，讨论这桩宿舍最新的耻辱。
“这也欺人太甚了，”这是大家的一致看法。而且众人异口同声：总得想点办法。这最新一桩事，把宿舍里反希恩的情绪煽到了危险的高度。全校再没有哪个宿舍比西摩宿舍更爱惜名声。多年以来，这宿舍在板球场和足球场上——场下也一样——始终独占鳌头。偶尔别的宿舍也会真的捧走某座奖杯，但每逢这种时候，西摩宿舍永远是他们最危险的对手。别的宿舍起起落落，今年风光，明年稀烂；西摩宿舍却总能把优异保持在一个稳定的水平上。校板球队和橄榄球队里，它常年有一两个人；复活节假期代表学校去女王俱乐部（Queen's Club）打墙网球的搭档，一般也由它出上一人；即便这落了空，体操六人组或射击八人组里也总有它一两个名额，再不然就是有几位得了名牌大学奖学金的。宿舍的荣誉感，几乎比学校的荣誉感还要强烈。新生踏进宿舍的第一分钟起，大家就让他明白：进了西摩宿舍，就是领受了一分责任——他的名声不再属于他自己，而是属于宿舍。他若争气，光荣是西摩的光荣；他若丢脸，他就是对宿舍犯了罪。

===Original===
This second story about Sheen, therefore, stirred Seymour’s to the extent of giving the house a resemblance to a hornet’s nest into which a stone had been hurled. After school that day the house literally hummed. The noise of the two day-rooms talking it over could be heard in the road outside. The only bar that stood between the outraged Seymourites and Sheen was Drummond. As had happened before, Drummond resolutely refused to allow anything in the shape of an active protest, and no argument would draw him from this unreasonable attitude, though why it was that he had taken it up he himself could not have said. Perhaps it was that rooted hatred a boxer instinctively acquires of anything in the shape of unfair play that influenced him. He revolted against the idea of a whole house banding together against one of its members.
So even this fresh provocation did not result in any active interference with Sheen; but it was decided that he must be cut even more thoroughly than before.
And about the time when this was resolved, Sheen was receiving the congratulations of Francis on having positively landed a blow upon him. It was an event which marked an epoch in his career.
===Chinese===
于是，这第二桩关于希恩的传闻，把西摩宿舍搅得活像一只被人扔进了石头的马蜂窝。那天放学后，整座宿舍嗡嗡作响，两个走读生自习室议论此事的声浪，连外面的马路上都听得见。横在群情激愤的西摩子弟与希恩之间的唯一一道屏障，是德拉蒙德。和先前一样，德拉蒙德斩钉截铁地拒绝任何实际抗议行动，什么道理也搬不动他这“不讲理”的立场——至于他自己为什么持这个立场，他也说不上来。也许左右他的，是拳手对一切不公平行径本能的、根深蒂固的憎恶。全宿舍联手对付其中一个成员——这个念头让他打心里反感。
于是，连这新一轮的挑衅也没有招来对希恩什么实际的干涉；但大家议定，对他的抵制必须比先前更加彻底。
而大约就在这项决议出台的当口，希恩正在接受弗朗西斯的道贺，因为他结结实实打中了对方一拳。这是他拳坛生涯中划时代的一件大事。

## XXIII A Surprise for Seymour’s / 二十三 西摩宿舍大吃一惊

===Original===
Seymour’s house took in one copy of the *Sportsman* daily. On the morning after the Aldershot competition Linton met the paperboy at the door on his return from the fives courts, where he had been playing a couple of before-breakfast games with Dunstable. He relieved him of the house copy, and opened it to see how the Wrykyn pair had performed in the gymnastics. He did not expect anything great, having a rooted contempt for both experts, who were small and, except in the gymnasium, obscure. Indeed, he had gone so far on the previous day as to express a hope that Biddle, the more despicable of the two, would fall off the horizontal bar and break his neck. Still he might as well see where they had come out. After all, with all their faults, they were human beings like himself, and Wrykinians.
The competition was reported in the Boxing column. The first thing that caught his eye was the name of the school among the headlines. “Honours,” said the headline, “for St. Paul’s, Harrow, and Wrykyn.”
“Hullo,” said Linton, “what’s all this?”
Then the thing came on him with nothing to soften the shock. He had folded the paper, and the last words on the half uppermost were, “Final. Sheen beat Peteiro.”
Linton had often read novels in which some important document “swam before the eyes” of the hero or the heroine; but he had never understood the full meaning of the phrase until he read those words, “Sheen beat Peteiro.”
There was no mistake about it. There the thing was. It was impossible for the *Sportsman* to have been hoaxed. No, the incredible, outrageous fact must be faced. Sheen had been down to Aldershot and won a silver medal! Sheen! *Sheen!!* Sheen who had﻿—who was﻿—well, who, in a word, was **Sheen**!!!
===Chinese===
西摩宿舍每天订一份《体育人报》（*Sportsman*）。奥尔德肖特比赛之后的那个早晨，林顿从墙手球场（fives courts）回来，在门口撞见送报的报童——他方才正和邓斯特布尔打了几个早餐前的球局。他从报童手里接过宿舍那份报纸，翻开来看看雷金那对选手在体操项目上表现如何。他并不指望有什么了不起的成绩，对那两位行家怀有根深蒂固的轻蔑：他们个头矮小，出了体操房便籍籍无名。前一天他甚至说过，巴不得两人中更叫他看不上眼的比德尔（Biddle）从单杠上摔下来摔断脖子。不过，看看他们究竟排个什么名次，倒也无妨。毕竟，纵有千般不是，他们总还是跟他一样的人，还是雷金人。
这场比赛登在拳击栏里。首先映入他眼帘的，是标题之中学校的名字。“圣保罗（St. Paul’s）、哈罗（Harrow）与雷金，”标题写道，“荣膺殊荣。”
“嚯，”林顿说，“这是怎么回事？”
接着，那件事劈头盖脸砸了下来，没有任何缓冲。他已把报纸折起，最上面那一半的末尾几行字是：“决赛。希恩胜佩泰罗。”
林顿常在小说里读到，某份重要文书在男女主人公“眼前晃来晃去”；可直到读到“希恩胜佩泰罗”这几个字，他才算彻底领悟了这句话的全部含义。
这绝不会弄错。白纸黑字就在那儿。《体育人报》是断不可能被人骗了的。不，这个难以置信、骇人听闻的事实必须正视：希恩去了奥尔德肖特，还赢了一枚银牌！希恩！*希恩！！*那个曾经——那个现在是——唉，那个一言以蔽之，就是**希恩**的家伙！！！

===Original===
Linton read on like one in a dream.
“The Lightweights fell,” said the writer, “to a newcomer Sheen, of Wrykyn” (Sheen!), “a clever youngster with a strong defence and a beautiful straight left, doubtless the result of tuition from the middleweight ex-champion, Joe Bevan, who was in his corner for the final bout. None of his opponents gave him much trouble except Peteiro of Ripton, whom he met in the final. A very game and determined fight was seen when these two met, but Sheen’s skill and condition discounted the rushing tactics of his adversary, and in the last minute of the third round the referee stopped the encounter.” (Game and determined! Sheen!!) “Sympathy was freely expressed for Peteiro, who has thus been runner-up two years in succession. He, however, met a better man, and paid the penalty. The admirable pluck with which Sheen bore his punishment and gradually wore his man down made his victory the most popular of the day’s programme.”
*Well!*
Details of the fighting described Sheen as “cutting out the work,” “popping in several nice lefts,” “swinging his right for the point,” and executing numerous other incredible manoeuvres.
*Sheen!*
You caught the name correctly? *Sheen*, I’ll trouble you.
===Chinese===
林顿如坠梦中，继续往下读。
“轻量级金牌，”撰稿人写道，“为雷金的新手希恩（希恩！）所得。这位聪明的少年选手防守坚实，一手漂亮的左直拳，无疑得益于中量级前冠军乔·贝文的指点——决赛时贝文正守在他角上。诸位对手中，唯里普顿的佩泰罗给他添了些麻烦，两人恰在决赛相逢。这一战打得顽强而坚决，但希恩的技术与状态使对手猛冲猛打的战术大打折扣，第三回合最后一分钟，裁判终止了比赛。”（顽强而坚决！希恩！！）“人们对佩泰罗深表同情，他已连续两年屈居亚军。不过他这回是遇上了更强的人，只得认输。希恩承受击打时的过人胆气，以及逐渐拖垮对手的韧劲，使他的胜利成为当日赛程中最得人心的一场。”
*好家伙！*
战况细节里说，希恩“主导了攻势”，“连连送出几记漂亮的左拳”，“抡起右手直取下颌”，还使出了其他种种令人难以置信的招数。
*希恩！*
名字你没听错吧？劳驾，是*希恩*。

===Original===
Linton stared blankly across the school grounds. Then he burst into a sudden yell of laughter.
On that very morning the senior day-room was going to court-martial Sheen for disgracing the house. The resolution had been passed on the previous afternoon, probably just as he was putting the finishing touches to the “most popular victory of the day’s programme.” “This,” said Linton, “is rich.”
He grubbed a little hole in one of Mr. Seymour’s flowerbeds, and laid the *Sportsman* to rest in it. The news would be about the school at nine o’clock, but if he could keep it from the senior day-room till the brief interval between breakfast and school, all would be well, and he would have the pure pleasure of seeing that backbone of the house make a complete ass of itself. A thought struck him. He unearthed the *Sportsman*, and put it in his pocket.
He strolled into the senior day-room after breakfast.
“Anyone seen the *Sporter* this morning?” he inquired.
No one had seen it.
===Chinese===
林顿茫然地瞪望着校园操场。随即他爆出一阵狂笑。
就在今天早晨，高年级公共活动室（senior day-room）还要为败坏宿舍名誉一事开“军事法庭”审判希恩。决议是前一天下午通过的，多半恰在他为“当日赛程中最得人心的胜利”收尾的那一刻。“这下可太妙了。”林顿说。
他在西摩先生的一块花坛里刨出个小坑，让《体育人报》入土为安。到九点钟，这消息就会传遍全校，可要是他能瞒住高年级活动室，一直瞒到早餐后上课前那一小段工夫，那就万事大吉，他便能痛痛快快地看着宿舍的这根顶梁柱出尽洋相。他灵机一动，又把《体育人报》挖了出来，塞进口袋。
早饭后，他溜溜达达地走进高年级活动室。
“今儿早上有人瞧见《体育报儿》了吗？”他问道。
谁都没瞧见。

===Original===
“The thing hasn’t come,” said someone.
“Good!” said Linton to himself.
At this point Stanning strolled into the room. “I’m a witness,” he said, in answer to Linton’s look of inquiry. “We’re doing this thing in style. I depose that I saw the prisoner cutting off on the﻿—whatever day it was, when he ought to have been saving our lives from the fury of the mob. Hadn’t somebody better bring the prisoner into the dock?”
“I’ll go,” said Linton promptly. “I may be a little time, but don’t get worried. I’ll bring him all right.”
He went upstairs to Sheen’s study, feeling like an impresario about to produce a new play which is sure to create a sensation.
Sheen was in. There was a ridge of purple under his left eye, but he was otherwise intact.
===Chinese===
“报纸还没送到。”有人说。
“好！”林顿心里说。
正在这时，斯坦宁（Stanning）踱了进来。“我是证人。”他答着林顿探询的目光说，“咱们这事儿可要办得像模像样。我作证：我亲眼看见囚犯在某日——管它哪日——临阵开溜，那工夫他本该救我们于暴徒的怒火才是。是不是该有人把囚犯提到被告席上来？”
“我去。”林顿一口应承，“兴许要耽搁一会儿，可别着急。我保准把他带到。”
他上楼到希恩的书房去，那心情好比一位剧团经理，即将推出一部必定轰动的新戏。
希恩在屋里。他左眼下面横着一道青紫，其余倒是完好无损。

===Original===
“ ’Gratulate you, Sheen,” said Linton.
For an instant Sheen hesitated. He had rehearsed this kind of scene in his mind, and sometimes he saw himself playing a genial, forgiving, let’s-say-no-more-about-it-we-all-make-mistakes-but-in-future! role, sometimes being cold haughty, and distant, and repelling friendly advances with icy disdain. If anybody but Linton had been the first to congratulate him he might have decided on this second line of action. But he liked Linton, and wanted to be friendly with him.
“Thanks,” he said.
Linton sat down on the table and burst into a torrent of speech.
“You *are* a man! What did you want to do it for? Where the dickens did you learn to box? And why on earth, if you can win silver medals at Aldershot, didn’t you box for the house and smash up that sidey ass Stanning? I say, look here, I suppose we haven’t been making idiots of ourselves all the time, have we?”
“I shouldn’t wonder,” said Sheen. “How?”
===Chinese===
“恭喜你了，希恩。”林顿说。
希恩迟疑了一刹那。这类场面他早在心里排演过：有时他看见自己扮演一位和蔼宽厚、过去的事一笔勾销、谁还不犯个错往后注意就行的角色，有时又变得冷淡高傲、拒人千里，以冰冷的轻蔑挡开一切示好。若是头一个来道贺的不是林顿，他也许就选了第二条路。可他喜欢林顿，也愿意和他交好。
“多谢。”他说。
林顿往桌上一坐，滔滔不绝地说开了。
“你*真够*条汉子！干吗要去干这个？你到底是在哪儿学的拳击？还有，既然你能在奥尔德肖特拿银牌，干吗不替宿舍出战，把斯坦宁那个装腔作势的蠢货打个稀烂？我说，你听着，我们该不会一直在自说傻话吧？”
“难说没有。”希恩道，“怎么说？”

===Original===
“I mean, you did﻿—What I mean to say is﻿—Oh, hang it, *you* know! You did cut off when we had that row in the town, didn’t you?”
“Yes,” said Sheen, “I did.”
With that medal in his pocket it cost him no effort to make the confession.
“I’m glad of that. I mean, I’m glad we haven’t been such fools as we might have been. You see, we only had Stanning’s word to go on.”
Sheen started.
“Stanning!” he said. “What do you mean?”
===Chinese===
“我是说，你当时——我想说的是——唉，得了，*你*自己明白！那天镇上乱子起来的时候，你确实先溜了，是不是？”
“是的，”希恩说，“我溜了。”
口袋里揣着那枚奖章，这声承认不费他半点力气。
“那就好。我是说，幸亏我们没闹到本可能闹出的那种傻份上。要知道，我们当时全凭斯坦宁一面之词。”
希恩一怔。
“斯坦宁！”他说，“你这是什么意思？”

===Original===
“He was the chap who started the story. Didn’t you know? He told everybody.”
“I thought it was Drummond,” said Sheen blankly. “You remember meeting me outside his study the day after? I thought he told you then.”
“Drummond! Not a bit of it. He swore you hadn’t been with him at all. He was as sick as anything when I said I thought I’d seen you with him.”
“I﻿—” Sheen stopped. “I wish I’d known,” he concluded. Then, after a pause, “So it was Stanning!”
“Yes﻿—conceited beast. Oh. I say.”
“Um?”
===Chinese===
“传闲话的就是这小子。你不知道么？他逢人便说。”
“我还以为是德拉蒙德。”希恩茫然道，“记得第二天你在他书房外面撞见我吗？我当时以为是那时候他告诉你的。”
“德拉蒙德！压根不是。他赌咒说你根本没跟他在一起。我说我好像看见你跟他一道，他还气得不行。”
“我——”希恩顿住了。“早知道就好了。”他末了说。停了片刻，又道：“原来是斯坦宁！”
“就是——自大的畜生。哦，我说。”
“嗯？”

===Original===
“I see it all now. Joe Bevan taught you to box.”
“Yes.”
“Then that’s how you came to be at the ‘Blue Boar’ that day. He’s the Bevan who runs it.”
“That’s his brother. He’s got a gymnasium up at the top. I used to go there every day.”
“But I say, Great Scott, what are you going to do about that?”
“How do you mean?”
===Chinese===
“这下我全明白了。是乔·贝文教你打的拳。”
“是。”
“那么那天你待在‘蓝野猪’就是这么回事。开那店的正是贝文。”
“那家店是他哥哥开的。他在顶楼有个健身房。我从前天天去。”
“可是我说，天哪，这事你打算怎么办？”
“什么怎么办？”

===Original===
“Why, Spence is sure to ask you who taught you to box. He must know you didn’t learn with the instructor. Then it’ll all come out, and you’ll get dropped on for going up the river and going to the pub.”
“Perhaps he won’t ask,” said Sheen.
“Hope not. Oh, by the way﻿—”
“What’s up?”
“Just remembered what I came up for. It’s an awful rag. The senior day-room are going to court-martial you.”
“Court-martial me!”
===Chinese===
“嗐，斯彭斯（Spence）准会问你是谁教你打拳的。他肯定知道你不是跟学校的拳击教练学的。到时全都会兜出来，你去河上游、上那家酒馆的事就得挨整。”
“也许他不会问。”希恩说。
“希望如此。哦，对了——”
“什么事？”
“刚想起来我上来是干吗的。有场绝妙的恶作剧。高年级活动室要开‘军事法庭’审你。”
“军事法庭审我！”

===Original===
“For funking. They don’t know about Aldershot, not a word. I bagged the *Sportsman* early, and hid it. They are going to get the surprise of their lifetime. I said I’d come up and fetch you.”
“I shan’t go,” said Sheen.
Linton looked alarmed.
“Oh, but I say, you must. Don’t spoil the thing. Can’t you see what a rag it’ll be?”
“I’m not going to sweat downstairs for the benefit of the senior day-room.”
“I say,” said Linton, “Stanning’s there.”
===Chinese===
“审你怯阵。奥尔德肖特的事他们一无所知，半个字都不知道。我一早就把《体育人报》截了下来藏好。他们就要收到一辈子最大的一次意外了。我说我上楼来提你。”
“我不去。”希恩说。
林顿面露惊慌。
“喂，我说，你可一定得去。别搅了这台好戏。你难道看不出这有多乐吗？”
“我才不打算为高年级活动室的福祉累得满头大汗跑下楼去。”
“我说，”林顿道，“斯坦宁也在那儿呢。”

===Original===
“What!”
“He’s a witness,” said Linton, grinning.
Sheen got up.
“Come on,” he said.
Linton came on.
---
===Chinese===
“什么！”
“他是证人。”林顿咧嘴笑道。
希恩站起身来。
“走吧。”他说。
林顿应声就走。
---

===Original===
Down in the senior day-room the court was patiently awaiting the prisoner. Eager anticipation was stamped on its expressive features.
“Beastly time he is,” said Clayton. Clayton was acting as president.
“We shall have to buck up,” said Stanning. “Hullo, here he is at last. Come in, Linton.”
“I was going to,” said Linton, “but thanks all the same. Come along, Sheen.”
“Shut that door, Linton,” said Stanning from his seat on the table.
“All right, Stanning,” said Linton. “Anything to oblige. Shall I bring up a chair for you to rest your feet on?”
===Chinese===
楼下高年级活动室里，法庭正耐心等候着囚犯。一张张表情丰富的脸上都盖满了急切期待的印记。
“他也真够磨蹭的。”克莱顿（Clayton）说。克莱顿充任庭长。
“咱们得抓紧点儿。”斯坦宁说，“喂，他总算来了。请进，林顿。”
“我本来就要进了，”林顿说，“不过还是多谢费心。来吧，希恩。”
“把门关上，林顿。”斯坦宁从桌上他的座位上发话。
“好嘞，斯坦宁，”林顿说，“愿为您效劳。要不要我搬把椅子来给您搁搁脚？”

===Original===
“Forge ahead, Clayton,” said Stanning to the president.
The president opened the court-martial in unofficial phraseology.
“Look here, Sheen,” he said, “we’ve come to the conclusion that this has got a bit too thick.”
“You mustn’t talk in that chatty way, Clayton,” interrupted Linton. “ ‘Prisoner at the bar’s’ the right expression to use. Why don’t you let somebody else have a look in? You’re the rottenest president of a court-martial I ever saw.”
“Don’t rag, Linton,” said Clayton, with an austere frown. “This is serious.”
“Glad you told me,” said Linton. “Go on.”
===Chinese===
“接着来，克莱顿。”斯坦宁对庭长说。
庭长用非官方的措辞宣布军事法庭开庭。
“听着，希恩，”他说，“我们一致认为，这事有点儿太出格了。”
“你可不能这么随口闲聊，克莱顿，”林顿插嘴道，“规范的说法是‘被告席上的囚犯’。你怎么不让别人也插上一手？你是我见过的最蹩脚的军事法庭庭长。”
“别捣乱，林顿，”克莱顿板着脸皱眉道，“这是正经事。”
“亏得你告诉我。”林顿说，“接着讲。”

===Original===
“Can’t you sit down, Linton!” said Stanning.
“I was only waiting for leave. Thanks. You were saying something, Clayton. It sounded pretty average rot, but you’d better unburden your soul.”
The president resumed.
“We want to know if you’ve anything to say﻿—”
“You don’t give him a chance,” said Linton. “You bag the conversation so.”
“﻿—about disgracing the house.”
===Chinese===
“你就不能坐下吗，林顿！”斯坦宁说。
“我这不正等着恩准么。多谢。你方才说到哪儿了，克莱顿？听着净是平平常常的胡话，不过你还是把心里话倒出来吧。”
庭长继续发言。
“我们想知道你有什么要申辩的——”
“你根本不给他机会，”林顿说，“话全叫你一人包圆了。”
“——关于败坏宿舍名誉一事。”

===Original===
“By getting the Gotford, you know, Sheen,” explained Linton. “Clayton thinks that work’s a bad habit, and ought to be discouraged.”
Clayton glared, and looked at Stanning. He was not equal to the task of tackling Linton himself.
Stanning interposed.
“Don’t rot, Linton. We haven’t much time as it is.”
“Sorry,” said Linton.
“You’ve let the house down awfully,” said Clayton.
===Chinese===
“就是说你得了戈特福德奖（the Gotford），你知道吧，希恩，”林顿解释道，“克莱顿认为用功是个坏习惯，理应劝阻。”
克莱顿怒目而视，转头去看斯坦宁。要他自己对付林顿，他还办不到。
斯坦宁插了进来。
“别胡闹，林顿。咱们时间本来就不多。”
“抱歉。”林顿说。
“你让宿舍大大地丢了脸。”克莱顿说。

===Original===
“Yes?” said Sheen.
Linton took the paper out of his pocket, and smoothed it out.
“Seen the *Sporter*?” he asked casually. His neighbour grabbed at it.
“I thought it hadn’t come,” he said.
“Good account of Aldershot,” said Linton.
He leaned back in his chair as two or three of the senior day-room collected round the *Sportsman*.
===Chinese===
“是吗？”希恩说。
林顿从口袋里掏出报纸，抹平了。
“看过《体育报儿》了吗？”他随口问道。他旁边的人一把抢了过去。
“我还当没送来呢。”那人说。
“上面有奥尔德肖特的精彩报道。”林顿说。
他往椅背上一靠，活动室里已有两三个人围拢到那张《体育人报》旁。

===Original===
“Hullo! We won the gym!”
“Rot! Let’s have a look!”
This tremendous announcement quite eclipsed the court-martial as an object of popular interest. The senior day-room surged round the holder of the paper.
“Give us a chance,” he protested.
“We can’t have. Where is it? Biddle and Smith are simply hopeless. How the dickens can they have got the shield?”
“What a goat you are!” said a voice reproachfully to the possessor of the paper. “Look at this. It says Cheltenham got it. And here we are﻿—seventeenth. Fat lot of shield we’ve won.”
===Chinese===
“喂！咱们体操赢了！”
“胡扯！让我瞧瞧！”
这一惊雷般的宣告顿时压倒了军事法庭，成了众人瞩目的中心。高年级活动室的人潮水般围住拿报纸的那位。
“行行好，让我看看。”他抗议道。
“可我们等不及。在哪儿呢？比德尔和史密斯（Smith）简直没戏。他们到底怎么拿的奖盾？”
“你可真够笨的！”一个声音责备拿报纸的人，“瞧这儿。明明写着切尔滕纳姆（Cheltenham）拿了奖盾。咱们呢——第十七。咱这奖盾可赢得真不少啊。”

===Original===
“Then what the deuce does this mean? ‘Honours for St. Paul’s, Harrow, and Wrykyn.’ ”
“Perhaps it refers to the boxing,” suggested Linton.
“But we didn’t send anyone up. Look here. Harrow won the Heavies. St. Paul’s got the Middles. *Hullo!*”
“Great Scott!” said the senior day-room.
There was a blank silence. Linton whistled softly to himself.
The gaze of the senior day-room was concentrated on that ridge of purple beneath Sheen’s left eye.
===Chinese===
“那这到底什么意思？‘圣保罗、哈罗与雷金荣膺殊荣。’”
“多半说的是拳击。”林顿提示道。
“可咱们没派人去啊。你看。重量级是哈罗得的。中量级归了圣保罗。*咦！*
“我的老天！”高年级活动室齐声道。
一片茫然的寂静。林顿独自轻轻吹起口哨。
高年级活动室众人的目光，齐刷刷聚焦在希恩左眼底下那道青紫上。

===Original===
Clayton was the first to speak. For some time he had been waiting for sufficient silence to enable him to proceed with his presidential duties. He addressed himself to Sheen.
“Look here, Sheen,” he said, “we want to know what you’ve got to say for yourself. You go disgracing the house﻿—”
The stunned senior day-room were roused to speech.
“Oh, chuck it, Clayton.”
“Don’t be a fool, Clayton.”
“Silly idiot!”
===Chinese===
克莱顿头一个开口。有一阵子了，他一直在等候足够安静的场面，好继续履行庭长职责。他冲着希恩说起话来。
“听着，希恩，”他说，“我们想知道你有何辩解。你一味败坏宿舍的名誉——”
惊呆的高年级活动室众人被激得开了腔。
“哎，算了吧，克莱顿。”
“别犯傻了，克莱顿。”
“蠢货！”

===Original===
Clayton looked round in pained surprise at this sudden withdrawal of popular support.
“You’d better be polite to Sheen,” said Linton; “he won the Lightweights at Aldershot yesterday.”
The silence once more became strained.
