“Well,” said Sheen, “weren’t you going to court-martial me, or something? Clayton, weren’t you saying something?”
Clayton started. He had not yet grasped the situation entirely; but he realised dimly that by some miracle Sheen had turned in an instant into a most formidable person.
“Er﻿—no,” he said. “No, nothing.”
===Chinese===
大众的支持说撤就撤，克莱顿又痛又惊地环顾四周。
“你们对希恩还是客气点好，”林顿说，“昨天奥尔德肖特的轻量级是他拿的。”
寂静再度变得紧绷起来。
“怎么，”希恩说，“你们不是要军事法庭审我什么的吗？克莱顿，你方才不是有话要说？”
克莱顿一惊。他还没把局面全弄明白，却隐隐意识到，凭着某种奇迹，希恩转眼间变成了一个惹不起的人物。
“呃——没了，”他说，“没了，没什么。”

===Original===
“The thing seems to have fallen through, Sheen,” said Linton. “Great pity. Started so well, too. Clayton always makes a mess of things.”
“Then I’d just like to say one thing,” said Sheen.
Respectful attention from the senior day-room.
“I only want to know why you can’t manage things of this sort by yourselves, without dragging in men from other houses.”
“Especially men like Stanning,” said Linton. “The same thing occurred to me. It’s lucky Drummond wasn’t here. Remember the last time, you chaps?”
The chaps did. Stanning became an object of critical interest. After all, who *was* Stanning? What right had he to come and sit on tables in Seymour’s and interfere with the affairs of the house?
===Chinese===
“这事怕是黄了，希恩，”林顿说，“真可惜。开头还那么多好呢。克莱顿办事从来就是一团糟。”
“那我只想说一件事。”希恩说。
高年级活动室恭恭敬敬地洗耳恭听。
“我只想知道，这种事你们为什么自己不能了断，非要拉别宿舍的人进来。”
尤其是斯坦宁这种人，”林顿说，“我也正想到这茬。幸亏德拉蒙德不在。上回那事还记得吧，各位？”
各位都记得。斯坦宁顿时成了众目审视的对象。说到底，斯坦宁*算老几*？他凭什么跑到西摩宿舍来往桌上一坐，插手本宿舍的事务？

===Original===
The allusion to “last time” was lost upon Sheen, but he saw that it had not improved Stanning’s position with the spectators.
He opened the door.
“Goodbye, Stanning,” he said.
“If I hadn’t hurt my wrist﻿—” Stanning began.
“Hurt your wrist!” said Sheen. “You got a bad attack of Peteiro. That was what was the matter with you.”
“You think that everyone’s a funk like yourself,” said Stanning.
===Chinese===
“上回”所指何事，希恩不甚了了，但他看得出，这话没让斯坦宁在观众心中的地位有所改善。
他把门打开了。
“再见，斯坦宁。”他说。
“要不是我伤了手腕——”斯坦宁开口道。
“伤手腕！”希恩说，“你是害了一场厉害的佩泰罗病。你的病根儿就在这儿。”
“你以为人人都跟你一样是个临阵退缩的胆小鬼。”斯坦宁说。

===Original===
“Pity they aren’t,” said Linton; “we should do rather well down at Aldershot. And he wasn’t such a terror after all, Sheen, was he? You beat him in two and a half rounds, didn’t you? Think what Stanning might have done if only he hadn’t sprained his poor wrist just in time.
“Look here, Linton﻿—”
“Some are born with sprained wrists,” continued the speaker, “some achieve sprained wrists﻿—like Stanning﻿—”
Stanning took a step towards him.
===Chinese===
“可惜他们不是，”林顿说，“不然咱们在奥尔德肖特可就大获全胜了。再说他到底也没那么可怕，是不是，希恩？你两个半回合就把他打发了，对吧？想想看，斯坦宁要不是那手腕扭伤得那么是时候，他本来能有多大战绩啊。
“我说，林顿——”
“有的人天生手腕就是扭的，”这位演说家继续道，“有的人是挣来扭的手腕——比如斯坦宁——（仿莎翁名句）”
斯坦宁朝他跨上一步。

===Original===
“Don’t forget you’ve a sprained wrist,” said Linton.
“Come on, Stanning,” said Sheen, who was still holding the door open, “you’ll be much more comfortable in your own house. I’ll show you out.”
“I suppose,” said Stanning in the passage, “you think you’ve scored off me.”
“That,” said Sheen pleasantly, “is rather the idea. Goodbye.”
===Chinese===
“别忘了你手腕可是扭了的。”林顿说。
“请吧，斯坦宁，”仍扶着门敞开的希恩说，“回你自己宿舍舒服多了。我送你出去。”
“我猜，”斯坦宁在过道里说，“你以为这下赢了我。”
“那个嘛，”希恩和颜悦色地说，“正是我的本意。再见。”

## VI Albert Redivivus / 六 阿尔伯特卷土重来

===Original===
By murdering in cold blood a large and respected family, and afterwards depositing their bodies in a reservoir, one may gain, we are told, much unpopularity in the neighbourhood of one’s crime; while robbing a church will get one cordially disliked especially by the vicar. But, to be really an outcast, to feel that one has no friend in the world, one must break an important public-school commandment.
Sheen had always been something of a hermit. In his most sociable moments he had never had more than one or two friends; but he had never before known what it meant to be completely isolated. It was like living in a world of ghosts, or, rather, like being a ghost in a living world. That disagreeable experience of being looked through, as if one were invisible, comes to the average person, it may be half a dozen times in his life. Sheen had to put up with it a hundred times a day. People who were talking to one another stopped when he appeared and waited until he had passed on before beginning again. Altogether, he was made to feel that he had done for himself, that, as far as the life of the school was concerned, he did not exist.
There had been some talk, particularly in the senior day-room, of more active measures. It was thought that nothing less than a court-martial could meet the case. But the house prefects had been against it. Sheen was in the sixth, and, however monstrous and unspeakable might have been his acts, it would hardly do to treat him as if he were a junior. And the scheme had been definitely discouraged by Drummond, who had stated, without wrapping the gist of his remarks in elusive phrases, that in the event of a court-martial being held he would interview the president of the same and knock his head off. So Seymour’s had fallen back on the punishment which from their earliest beginnings the public schools have meted out to their criminals. They had cut Sheen dead.
In a way Sheen benefited from this excommunication. Now that he could not even play fives, for want of an opponent, there was nothing left for him to do but work. Fortunately, he had an object. The Gotford would be coming on in a few weeks, and the more work he could do for it, the better. Though Stanning was the only one of his rivals whom he feared, and though *he* was known to be taking very little trouble over the matter, it was best to run as few risks as possible. Stanning was one of those people who produce great results in their work without seeming to do anything for them.
So Sheen shut himself up in his study and ground grimly away at his books, and for exercise went for cross-country walks. It was a monotonous kind of existence. For the space of a week the only Wrykinian who spoke a single word to him was Bruce, the son of the Conservative candidate for Wrykyn: and Bruce’s conversation had been limited to two remarks. He had said, “You might play that again, will you?” and, later, “Thanks.” He had come into the music-room while Sheen was practising one afternoon, and had sat down, without speaking, on a chair by the door. When Sheen had played for the second time the piece which had won his approval, Bruce thanked him and left the room. As the solitary break in the monotony of the week, Sheen remembered the incident rather vividly.
===Chinese===
据说，冷血地屠灭一户人丁兴旺、受人敬重的人家，再把尸首沉入水库，可以在案发地一带博得极坏的名声；而抢劫教堂则会招人切齿痛恨，尤以牧师为甚。然而，要想真正沦为社会弃儿，体会到举世无友、天地间再无一个亲人的滋味，就非得触犯一条公学的重要诫命不可。
希恩向来有些隐士气质。在他最爱交际的时刻，他的朋友也从没多过一两个；可他此前从未体会过彻底孤立的滋味。这就像活在一个幽灵世界里，或者不如说，更像自己做了幽灵，飘荡在活人中间。被人视若无物、仿佛自己是透明的，这种不愉快的经历，普通人一生里也许只会碰上五六次，希恩却一天要忍受一百回。正彼此交谈的人一见他来便住了口，直等他走远了才重新开口。总而言之，大家让他感到自己已经彻底完了：就学校的生活而言，他这个人已经不存在了。
尤其是在高年级活动室里，大家议论过要采取更积极的措施。有人认为，此案非一场公审不足以料理。但宿舍的级长们反对。希恩在六年级，无论他的行径何等骇人听闻、何等不可名状，把他当低年级生对待总归不妥。这个计划还被德拉蒙德明确地打压了下去——他毫不用闪烁其词遮掩话锋，直截了当地声明：倘若真开公审，他就去会一会那位公审主席，把他的脑袋敲下来。于是西摩宿舍退而采用了公学自开山以来对付罪人的那项惩罚：他们对希恩彻底视而不见。
从某种意义上说，希恩反倒因这项逐出令得了好处。如今他连墙手球（fives）都没得打——找不到对手——便只剩下一件事可做：用功。所幸他有个目标。再过几周戈特福德考试就要来临，能为之做的功课越多越好。虽说斯坦宁是他的竞争对手中唯一令他忌惮的一个，虽说尽人皆知*他*在这事上没下多少功夫，还是尽量少冒险为妙。斯坦宁属于那种人：看上去不费吹灰之力，成果却硕果累累。
于是希恩把自己关进书房，咬牙苦读，锻炼则靠乡间远足。这是一种单调的生活。整整一个星期里，同他说过一句话的雷金人只有布鲁斯（Bruce）——雷金保守党候选人的公子——而布鲁斯的谈话总共限于两句。他说过：“劳驾，把那首再弹一遍，行吗？”后来又说了一句：“谢谢。”那天下午他趁希恩在音乐室练琴时走了进来，一言不发地在门边一把椅子上坐下。希恩把赢得他青睐的那支曲子又弹了一遍之后，布鲁斯道了谢，离开了房间。作为这一星期单调生活中仅有的插曲，希恩对这件事记得相当真切。

===Original===
Since the great rout of Albert and his minions outside Cook’s, things, as far as the seniors were concerned, had been quiet between school and town. Linton and Dunstable had gone to and from Cook’s two days in succession without let or hindrance. It was generally believed that, owing to the unerring way in which he had put his head in front of Drummond’s left on that memorable occasion, the scarlet-haired one was at present dry-docked for repairs. The story in the school﻿—it had grown with the days﻿—was that Drummond had laid the enemy out on the pavement with a sickening crash, and that he had still been there at, so to speak, the close of play. As a matter of fact, Albert was in excellent shape, and only an unfortunate previous engagement prevented him from ranging the streets near Cook’s as before. Sir William Bruce was addressing a meeting in another part of the town, and Albert thought it his duty to be on hand to boo.
In the junior portion of the school the feud with the town was brisk. Mention has been made of a certain St. Jude’s, between which seat of learning and the fags of Dexter’s and the School House there was a spirited vendetta.
Jackson, of Dexter’s was one of the pillars of the movement. Jackson was

> a calm-brow’d lad,
 

 Yet mad, at moments, as a hatter,
===Chinese===
自从阿尔伯特和他那班喽啰在库克商店（Cook's）外吃了那场大溃败之后，就高年级而论，学校与镇上倒相安无事。林顿和邓斯特布尔连着两天往返库克商店，畅通无阻。人们普遍相信，由于在那难忘的场合他把自己的脑袋结结实实地送到了德拉蒙德的左拳底下，那位红头发的主儿眼下正在干船坞里检修。学校里流传的说法——且愈传愈玄——是德拉蒙德把那敌人往人行道上一放，发出令人牙酸的巨响，而且可以说直到终场时分他还躺在原地。事实上，阿尔伯特状态极佳，只因先前已有约在身，才没能照旧在库克商店一带的街面上巡弋。威廉·布鲁斯爵士（Sir William Bruce）正在城的另一头向一个集会发表演说，阿尔伯特认为自己有义务到场喝倒彩。
在学校的低年级部分，同镇上的宿怨正打得火热。前文提到过一所圣犹大学校（St. Jude's），在那座学府与德克斯特宿舍（Dexter's）及学校宿舍（School House）的低年级学童之间，正进行着一场热火朝天的世仇私斗。
德克斯特宿舍的杰克逊（Jackson）是这场运动的中坚人物之一。杰克逊

> 是个眉宇沉静的少年，
 

 却又时时疯癫，像个帽匠，

===Original===

and he derived a great deal of pleasure from warring against St. Jude’s. It helped him to enjoy his meals. He slept the better for it. After a little turn up with a Judy he was fuller of that spirit of manly fortitude and forbearance so necessary to those whom Fate brought frequently into contact with Mr. Dexter. The Judies wore mortarboards, and it was an enjoyable pastime sending these spinning into space during one of the usual rencontres in the High Street. From the fact that he and his friends were invariably outnumbered, there was a sporting element in these affairs, though occasionally this inferiority of numbers was the cause of his executing a scientific retreat with the enemy harassing his men up to the very edge of the town. This had happened on the last occasion. There had been casualties. No fewer than six house-caps had fallen into the enemy’s hands, and he himself had been tripped up and rolled in a puddle.
He burned to avenge this disaster.
“Coming down to Cook’s?” he said to his ally, Painter. It was just a week since the Sheen episode.
“All right,” said Painter.
“Suppose we go by the High Street,” suggested Jackson, casually.
===Chinese===

他从与圣犹大的交战中得到莫大的乐趣。这战争助他胃口大开，也让他睡得更沉。同“犹大崽子”小小交手一回之后，他浑身充盈着男子汉的坚忍与克制精神——凡命运使之频频与德克斯特先生（Mr. Dexter）打交道的人，这种精神必不可少。犹大崽子们戴平顶帽，在大街（High Street）惯常的遭遇战中把这些帽子打飞上天，是一桩赏心乐事。由于他和他的朋友们在人数上总是处于劣势，这些交锋便带上了几分竞技的味道；不过偶尔，人数的悬殊也会害他执行一场科学的撤退，由敌军穷追不舍、一路袭扰，直追到镇子边缘。上一次就是这样，还折损了人马：不少于六顶宿舍帽落入敌手，他自己也被绊了个跟头，在泥水坑里滚了一身。
他急欲血洗这场灾难。
“下楼去库克商店吗？”他对盟友佩因特（Painter）说。这时距希恩事件刚好一个星期。
“行啊。”佩因特说。
“咱们走大街过去怎么样？”杰克逊随口提议。

===Original===
“Then we’d better get a few more chaps,” said Painter.
A few more chaps were collected, and the party, numbering eight, set off for the town. There were present such stalwarts as Borwick and Crowle, both of Dexter’s, and Tomlin, of the School House, a useful man to have by you in an emergency. It was Tomlin who, on one occasion, attacked by two terrific champions of St. Jude’s in a narrow passage, had vanquished them both, and sent their mortarboards miles into the empyrean, so that they were never the same mortarboards again, but wore ever after a bruised and draggled look.
The expedition passed down the High Street without adventure, until, by common consent, it stopped at the lofty wall which bounded the playground of St. Jude’s.
From the other side of the wall came sounds of revelry, shrill squealings and shoutings. The Judies were disporting themselves at one of their weird games. It was known that they played touch-last, and Scandal said that another of their favourite recreations was marbles. The juniors at Wrykyn believed that it was to hide these excesses from the gaze of the public that the playground wall had been made so high. Eyewitnesses, who had peeped through the door in the said wall, reported that what the Judies seemed to do mostly was to chase one another about the playground, shrieking at the top of their voices. But, they added, this was probably a mere ruse to divert suspicion.
They had almost certainly got the marbles in their pockets all the time.
===Chinese===
“那最好再约上几个人。”佩因特说。
又纠合了几个人，一队八人，向镇上进发。在场的有德克斯特宿舍的好汉博威克（Borwick）与克劳尔（Crowle），还有学校宿舍的汤姆林（Tomlin）——紧急关头带在身边颇有用处的一员。汤姆林曾在一条窄巷里遭到圣犹大两名悍勇冠军的夹击，结果将二人双双击败，把他们的平顶帽打得飞上九霄，从此那两顶帽子再不复旧观，永远带着一副碰伤拖沓的邋遢相。
远征队顺大街一路行进，并无事故，直到不约而同地在圣犹大操场那堵高墙跟前停了下来。
墙那边传来寻欢作乐的声响，尖利的尖叫与喊闹。犹大崽子们正在玩他们那些古怪游戏中的一种。据知他们玩“捉尾巴”（touch-last），风言风语还说，他们另一项心爱的消遣是打弹珠。雷金的低年级生相信，操场围墙修得那么高，正是为了把这些过火行径遮掩起来，不叫公众看见。曾有目击者从那堵墙上的门洞向内窥看，报告说犹大崽子们的主要活动似乎就是在操场上互相追逐，扯着嗓子尖声喊叫。不过他们又补充说，这多半只是转移怀疑的障眼法。
而弹珠几乎可以肯定一直就在他们口袋里。

===Original===
The expedition stopped, and looked itself in the face.
“How about buzzing something at them?” said Jackson earnestly.
“You can get oranges over the road,” said Tomlin in his helpful way.
Jackson vanished into the shop indicated, and reappeared a few moments later with a brown paper bag.
“It seems a beastly waste,” suggested the economical Painter.
===Chinese===
远征队停下脚步，面面相觑。
“扔点什么过去砸他们怎么样？”杰克逊恳切地说。
“马路对面能买到橙子。”汤姆林好心支招。
杰克逊一头扎进所指的那家店，片刻之后拎着一只棕色纸袋重新出现。
“这未免太糟蹋了。”节俭的佩因特提出异议。

===Original===
“That’s all right,” said Jackson, “they’re all bad. The man thought I was rotting him when I asked if he’d got any bad oranges, but I got them at last. Give us a leg up, someone.”
Willing hands urged him to the top of the wall. He drew out a green orange, and threw it.
There was a sudden silence on the other side of the wall. Then a howl of wrath went up to the heavens. Jackson rapidly emptied his bag.
“Got him!” he exclaimed, as the last orange sped on its way. “Look out, they’re coming!”
The expedition had begun to move off with quiet dignity, when from the doorway in the wall there poured forth a stream of mortarboarded warriors, shrieking defiance. The expedition advanced to meet them.
===Chinese===
“不要紧，”杰克逊说，“全是坏果。我问他有没有坏橙子，那家伙还当我是拿他开涮，不过最后还是让我买到了。谁来托我一把。”
几只热心肠的手把他顶上了墙头。他掏出一枚青皮橙子，掷了出去。
墙那边骤然一静。紧接着一声怒嚎冲天而起。杰克逊飞快地把袋子倒空。
“打中了！”最后一枚橙子脱手飞出时，他大喊道，“当心，他们来了！”
远征队正以安静庄重的姿态开始撤离，忽见墙上的门洞里涌出一股头戴平顶帽的武士，厉声叫骂着挑战。远征队迎上前去。

===Original===
As usual, the Judies had the advantage in numbers, and, filled to the brim with righteous indignation, they were proceeding to make things uncommonly warm for the invaders﻿—Painter had lost his cap, and Tomlin three waistcoat buttons﻿—when the eye of Jackson, roving up and down the street, was caught by a Seymour’s cap. He was about to shout for assistance when he perceived that the newcomer was Sheen, and refrained. It was no use, he felt, asking Sheen for help.
But just as Sheen arrived and the ranks of the expedition were beginning to give way before the strenuous onslaught of the Judies, the latter, almost with one accord, turned and bolted into their playground again. Looking round, Tomlin, that first of generals, saw the reason, and uttered a warning.
A mutual foe had appeared. From a passage on the left of the road there had debouched on to the field of action Albert himself and two of his band.
The expedition flew without false shame. It is to be doubted whether one of Albert’s calibre would have troubled to attack such small game, but it was the firm opinion of the Wrykyn fags and the Judies that he and his men were to be avoided.
The newcomers did not pursue them. They contented themselves with shouting at them. One of the band threw a stone.
===Chinese===
和往常一样，犹大崽子们占了人数的便宜；他们义愤填膺，正要让入侵者好好吃一番苦头——佩因特丢了帽子，汤姆林掉了三粒马甲纽扣——这时杰克逊的目光在街上来回逡巡，忽然被一顶西摩宿舍的帽子吸住了。他正要呼救，却认出来者正是希恩，便忍住了。他觉得，求希恩帮忙是无济于事的。
然而就在希恩赶到、远征队的阵线在犹大崽子凶猛的冲击下开始溃退之际，后者却几乎不约而同地转身，一口气逃回了他们的操场。汤姆林——这位头一等的将才——环顾四周，看清了缘故，发出一声示警。
共同的仇敌现身了。从大路左侧的一条巷子里，阿尔伯特本人携两名党羽，杀上了战场。
远征队毫不扭捏，撒腿便飞。像阿尔伯特这种分量的人物是否会费神去追击这路小猎物，颇可怀疑；但雷金的小子们和犹大崽子们一致笃信：此人及其部众，避之为上。
新来的人没有追赶，只冲着他们叫骂了两声了事。其中一名党羽掷出一块石头。

===Original===
Then they caught sight of Sheen.
Albert said, “Oo er!” and advanced at the double. His companions followed him.
Sheen watched them come, and backed against the wall. His heart was thumping furiously. He was in for it now, he felt. He had come down to the town with this very situation in his mind. A wild idea of doing something to restore his self-respect and his credit in the eyes of the house had driven him to the High Street. But now that the crisis had actually arrived, he would have given much to have been in his study again.
Albert was quite close now. Sheen could see the marks which had resulted from his interview with Drummond. With all his force Sheen hit out, and experienced a curious thrill as his fist went home. It was a poor blow from a scientific point of view, but Sheen’s fives had given him muscle, and it checked Albert. That youth, however, recovered rapidly, and the next few moments passed in a whirl for Sheen. He received a stinging blow on his left ear, and another which deprived him of his whole stock of breath, and then he was on the ground, conscious only of a wish to stay there forever.
===Chinese===
接着，他们望见了希恩。
阿尔伯特说了声“嚯，喂！”便跑步冲上前来。他的同伙紧随其后。
希恩望着他们逼近，退到墙根。他的心狂跳不止。他觉得，这下躲不过去了。他下山进城时，心里装的就是这个局面。正是想做点什么来挽回自尊、挽回宿舍众人眼中自己的体面，这个狂乱的念头把他驱到了大街上。可如今危机当真来临，他宁愿付出很大代价换回自己的书房。
阿尔伯特已经近在眼前。希恩能看见他与德拉蒙德那场会晤留下的印记。希恩倾尽全力挥拳出击，拳头结结实实砸中对方时，他体验到一阵奇异的战栗。从拳理上看，这是记拙劣的拳，但希恩打墙手球练出了肌肉，这一拳把阿尔伯特打得顿住了。然而那少年恢复得飞快，接下来的片刻对希恩而言天旋地转：左耳挨了一记火辣辣的拳，又挨了一记把他肺里的气全打光的拳，随后他便倒在地上，心里只剩一个念头——就这样永远躺下去才好。

## XXIV Bruce Explains / 二十四 布鲁斯解惑

===Original===
Mr. Spence was a master with a great deal of sympathy and a highly developed sense of duty. It was the combination of these two qualities which made it so difficult for him to determine on a suitable course of action in relation to Sheen’s out-of-bounds exploits. As a private individual he had nothing but admiration for the sporting way in which Sheen had fought his uphill fight. He felt that he himself in similar circumstances would have broken any number of school rules. But, as a master, it was his duty, he considered, to report him. If a master ignored a breach of rules in one case, with which he happened to sympathise, he would in common fairness be compelled to overlook a similar breach of rules in other cases, even if he did not sympathise with them. In which event he would be of small use as a master.
On the other hand, Sheen’s case was so exceptional that he might very well compromise to a certain extent between the claims of sympathy and those of duty. If he were to go to the headmaster and state baldly that Sheen had been in the habit for the last half-term of visiting an upriver public-house, the headmaster would get an entirely wrong idea of the matter, and suspect all sorts of things which had no existence in fact. When a boy is accused of frequenting a public-house, the head-magisterial mind leaps naturally to Stale Fumes and the Drunken Stagger.
So Mr. Spence decided on a compromise. He sent for Sheen, and having congratulated him warmly on his victory in the Lightweights, proceeded as follows:
“You have given me to understand, Sheen, that you were taught boxing by Bevan?”
===Chinese===
斯彭斯先生是一位极富同情心、责任感又极强的老师。正是这两种品质的交织，使他在如何处置希恩越界壮举这件事上，难以定下一条合适的路线。作为一个私人，他对希恩那场逆风之战打得如此光明磊落，只有满心的赞赏。他觉得换了自己处在同样的境地，多少条校规都会照样违反。可是身为教师，他认为举报希恩是他的职责。倘若一位老师碰上自己同情的个案便姑息了一次违规，那么出于起码的公道，往后遇到类似的违规——哪怕他并不同情——也不得不睁一只眼闭一只眼。真到那一步，他这个老师也就没什么用处了。
不过话说回来，希恩的案子实在特殊，他大可以在同情与职责两造之间做一点折中。倘若他跑到校长面前，直统统地说希恩这半个学期以来一直有去河上游一家酒馆的习惯，校长便会对这事产生完全错误的印象，怀疑出种种实际上子虚乌有的名堂来。一个学生被指认常光顾酒馆，做校长的那颗心自然会立刻联想到陈腐的酒气和醉汉的踉跄。
于是斯彭斯先生定下了折中的办法。他把希恩叫来，先就他轻量级夺魁热烈道贺了一番，然后说了下面这番话：
“你曾让我了解到，希恩，你的拳击是贝文教的？”

===Original===
“Yes, sir.”
“At the ‘Blue Boar’?”
“Yes, sir.”
“This puts me in a rather difficult position, Sheen. Much as I dislike doing it, I am afraid I shall have to report this matter to the headmaster.”
Sheen said he supposed so. He saw Mr. Spence’s point.
“But I shall not mention the ‘Blue Boar.’ If I did, the headmaster might get quite the wrong impression. He would not understand all the circumstances. So I shall simply mention that you broke bounds by going up the river. I shall tell him the whole story, you understand, and it’s quite possible that you will hear no more of the affair. I’m sure I hope so. But you understand my position?”
===Chinese===
“是的，先生。”
“在‘蓝野猪’？”
“是的，先生。”
“这就让我相当为难了，希恩。尽管十分不情愿，恐怕我还是得把这事报告校长。”
希恩说他也这么想。他明白斯彭斯先生的难处。
“不过我不会提‘蓝野猪’。要是一提，校长很可能产生天大的误会。他不会了解全部情况。所以我只说你沿河上行、越了界。我会把事情的原委讲清楚，你明白吧，这样这事很有可能就此了结、再无下文。我当然希望如此。不过，我的处境你总明白？”

===Original===
“Yes, sir.”
“That’s all, then, Sheen. Oh, by the way, you wouldn’t care for a game of fives before breakfast tomorrow, I suppose?”
“I should like it, sir.”
“Not too stiff?”
“No, sir.”
“Very well, then. I’ll be there by a quarter-past seven.”
===Chinese===
“明白，先生。”
“那就这样吧，希恩。哦，对了，明天早饭前来一场墙手球，你有兴趣吧？”
“求之不得，先生。”
“不会太累吧？”
“不会，先生。”
“那好。我七点一刻到场。”

===Original===
---
Jack Bruce was waiting to see the headmaster in his study at the end of afternoon school.
“Well, Bruce,” said the headmaster, coming into the room and laying down some books on the table, “do you want to speak to me? Will you give your father my congratulations on his victory. I shall be writing to him tonight. I see from the paper that the polling was very even. Apparently one or two voters arrived at the last moment and turned the scale.”
“Yes, sir.”
===Chinese===
---
下午的课刚结束，杰克·布鲁斯（Jack Bruce）正在书房里等着见校长。
“你好啊，布鲁斯，”校长走进房间，把几本书搁在桌上，说道，“你有话要跟我说？请替我向你父亲道贺，恭喜他获胜。今晚我就给他写信。我从报上看到票数咬得极紧。显然有一两个选民在最后一刻赶到，扭转了大局。”
“好的，先生。”

===Original===
“It is a most gratifying result. I am sure that, apart from our political views, we should all have been disappointed if your father had not won. Please congratulate him sincerely.”
“Yes, sir.”
“Well, Bruce, and what was it that you wished to see me about?”
Bruce was about to reply when the door opened, and Mr. Spence came in.
“One moment, Bruce,” said the headmaster. “Yes, Spence?”
Mr. Spence made his report clearly and concisely. Bruce listened with interest. He thought it hardly playing the game for the gymnasium master to hand Sheen over to be executed at the very moment when the school was shaking hands with itself over the one decent thing that had been done for it in the course of the athletic year; but he told himself philosophically that he supposed masters had to do these things. Then he noticed with some surprise that Mr. Spence was putting the matter in a very favourable light for the accused. He was avoiding with some care any mention of the “Blue Boar.” When he had occasion to refer to the scene of Sheen’s training, he mentioned it vaguely as a house.
===Chinese===
“这是个最令人满意的结果。我敢说，撇开政治立场不谈，要是你父亲没赢，我们大家都会失望的。请代我向他致以诚挚的祝贺。”
“好的，先生。”
“好了，布鲁斯，你要见我，是为了什么事？”
布鲁斯正要回答，门开了，斯彭斯先生走了进来。
“稍等一下，布鲁斯，”校长说。“什么事，斯彭斯？”
斯彭斯先生把事情汇报得清楚而简洁。布鲁斯听得津津有味。他想，学校正为这一年体育季里唯一的一件体面事互相道贺，体操老师偏挑这个节骨眼把希恩交出去正法，实在算不上光明磊落；不过他又达观地告诉自己，老师嘛，总得干这些事。随即他有些惊讶地注意到，斯彭斯先生正把事情往对被告大为有利的方向说。他小心翼翼地回避“蓝野猪”三个字。每当不得不提希恩训练的地点，他就含含糊糊地说成一栋房子。

===Original===
“This man Bevan, who is an excellent fellow and a personal friend of my own, has a house some way up the river.”
Of course a public-house *is* a house.
“Up the river,” said the headmaster meditatively.
It seemed that that was all that was wrong. The prosecution centred round that point, and no other. Jack Bruce, as he listened, saw his way of coping with the situation.
“Thank you, Spence,” said the headmaster at the conclusion of the narrative. “I quite understand that Sheen’s conduct was very excusable. But﻿—I distinctly said﻿—I placed the upper river out of bounds.﻿ ﻿… Well, I will see Sheen, and speak to him. I will speak to him.”
Mr. Spence left the room.
===Chinese===
“这位贝文，人品极好，也是我私人的朋友，在河的上游有一栋房子。”
当然，酒馆（public-house）也*是*房子（house）。
“河的上游，”校长沉吟道。
看来问题全都出在这里。控方的指控只围着这一点转，别无其他。杰克·布鲁斯听着听着，便想出了应付这局面的法子。
“谢谢你，斯彭斯，”校长在听完汇报后说。“我完全明白，希恩的行为情有可原。可是——我明确说过——河的上游是划为禁区的。……好吧，我会见见希恩，同他谈谈。我会同他谈谈。”
斯彭斯先生退了出去。

===Original===
“Please sir﻿—” said Jack Bruce.
“Ah, Bruce. I am afraid I have kept you some little time. Yes?”
“I couldn’t help hearing what Mr. Spence was saying to you about Sheen, sir. I don’t think he knows quite what really happened.”
“You mean﻿—?”
“Sheen went there by road. I used to take him in my motor.”
“Your﻿—! What did you say, Bruce?”
===Chinese===
“校长，请容我说——”杰克·布鲁斯开口道。
“啊，布鲁斯。恐怕让你等了这半天。什么事？”
“斯彭斯先生跟您讲希恩的那些话，我想不听也难，先生。我觉得他并不完全清楚事情的原委。”
“你是说——？”
“希恩去那儿走的是陆路。以前都是我用汽车送他去的。”
“你的——！你说什么，布鲁斯？”

===Original===
“My motorcar, sir. That’s to say, my father’s. We used to go together every day.”
“I am glad to hear it. I am glad. Then I need say nothing to Sheen after all. I am glad.﻿ ﻿… But﻿—er﻿—Bruce,” proceeded the headmaster after a pause.
“Yes, sir?”
“Do you﻿—are you in the habit of driving a motorcar frequently?”
“Every day, sir. You see, I am going to take up motors when I leave school, so it’s all education.”
The headmaster was silent. To him the word “Education” meant Classics. There was a Modern side at Wrykyn, and an Engineering side, and also a Science side; but in his heart he recognised but one Education﻿—the Classics. Nothing that he had heard, nothing that he had read in the papers and the monthly reviews had brought home to him the spirit of the age and the fact that Things were not as they used to be so clearly as this one remark of Jack Bruce’s. For here was Bruce admitting that in his spare time he drove motors. And, stranger still, that he did it not as a wild frolic but seriously, with a view to his future career.
===Chinese===
“我的汽车，先生。确切说，是我父亲的。我们以前每天都一起去。”
“听你这么说我真高兴。我真高兴。那我毕竟用不着对希恩说什么了。真高兴。……不过——呃——布鲁斯，”校长顿了顿，接着说道。
“怎么，先生？”
“你——你是否常有开汽车的习惯？”
“每天都开，先生。您知道，我毕业以后要投身汽车行业，所以这全算是受教育。”
校长沉默了。对他而言，“教育”这个词就等于古典学。雷金学院设有近世学科，设有工程学科，也设有理学科；可在他心底，他只承认一种教育——古典学。他听到过的一切，他在报章和月刊评论里读到过的一切，都不如杰克·布鲁斯这一句话，把时代的精神和“世事已不复当年”这个事实如此清晰地摆到他面前。因为布鲁斯在这里招认了：他课余时间是开汽车的。而更匪夷所思的是，他干这事并非胡闹取乐，而是郑重其事，为将来的事业做准备。

===Original===
“The old order changeth,” thought the headmaster a little sadly.
Then he brought himself back from his mental plunge into the future.
“Well, well, Bruce,” he said, “we need not discuss the merits and demerits of driving motorcars, need we? What did you wish to see me about?”
“I came to ask if I might get off morning school tomorrow, sir. Those voters who got to the poll just in time and settled the election﻿—I brought them down in the car. And the policeman﻿—he’s a Radical, and voted for Pedder﻿—Mr. Pedder﻿—has sworn﻿—says I was exceeding the speed limit.”
The headmaster pressed a hand to his forehead, and his mind swam into the future.
“Well, Bruce?” he said at length, in the voice of one whom nothing can surprise now.
===Chinese===
“旧秩序正在更迭，”校长略带伤感地想。
随后他把自己从对未来的神游中拽了回来。
“好了，好了，布鲁斯，”他说，“开汽车的利弊得失，我们就不必讨论了吧？你究竟为什么来见我？”
“我来是想问，明天上午的课我能不能请假，先生。那批赶在最后一刻到场、一锤定音的选民——是我开车送来的。还有那个警察——他是个激进派，投了佩德一票——佩德先生——他起了誓——说我超速了。”
校长一只手按住额头，思绪又游进了未来。
“怎么样，布鲁斯？”他终于开口，用的是那种从此再没有什么能让他吃惊的人的口气。

===Original===
“He says I was going twenty-eight miles an hour. And if I can get to the Court tomorrow morning I can prove that I wasn’t. I brought them to the poll in the little runabout.”
“And the﻿—er﻿—little runabout,” said the headmaster, “does not travel at twenty-eight miles an hour?”
“No, sir. It can’t go more than twenty at the outside.”
“Very well, Bruce, you need not come to school tomorrow morning.”
“Thank you, sir.”
The headmaster stood thinking.﻿ ﻿… The new order.﻿ ﻿…
===Chinese===
“他说我开到了每小时二十八英里。要是明天上午我能出庭，就能证明我没开那么快。送他们去投票站的是那辆小轻便车。”
“那么——呃——那辆小轻便车，”校长说，“跑不到每小时二十八英里？”
“跑不到，先生。撑死也不超过二十英里。”
“好吧，布鲁斯，明天上午你不必来上课了。”
“谢谢您，先生。”
校长站在那里出神。……新秩序。……

===Original===
“Bruce,” he said.
“Yes, sir?”
“Tell me, do I look very old?”
Bruce stared.
“Do I look three hundred years old?”
“No, sir,” said Bruce, with the stolid wariness of the boy who fears that a master is subtly chaffing him.
===Chinese===
“布鲁斯，”他说。
“怎么，先生？”
“告诉我，我看上去很老吗？”
布鲁斯瞪大了眼睛。
“我看上去有三百岁吗？”
“没有，先生，”布鲁斯说，带着那种生怕被老师不动声色地取笑的少年特有的沉稳警惕。

===Original===
“I feel more, Bruce,” said the headmaster, with a smile. “I feel more. You will remember to congratulate your father for me, won’t you?”
---
Outside the door Jack Bruce paused in deep reflection.
“Rum!” he said to himself. “Jolly rum!”
===Chinese===
“我感觉不止，布鲁斯，”校长微笑着说。“我感觉不止。你会记得替我向你父亲道贺的，对吧？”
---
门外，杰克·布鲁斯停下来陷入了深深的思索。
“怪！”他自言自语。“真叫一个怪！”

===Original===
---
On the senior gravel he met Sheen.
“Hullo, Sheen,” he said, “what are you going to do?”
“Drummond wants me to tea with him in the infirmary.”
“It’s all right, then?”
“Yes. I got a note from him during afternoon school. You coming?”
===Chinese===
---
在高年级碎石操场上，他遇见了希恩。
“喂，希恩，”他说，“你这是去干什么？”
“德拉蒙德拉我去医务室陪他喝茶。”
“那就没事了？”
“没事了。下午上课时我收到他一张条子。你去吗？”

===Original===
“All right. I say, Sheen, the Old Man’s rather rum sometimes, isn’t he?”
“What’s he been doing now?”
“Oh﻿—nothing. How do you feel after Aldershot? Tell us all about it. I’ve not heard a word yet.”
===Chinese===
“行。我说，希恩，老头子有时候挺怪的，是不是？”
“他又干什么了？”
“哦——没什么。奥尔德肖特回来感觉如何？给我们讲讲呗。到现在我一个字还没听着呢。”

## Dedication / 献辞

===Original===
To 

 My Brother 

 Dick
===Chinese===
献给

我的兄弟

迪克（Dick）

## XIII Deus Ex Machina / 十三 解围之神（Deus ex machina）

===Original===
It did not occur to Sheen immediately that his boat had actually gone. The full beauty of the situation was some moments in coming home to him. At first he merely thought that somebody had moved it to another part of the bank, as the authorities at the inn had done once or twice in the past, to make room for the boats of fresh visitors. Walking along the lawn in search of it, he came upon the stake to which Dunstable’s submerged craft was attached. He gave the rope a tentative pull, and was surprised to find that there was a heavy drag on the end of it.
Then suddenly the truth flashed across him. “Heavens!” he cried, “it’s sunk.”
Joe Bevan and other allies lent their aid to the pulling. The lost boat came out of the river like some huge fish, and finally rested on the bank, oozing water and drenching the grass in all directions.
Joe Bevan stooped down, and examined it in the dim light.
“What’s happened here, sir,” he said, “is that there’s a plank gone from the bottom. Smashed clean out, it is. Not started it isn’t. Smashed clean out. That’s what it is. Someone must have been here and done it.”
===Chinese===
希恩起初并没想到船是真的没了。这局面的全部严重性，过了好一会儿才在他心里落定。起初他只当是有人把它挪到了河岸另一处——客栈方面从前也这么干过一两回，好给新来客人的船腾地方。他沿着草坪一路找去，撞见了那根系着邓斯特布尔那艘沉船的木桩。他试探着拉了拉绳子，惊异地发现绳头坠着沉沉的一大团。
接着，真相倏地闪过脑际。“天哪！”他叫道，“船沉了。”
乔·贝文和其他几位盟友一起帮忙拉拽。那条失踪的船像一条大鱼似的出了河，最后搁在岸上，兀自淌着水，把四下的草地浇了个透湿。
乔·贝文俯下身，就着昏暗的光线察看。
“这儿出的事，先生，”他说，“是船底掉了一块板。整块砸穿的，可不是松动。整块砸穿，就是这么回事。准是有人到这儿来干的。”

===Original===
Sheen looked at the boat, and saw that he was right. A plank in the middle had been splintered. It looked as if somebody had driven some heavy instrument into it. As a matter of fact, Albert had effected the job with the butt-end of an oar.
The damage was not ruinous. A carpenter could put the thing right at no great expense. But it would take time. And meanwhile the minutes were flying, and lockup was now little more than half an hour away.
“What’ll you do, sir?” asked Bevan.
That was just what Sheen was asking himself. What could he do? The road to the school twisted and turned to such an extent that, though the distance from the “Blue Boar” to Seymour’s was only a couple of miles as the crow flies, he would have to cover double that distance unless he took a shortcut across the fields. And if he took a shortcut in the dark he was certain to lose himself. It was a choice of evils. The “Blue Boar” possessed but one horse and trap, and he had seen that driven away to the station in charge of a fisherman’s luggage half an hour before.
“I shall have to walk,” he said.
===Chinese===
希恩看了看船，发现他说的没错。中间一块船板裂成了碎片，看上去像是有人用什么重家伙凿进去的。事实是，阿尔伯特用一支桨的桨柄完成了这件活计。
损失并非毁灭性的。找个木匠花不了几个钱就能修好，可那需要时间。而这会儿分分秒秒正飞逝而去，离锁舍已只有半个钟头了。
“您打算怎么办，先生？”贝文问。
这正是希恩在问自己的。他能怎么办？通往学校的路弯弯绕绕得厉害，从“蓝野猪”客栈（“Blue Boar”）到西摩宿舍直线距离不过两英里，可若不抄田野间的近道，他就得走上双倍的路。而黑天里抄近道，他准会迷路。真是两害相权取其轻。“蓝野猪”客栈只有一辆单马马车，而且半个钟头前他已亲眼见它载着一位渔夫的行李往车站去了。
“我只得步行了。”他说。

===Original===
“It’s a long way. You’ll be late, won’t you?” said Mr. Bevan.
“It can’t be helped. I suppose I shall. I wonder who smashed that boat,” he added after a pause.
Passing through the inn on his way to the road, he made inquiries. It appeared that two young gentlemen from the school had been there to tea. They had arrived in a boat and gone away in a boat. Nobody else had come into the inn. Suspicion obviously rested upon them.
“Do you remember anything about them?” asked Sheen.
Further details came out. One of the pair had worn a cap like Sheen’s. The other’s headgear, minutely described, showed him that its owner was a member of the school second eleven.
===Chinese===
“路可不近。你要迟到了，是不是？”贝文先生说。
“没法子。大概是要迟了。”顿了顿他又说，“真不知是谁砸了那条船。”
上大路前穿过客栈时，他打听了一番。据说有两位学校的年轻绅士来喝过下午茶，坐船来，又坐船走的。此外没有别人进过客栈。嫌疑显然落在这两位身上。
“关于他们，你们还记得什么吗？”希恩问。
又问出了更多细节。两人中有一个戴的帽子与希恩的那顶相仿；另一人的头上穿戴，经仔细描述，表明其主人是学校乙组足球队的队员。

===Original===
Sheen pursued the inquiry. He would be so late in any case that a minute or so more or less would make no material difference; and he was very anxious to find out, if possible, who it was that had placed him in this difficulty. He knew that he was unpopular in the school, but he had not looked for this sort of thing.
Then somebody suddenly remembered having heard one of the pair address the other by name.
“What name?” asked Sheen.
His informant was not sure. Would it be Lindon?
“Linton,” said Sheen.
===Chinese===
希恩追问下去。反正他已经必迟无疑，多一两分钟无关大局；而他极想弄清楚，究竟是谁把他置于这步困境——如果弄得清的话。他知道自己在学校里不得人心，可他没料到会有人来这一手。
这时有人忽然记起，曾听见那两人中的一个喊过另一个的名字。
“什么名字？”希恩问。
提供线索的人记不真切。是不是叫林登（Lindon）？
“是林顿。”希恩说。

===Original===
That was it.
Sheen thanked him and departed, still puzzled. Linton, as he knew him, was not the sort of fellow to do a thing like that. And the other, the second eleven man, must be Dunstable. They were always about together. He did not know much about Dunstable, but he could hardly believe that this sort of thing was his form either. Well, he would have to think of that later. He must concentrate himself now on covering the distance to the school in the minimum of time. He looked at his watch. Twenty minutes more. If he hurried, he might not be so very late. He wished that somebody would come by in a cart, and give him a lift.
He stopped and listened. No sound of horse’s hoof broke the silence. He walked on again.
Then, faint at first, but growing stronger every instant, there came from some point in the road far behind him a steady droning sound. He almost shouted with joy. A motor! Even now he might do it.
But could he stop it? Would the motorist pay any attention to him, or would he flash past and leave him in the dust? From the rate at which the drone increased the car seemed to be travelling at a rare speed.
===Chinese===
正是这个。
希恩道了谢，满腹狐疑地离开了。就他所知，林顿不是干这种事的人。另一个，那个乙组队的人，准是邓斯特布尔。他们俩总在一处晃。他对邓斯特布尔知之甚少，但很难相信这等行径也是他的路数。罢了，这事只好回头再想。眼下他必须心无旁骛，用最短的时间赶完这段路。他看了看表：还剩二十分钟。要是抓紧，也许不至于迟到得太离谱。他盼着有谁能赶辆大车路过，捎他一程。
他停下侧耳细听。寂静中没有半点马蹄声。他又走了下去。
就在这时，从身后远处路面的某一点，传来一阵平稳的嗡嗡声，起初微弱，随即一刻比一刻响亮。他险些欢呼出声。汽车！事到如今说不定还来得及。
可他拦得住吗？开车的人会理会他吗，还是会一闪而过，把他撇在尘土里？听那嗡声增大的速度，车似乎正以罕见的高速行驶。

===Original===
He moved to one side of the road, and waited. He could see the lights now, flying towards him.
Then, as the car hummed past, he recognised its driver, and put all he knew into a shout.
“Bruce!” he cried.
For a moment it seemed as if he had not been heard. The driver paid not the smallest attention, as far as he could see. He looked neither to the left nor to right. Then the car slowed down, and, backing, came slowly to where he stood.
“Hullo,” said the driver, “who’s that?”
===Chinese===
他挪到路边，等着。此刻他已能看见车灯，正朝他飞来。
车嗡嗡驶过的一瞬，他认出了开车的人，便使出浑身解数大喊了一声。
“布鲁斯！”他喊道。
有一阵子他似乎没被听见。就他所能判断的，开车人对他的招呼毫不在意，目不斜视。随后车慢了下来，倒着退回，缓缓来到他站的地方。
“喂，”开车人说，“是谁？”

===Original===
Jack Bruce was alone in the car, muffled to the eyes in an overcoat. It was more by his general appearance than his face that Sheen had recognised him.
“It’s me, Sheen. I say, Bruce, I wish you’d give me a lift to Seymour’s, will you?”
There was never any waste of words about Jack Bruce. Of all the six hundred and thirty-four boys at Wrykyn he was probably the only one whose next remark in such circumstances would not have been a question. Bruce seldom asked questions﻿—never, if they wasted time.
“Hop in,” he said.
Sheen consulted his watch again.
===Chinese===
杰克·布鲁斯（Jack Bruce）独自坐在车里，大衣裹到眼睛底下。希恩认出他，与其说是凭那张脸，不如说是凭他的整个轮廓。
“是我，希恩。我说，布鲁斯，劳驾捎我到西摩宿舍，行吗？”
杰克·布鲁斯说话从不浪费字句。雷金学院六百三十四个男生里，处在这样的情形下，下一句接的不是问话的，多半只有他一人。布鲁斯很少发问——要是发问费时间，就压根儿不问。
“上车。”他说。
希恩又看了看表。

===Original===
“Lockup’s in a quarter of an hour,” he said, “but they give us ten minutes’ grace. That allows us plenty of time, doesn’t it?”
“Do it in seven minutes, if you like.”
“Don’t hurry,” said Sheen. “I’ve never been in a motor before, and I don’t want to cut the experience short. It’s awfully good of you to give me a lift.”
“That’s all right,” said Bruce.
“Were you going anywhere? Am I taking you out of your way?”
===Chinese===
“一刻钟后就锁舍了，”他说，“不过还能宽限十分钟。时间尽够了，是不是？”
“愿意的话，七分钟就到。”
“别赶，”希恩说，“我从没坐过汽车，可不想把这番体验缩短。你肯捎我一程，真是太好了。”
“没什么。”布鲁斯说。
“你原是要去哪儿吗？我是不是让你绕路了？”

===Original===
“No. I was just trying the car. It’s a new one. The pater’s just got it.”
“Do you do much of this?” said Sheen.
“Good bit. I’m going in for the motor business when I leave school.”
“So all this is training?”
“That’s it.”
===Chinese===
“没有。我就是试试车。新车，老爸刚买的。”
“你常开吗？”希恩问。
“常开。毕业后我打算干汽车这一行。”
“这么说，全都是练手了？”
“正是。”

===Original===
There was a pause.
“You seemed to be going at a good pace just now,” said Sheen.
“About thirty miles an hour. She can move all right.”
“That’s faster than you’re allowed to go, isn’t it?”
“Yes.”
===Chinese===
双方沉默了一阵。
“方才你那车速可不慢。”希恩说。
“一个钟头三十英里上下。她跑起来不含糊。”
“这可比规定的限速快了，对吧？”
“对。”

===Original===
“You’ve never been caught, have you?”
“Not yet. I want to see how much pace I can get out of her, because she’ll be useful when the election really comes on. Bringing voters to the poll, you know. That’s why the pater bought this new car. It’s a beauty. His other’s only a little runabout.”
“Doesn’t your father mind your motoring?”
“Likes it,” said Jack Bruce.
It seemed to Sheen that it was about time that he volunteered some information about himself, instead of plying his companion with questions. It was pleasant talking to a Wrykinian again; and Jack Bruce had apparently either not heard of the Albert incident, or else he was not influenced by it in any way.
===Chinese===
“你没被抓过吧？”
“还没有。我想试试能从她身上榨出多快的速度，因为等竞选真到了节骨眼上，她大有用处。你知道，拉选民去投票嘛。老爸就是为这个买的新车。真是辆好车。他原来那辆只是辆小代步车。”
“你父亲不介意你开车？”
“喜欢着呢。”杰克·布鲁斯说。
希恩觉得，也该轮到他主动讲讲自己的情况了，不能光拿问题去烦同伴。能再同一个雷金人说话是件愉快的事；而杰克·布鲁斯显然要么没听说过阿尔伯特事件，要么丝毫不为所动。

===Original===
“You’ve got me out of an awful hole, Bruce,” he began.
“That’s all right. Been out for a walk?”
“I’d been to the ‘Blue Boar.’ ”
“Oh!” said Bruce. He did not seem to wish to know why Sheen had been there.
Sheen proceeded to explain.
===Chinese===
“你可把我从大坑里捞出来了，布鲁斯。”他开了口。
“没什么。出去散步了？”
“我去了趟‘蓝野猪’客栈。”
“哦！”布鲁斯说。他似乎并不想知道希恩去那儿干什么。
希恩接着解释起来。

===Original===
“I suppose you’ve heard all about me,” he said uncomfortably. “About the town, you know. That fight. Not joining in.”
“Heard something about it,” said Bruce.
“I went downtown again after that,” said Sheen, “and met the same fellows who were fighting Linton and the others. They came for me, and I was getting awfully mauled when Joe Bevan turned up.”
“Oh, is Joe back again?”
“Do you know him?” asked Sheen in surprise.
===Chinese===
“我想我的事你都听说了，”他局促地说，“镇上那回，你知道。那场打斗。我没上前。”
“听说过一些。”布鲁斯说。
“那之后我又下了一趟镇，”希恩说，“撞上了先前正跟林顿他们打的那伙人。他们朝我扑过来，我眼看要被打得够呛，恰好乔·贝文赶到了。”
“哦，乔回来了？”
“你认识他？”希恩诧异地问。

===Original===
“Oh yes. I used to go to the ‘Blue Boar’ to learn boxing from him all last summer holidays.”
“Did you really? Why, that’s what I’m doing now.”
“Good man,” said Bruce.
“Isn’t he a splendid teacher?”
“Ripping.”
===Chinese===
“当然。去年整个暑假，我常去‘蓝野猪’客栈跟他学拳击。”
“真的吗？嘿，我现在干的就是这个。”
“好样的。”布鲁斯说。
“他是不是位了不起的老师？”
“没得说。”

===Original===
“But I didn’t know you boxed, Bruce. You never went in for any of the School competitions.”
“I’m rather a rottenweight. Ten six. Too heavy for the Lightweights and not heavy enough for the Middles. Besides, the competitions here are really inter-house. They don’t want day-boys going in for them. Are you going to box for Seymour’s?”
“That’s what I want to do. You see, it would be rather a score, wouldn’t it? After what’s happened, you know.”
“I suppose it would.”
“I should like to do something. It’s not very pleasant,” he added, with a forced laugh, “being considered a disgrace to the house, and cut by everyone.”
===Chinese===
“可我不知道你会拳击啊，布鲁斯。学校的比赛你一次也没参加过。”
“我这体重最尴尬。十英石六磅。打轻量级太重，打中量级又不够重。再说，这里的比赛其实是宿舍之间的，不欢迎走读生参加。你要代表西摩宿舍出赛吗？”
“我正有此意。你知道，出了那档子事之后，这可算得上一雪前耻了，对不对？”
“大概是的。”
“我总想做点什么。被人当作宿舍的耻辱、被所有人冷落，”他强笑着补充道，“滋味不太好受。”

===Original===
“Suppose not.”
“The difficulty is Drummond. You see, we are both the same weight, and he’s much better than I am. I’m hoping that he’ll go in for the Middles and let me take the Lightweights. There’s nobody he couldn’t beat in the Middles, though he would be giving away a stone.”
“Have you asked him?”
“Not yet. I want to keep it dark that I’m learning to box, just at present.”
“Spring it on them suddenly?”
===Chinese===
“想来是不好受。”
“难就难在德拉蒙德。你知道，我俩体重相当，而他比我强得多。我盼着他去报中量级，把轻量级让给我。中量级里没有他打不赢的人，哪怕他要以一英石之差让着对手。”
“你问过他吗？”
“还没有。眼下我还想瞒着我在学拳这件事。”
“到时候给他们个突然袭击？”

===Original===
“Yes. Of course, I can’t let it get about that I go to Joe Bevan, because I have to break bounds every time I do it.”
“The upper river’s out of bounds now for boarders, isn’t it?”
“Yes.”
Jack Bruce sat in silence for a while, his gaze concentrated on the road in front of him.
“Why go by river at all?” he said at last. “If you like, I’ll run you to the ‘Blue Boar’ in the motor every day.”
===Chinese===
“正是。当然，我去乔·贝文那儿的事不能传开，因为每去一趟我都得越界犯规。”
“上游河道现在对寄宿生是禁区了，对吧？”
“对。”
杰克·布鲁斯沉默地坐了一会儿，目光凝视着前方的路。
“干吗非要走河道？”他终于开口，“你要愿意，我每天开车送你去‘蓝野猪’客栈。”

===Original===
“Oh, I say, that’s awfully decent of you,” said Sheen.
“I should like to see old Joe again. I think I’ll come and spar, too. If you’re learning, what you want more than anything is somebody your own size to box with.”
“That’s just what Joe was saying. Will you really? I should be awfully glad if you would. Boxing with Joe is all right, but you feel all the time he’s fooling with you. I should like to try how I got on with somebody else.”
“You’d better meet me here, then, as soon after school as you can.”
As he spoke, the car stopped.
===Chinese===
“哎呀，你可真够意思。”希恩说。
“我也想再见见老乔。我看我还可以来陪练。你要学拳，最要紧的就是有个跟你身材相当的人对打。”
“乔正是这么说的。你当真愿意？那可太好了。跟乔对打固然好，可你时时刻刻都觉得他是在陪你玩儿。我想试试换个人我能打成什么样。”
“那你最好一放学就尽快在这儿同我会合。”
说话间，车停了。

===Original===
“Where are we?” asked Sheen.
“Just at the corner of the road behind the houses.”
“Oh, I know. Hullo, there goes the lockup bell. I shall do it comfortably.”
He jumped down.
“I say, Bruce,” he said, “I really am most awfully obliged for the lift. Something went wrong with my boat, and I couldn’t get back in it. I should have been frightfully in the cart if you hadn’t come by.”
===Chinese===
“我们到哪儿了？”希恩问。
“就在房子后面那条路的拐角上。”
“哦，我知道了。听，锁舍铃响了。我稳稳赶得上。”
他跳下车。
“我说，布鲁斯，”他说，“捎我这一程，我实在感激不尽。我的船出了毛病，没法坐它回来。要不是你路过，我可就惨透了。”

===Original===
“That’s all right,” said Jack Bruce. “I say, Sheen!”
“Hullo?”
“Are you going to practise in the music-room after morning school tomorrow?”
“Yes. Why?”
“I think I’ll turn up.”
===Chinese===
“没什么。”杰克·布鲁斯说，“哎，希恩！”
“怎么？”
“明天上午放学后，你要去音乐室练琴吗？”
“去。怎么了？”
“我想我会过去。”

===Original===
“I wish you would.”
“What’s that thing that goes like this? I forget most of it.”
He whistled a few bars.
“That’s a thing of Greig’s,” said Sheen.
===Chinese===
“那敢情好。”
“那支曲子是怎么哼来着？大半我都忘了。”
他吹了几小节口哨。
“那是格里格（Greig）的一支曲子。”希恩说。

===Original===
“You might play it tomorrow,” said Bruce.
“Rather. Of course I will.”
“Thanks,” said Jack Bruce. “Good night.”
He turned the car, and vanished down the road. From the sound Sheen judged that he was once more travelling at a higher rate of speed than the local police would have approved.
===Chinese===
“明天你就弹那支吧。”布鲁斯说。
“当然。一定弹。”
“谢了。”杰克·布鲁斯说，“晚安。”
他调转车头，消失在路的尽头。听那声响，希恩判断他再一次以当地警方不会赞许的速度疾驰而去。

## XVI Drummond Goes Into Retirement / 十六 德拉蒙德引退

===Original===
The return journey of a school team after a crushing defeat in a foreign match is never a very exhilarating business. Those members of the side who have not yet received their colours are wondering which of them is to be sacrificed to popular indignation and “chucked”: the rest, who have managed to get their caps, are feeling that even now two-thirds of the school will be saying that they are not worth a place in the third fifteen; while the captain, brooding apart, is becoming soured at the thought that Posterity will forget what little good he may have done, and remember only that it was in his year that the school got so many points taken off them by So-and-So. Conversation does not ripple and sparkle during these homecomings. The Wrykyn team made the journey in almost unbroken silence. They were all stiff and sore, and their feelings were such as to unfit them for talking to people.
The school took the thing very philosophically﻿—a bad sign. When a school is in a healthy, normal condition, it should be stirred up by a bad defeat by another school, like a disturbed wasps’ nest. Wrykyn made one or two remarks about people who could not play footer for toffee, and then let the thing drop.
Sheen was too busy with his work and his boxing to have much leisure for mourning over this latest example of the present inefficiency of the school. The examination for the Gotford was to come off in two days, and the inter-house boxing was fixed for the following Wednesday. In five days, therefore, he would get his chance of retrieving his lost place in the school. He was certain that he could, at any rate make a very good show against anyone in the school, even Drummond. Joe Bevan was delighted with his progress, and quoted Shakespeare volubly in his admiration. Jack Bruce and Francis added their tribute, and the knife and boot boy paid him the neatest compliment of all by refusing point-blank to have any more dealings with him whatsoever. His professional duties, explained the knife and boot boy, did not include being punched in the heye by blokes, and he did not intend to be put upon.
===Chinese===
一支校队在外校比赛惨败之后归程，从来不是一件多么快活的差事。队里还没挣到队色徽章的成员，正心里打鼓：民愤之下，牺牲品会轮到谁，谁会被“踢出去”；其余那些总算拿到了队帽的，则感觉即便到了现在，全校仍有三分之二的人在说，他们连橄榄球三队的位置都配不上；而队长独自闷坐着越想越苦：后人定会忘掉他打下过的那一点点江山，只记得正是在他任内，学校被某某某砍走了那么多分。这类回校路上，谈话是不会叮咚冒泡、熠熠生辉的。雷金学院全队几乎是全程一言不发地赶完这趟路的。人人都浑身酸痛，那心情也实在不宜与人交谈。
全校对这事的态度却达观得很——这可是个不祥之兆。一所学校若处于健康正常的状态，吃了别校一场惨败，就该像被捅了的马蜂窝一样炸起来。雷金不过就“连半点橄榄球都不会踢的家伙”发了一两句议论，便任这事不了了之。
希恩忙于功课和拳击，没多少闲情为学校眼下这般不济的最新例证哀叹。戈特福德（Gotford）奖学金考试两天后就要举行，宿舍拳击赛则定在下个星期三。也就是说，五天之内，他就有机会挽回自己在这所学校里失去的地位。他有把握，无论如何也能跟全校随便哪一位——哪怕是德拉蒙德——杀得有模有样。乔·贝文对他的进步喜出望外，钦佩之余滔滔不绝地引用起莎士比亚。杰克·布鲁斯和弗朗西斯也补上了各自的颂词，而那位磨刀擦靴的小伙计则给了他最干脆的恭维：一口回绝，声明从此概不奉陪。据磨刀擦靴的小伙计解释，他的本职工作可不包括挨家伙们照着眼珠子揍，他也不打算受这份窝囊气。

===Original===
“You’ll do all right,” said Jack Bruce, as they were motoring home, “if they’ll let you go in for it all. But how do you know they will? Have they chosen the men yet?”
“Not yet. They don’t do it till the day before. But there won’t be any difficulty about that. Drummond will let me have a shot if he thinks I’m good enough.”
“Oh, you’re good enough,” said Bruce.
And when, on Monday evening, Francis, on receipt of no fewer than four blows in a single round﻿—a record, shook him by the hand and said that if ever he happened to want a leetle darg that was a perfect bag of tricks and had got a pedigree, mind you, he, Francis, would be proud to supply that animal, Sheen felt that the moment had come to approach Drummond on the subject of the house boxing. It would be a little awkward at first, and conversation would probably run somewhat stiffly; but all would be well once he had explained himself.
But things had been happening in his absence which complicated the situation. Allardyce was having tea with Drummond, who had been stopping in with a sore throat. He had come principally to make arrangements for the match between his house and Seymour’s in the semifinal round of the competition.
===Chinese===
“你会打赢的，”坐车回家的路上，杰克·布鲁斯说，“前提是他们肯让你报名参赛。可你怎么知道他们肯？人选定了吗？”
“还没。他们要到赛前一天才定人。不过这不会有困难的。只要德拉蒙德觉得我够格，就会让我试一试。”
“哦，你够格是肯定的，”布鲁斯说。
到了星期一晚上，弗朗西斯一个回合里竟挨了他足足四拳——破纪录了——握住他的手说，哪天他要是不巧想弄一条小狗狗儿（leetle darg），满肚子鬼机灵、血统又顶纯正的，请放心，他弗朗西斯保管自豪地把那条畜生给送到。希恩感到，就宿舍拳击赛的事去找德拉蒙德开口的时机到了。头一遭总会有点别扭，话头八成也放不开；可只要他把原委讲清，一切都会顺当。
可就在他不在的当口，出了一些事，把局面弄复杂了。阿拉代斯（Allardyce）正在德拉蒙德屋里喝茶——德拉蒙德嗓子疼，一直待在屋里没出去。他来主要是为了商定宿舍拳击赛半决赛中他那个宿舍对阵西摩宿舍的比赛安排。

===Original===
“You’re looking bad,” he said, taking a seat.
“I’m feeling bad,” said Drummond. For the past few days he had been very much out of sorts. He put it down to a chill caught after the Ripton match. He had never mustered up sufficient courage to sponge himself with cold water after soaking in a hot bath, and he occasionally suffered for it.
“What’s up?” inquired Allardyce.
“Oh, I don’t know. Sort of beastly feeling. Sore throat. Nothing much. Only it makes you feel rather rotten.”
Allardyce looked interested.
===Chinese===
“你脸色不好，”他拣了个座说。
“我人也难受，”德拉蒙德说。这几天他一直很不舒服。他把这归咎于里普顿那场赛后受的凉。泡完热水澡，他从来鼓不起足够的勇气用冷水擦身，因此时不时要吃点苦头。
“怎么回事？”阿拉代斯问。
“哦，说不上来。就是一种难受得要命的劲儿。嗓子疼。也没什么大不了。只是叫人浑身不自在。”
阿拉代斯露出了感兴趣的神色。

===Original===
“I say,” he said, “it looks as if﻿—I wonder. I hope you haven’t.”
“What?”
“Mumps. It sounds jolly like it.”
“Mumps! Of course I’ve not. Why should I?”
Allardyce produced a letter from his pocket. “I got this from Keith, the Ripton captain, this morning. You know they’ve had a lot of the thing there. Oh, didn’t you? That was why they had such a bad team out.”
===Chinese===
“我说，”他开了口，“这看起来像是——难说。我盼着你可别是。”
“别是什么？”
“腮腺炎。听上去太像了。”
“腮腺炎！我当然没有。凭什么该我得？”
阿拉代斯从口袋里掏出一封信。“今早收到的，基思（Keith）写来的，就是里普顿的队长。你知道他们学校这阵子闹这个闹得凶。哦，你不知道？怪不得他们那场上场的队那么糟。”

===Original===
“Bad team!” murmured Drummond.
“Well, I mean not their best team. They had four of their men down with mumps. Here’s what Keith says. Listen. Bit about hoping we got back all right, and so on, first. Then he says﻿—here it is, ‘Another of our fellows has got the mumps. One of the forwards; rather a long man who was good out of touch. He developed it a couple of days after the match. It’s lucky that all our card games are over. We beat John’s, Oxford, last Wednesday, and that finished the card. But it’ll rather rot up the House matches. We should have walked the cup, but there’s no knowing what will happen now. I hope none of your lot caught the mumps from Browning during the game. It’s quite likely, of course. Browning ought not to have been playing, but I had no notion that there was anything wrong with him. He never said he felt bad.’ You’ve got it, Drummond. That’s what’s the matter with you.”
“Oh, rot,” said Drummond. “It’s only a chill.”
But the school doctor, who had looked in at the house to dose a small Seymourite who had indulged too heartily in the pleasures of the table, had other views, and before lockup Drummond was hurried off to the infirmary.
===Chinese===
“糟的队！”德拉蒙德喃喃道。
“呃，我是说，不是他们的最强阵容。他们有四个人染上腮腺炎躺倒了。基思是这么写的。你听着。先是一段盼我们平安返校之类的客套。然后他说——在这儿呢：‘我们又一个伙计得了腮腺炎。是个前锋；个子挺高，边线抛球很有一手。赛后两天发的病。幸好我们赛程上的比赛全打完了。上星期三我们赢了牛津的约翰学院（John's, Oxford），赛程就此收官。不过宿舍赛是要遭殃了。本来这奖杯我们手到擒来，现在可就没准了。但愿比赛的时候你们那边谁也没从布朗宁（Browning）身上传染上腮腺炎。当然，这很有可能。布朗宁本不该上场的，可我当时压根没想到他有什么不对劲。他从来没说过他难受。’你中招了，德拉蒙德。你的病根就是这个。”
“哦，胡扯，”德拉蒙德说。“不过是着了凉。”
可是校医——他来宿舍原是给一个贪嘴吃坏了的小西摩弟子开药的——却另有见解。熄灯锁门之前，德拉蒙德就被急急送进了医务室。

===Original===
Sheen went to Drummond’s study after preparation had begun, and was surprised to find him out. Not being on speaking terms with a single member of the house, he was always out-of-date as regarded items of school news. As a rule he had to wait until Jack Bruce told him before learning of any occurrence of interest. He had no notion that mumps was the cause of Drummond’s absence, and he sat and waited patiently for him in his study till the bell rang for prayers. The only possible explanation that occurred to him was that Drummond was in somebody else’s study, and he could not put his theory to the test by going and looking. It was only when Drummond did not put in an appearance at prayers that Sheen began to suspect that something might have happened.
It was maddening not to be able to make inquiries. He had almost decided to go and ask Linton, and risk whatever might be the consequences of such a step, when he remembered that the matron must know. He went to her, and was told that Drummond was in the infirmary.
He could not help seeing that this made his position a great deal more difficult. In ten minutes he could have explained matters to Drummond if he had found him in his study. But it would be a more difficult task to put the thing clearly in a letter.
Meanwhile, it was bedtime, and he soon found his hands too full with his dormitory to enable him to think out the phrasing of that letter. The dormitory, which was recruited entirely from the junior day-room, had heard of Drummond’s departure with rejoicings. They liked Drummond, but he was a good deal too fond of the iron hand for their tastes. A night with Sheen in charge should prove a welcome change.
===Chinese===
晚自习开始后，希恩去了德拉蒙德的书房，却惊愕地发现人不在。由于跟全宿舍没有一个人说话的交情，凡学校里的新闻，他向来是最后知道的那批。通常总要等杰克·布鲁斯讲给他听，他才能得知任何值得一提的事。他根本没想到腮腺炎是德拉蒙德缺席的原因，便坐在人家书房里耐心干等，一直等到晚祷的钟响。他所能想到的唯一可能的解释，是德拉蒙德在别人书房里，而这猜想又没法登门验证。直到晚祷时德拉蒙德仍然没有露面，希恩才开始疑心出了什么事。
打听不着消息，简直叫人发疯。他几乎已经打定主意去找林顿问，豁出去这一步可能招来什么后果算了，忽然想起护士长（matron）必定知情。他去找了她，得知德拉蒙德进了医务室。
他不能不看到，这使他的处境难办了许多。倘若在书房里堵到德拉蒙德，十分钟他便能把事情讲清。可是要在信里把话说明白，这难度就大得多了。
这时已到就寝时间，他很快便发现，宿舍里的事已经够他忙得四脚朝天，根本腾不出脑子去斟酌那封信的措辞。这间宿舍的成员清一色来自低年级走读生自习室，他们听说德拉蒙德走了，一片欢腾。他们喜欢德拉蒙德，只是依他们的口味，他太爱用铁腕了些。换希恩来管一晚上，理当是个受欢迎的变化。

===Original===
A deafening uproar was going on when Sheen arrived, and as he came into the room somebody turned the gas out. He found some matches on the chest of drawers, and lit it again just in time to see a sportive youth tearing the clothes off his bed and piling them on the floor. A month before he would not have known how to grapple with such a situation, but his evenings with Joe Bevan had given him the habit of making up his mind and acting rapidly. Drummond was wont to keep a swagger-stick by his bedside for the better observance of law and order. Sheen possessed himself of this swagger-stick, and reasoned with the sportive youth. The rest of the dormitory looked on in interested silence. It was a critical moment, and on his handling of it depended Sheen’s victory or defeat. If he did not keep his head he was lost. A dormitory is merciless to a prefect whose weakness they have discovered.
Sheen kept his head. In a quiet, pleasant voice, fingering the swagger-stick, as he spoke, in an absent manner, he requested his young friend to remake the bed﻿—rapidly and completely. For the space of five minutes no sound broke the silence except the rustle of sheets and blankets. At the end of that period the bed looked as good as new.
“Thanks,” said Sheen gratefully. “That’s very kind of you.”
He turned to the rest of the dormitory.
===Chinese===
希恩到时，宿舍里正闹得震天响，他刚进门，就有人把煤气灯关了。他在五斗橱上摸到几根火柴，重新点着灯，恰好看见一个顽劣少年正把他床上的铺盖往下扯，往地上堆。换一个月前，遇到这种局面他准会束手无策，可是跟乔·贝文度过的那些夜晚，已经让他养成了当机立断、雷厉风行的习惯。德拉蒙德惯于在床边备一根短杖（swagger-stick），以便更好地维护法纪。希恩把短杖抓到手里，同那位顽劣少年讲起了道理。宿舍其余的人饶有兴味地默观事态。这是危急关头，希恩的胜负存亡全系于他的处置。他若沉不住气，就全完了。宿舍对一个被他们摸清了底细的级长，是绝不留情的。
希恩沉住了气。他用平静悦耳的嗓音，一边心不在焉地把玩着短杖，一边请他的小朋友把床铺重新铺好——要快，还要彻底。整整五分钟，除了被单毯子的窸窣声，再没有任何声音打破寂静。五分钟期满，那张床焕然一新。
“多谢，”希恩感激地说。“您真客气。”
他转向宿舍其余的人。

===Original===
“Don’t let me detain you,” he said politely. “Get into bed as soon as you like.”
The dormitory got into bed sooner than they liked. For some reason the colossal rag they had planned had fizzled out. They were thoughtful as they crept between the sheets. Could these things be?
---
After much deliberation Sheen sent his letter to Drummond on the following day. It was not a long letter, but it was carefully worded. It explained that he had taken up boxing of late, and ended with a request that he might be allowed to act as Drummond’s understudy in the House competitions.
===Chinese===
“别让我耽搁各位，”他客气地说。“愿意什么时候睡就什么时候睡吧。”
宿舍众人上床上得比他们愿意的还要早。不知怎么，他们筹划的那场天大的胡闹就这么没了下文。他们钻进被窝时个个心事重重。这世道真是变了？
---
琢磨了半天，希恩第二天把信发给了德拉蒙德。信不长，但措辞很下了一番功夫。信里说明他近来练起了拳击，末了提出一项请求：希望获准在宿舍赛中充当德拉蒙德的替补。

===Original===
It was late that evening when the infirmary attendant came over with the answer.
Like the original letter, the answer was brief.
“Dear Sheen,” wrote Drummond, “thanks for the offer. I am afraid I can’t accept it. We must have the best man. Linton is going to box for the House in the Lightweights.”
===Chinese===
当天晚上很晚了，医务室的杂役捎来了回信。
和原信一样，回信很短。
“亲爱的希恩，”德拉蒙德写道，“谢谢你的好意。恐怕我不能接受。我们必须上最强的人。轻量级出战宿舍赛的，将是林顿。”

## XII Dunstable and Linton Go Up the River / 十二 邓斯特布尔与林顿泛舟河上

===Original===
There are some proud, spirited natures which resent rules and laws on principle as attempts to interfere with the rights of the citizen. As the Duchess in the play said of her son, who had had unpleasantness with the authorities at Eton because they had been trying to teach him things, “Silwood is a sweet boy, but he will not stand the bearing-rein.” Dunstable was also a sweet boy, but he, too, objected to the bearing-rein. And Linton was a sweet boy, and he had similar prejudices. And this placing of the town out of bounds struck both of them simultaneously as a distinct attempt on the part of the headmaster to apply the bearing-rein.
“It’s all very well to put it out of bounds for the kids,” said Dunstable, firmly, “but when it comes to Us﻿—why, I never heard of such a thing.”
Linton gave it as his opinion that such conduct was quite in a class of its own as regarded cool cheek.
“It fairly sneaks,” said Linton, with forced calm, “the Garibaldi.”
“Kids,” proceeded Dunstable, judicially, “are idiots, and can’t be expected to behave themselves downtown. Put the show out of bounds to them if you like. But We﻿—”
“We!” echoed Linton.
===Chinese===
天下有种高傲刚烈的天性，原则上一概憎恶规章制度，视之为对公民权利的干涉。正如那出戏里的公爵夫人谈起她在伊顿公学（Eton）跟校方闹了不愉快的儿子——校方竟一直想教他点东西——时所说：“西尔伍德（Silwood）是个乖孩子，可他不肯受勒颈缰（bearing-rein）的拘束。”邓斯特布尔也是个乖孩子，可他同样反对勒颈缰。林顿也是个乖孩子，也怀着类似的成见。于是，把镇子划为禁足区这件事，同时被两人认定：这分明是校长在给他们上勒颈缰。
“给小毛孩子们划禁区，倒也罢了，”邓斯特布尔语气坚决，“可轮到‘吾辈’头上——嗐，这种事我闻所未闻。”
林顿发表意见说，论厚颜无耻的程度，此等行径堪称独步。
“这一手阴损到家了，”林顿强自镇定地说，“简直是加里波第（Garibaldi，当时的校园俚语，用作惊叹语）。”
“小毛孩子嘛，”邓斯特布尔以法官的口吻继续道，“本是蠢材，指望他们进城守规矩，本属妄念。你尽可以把这去处对他们划为禁地。可是‘吾辈’——”
“‘吾辈’！”林顿应声道。

===Original===
“The fact is,” said Dunstable, “it’s a beastly nuisance, but we shall have to go downtown and up the river just to assert ourselves. We can’t have the thin end of the wedge coming and spoiling our liberties. We may as well chuck life altogether if we aren’t able to go to the town whenever we like.”
“And Albert will be pining away,” added Linton.
---
“Hullo, young gentlemen,” said the town boatman, when they presented themselves to him, “what can I do for you?”
“I know it seems strange,” said Dunstable, “but we want a boat. We are the Downtrodden British Schoolboys’ League for Demanding Liberty and seeing that We Get It. Have you a boat?”
The man said he believed he had a boat. In fact, now that he came to think of it, he rather fancied he had one or two. He proceeded to get one ready, and the two martyrs to the cause stepped in.
===Chinese===
“实情是，”邓斯特布尔说，“这麻烦透顶，可咱们偏得进城、上河走一遭，好昭示咱们的存在。不能让那楔子尖钻进来，毁了咱们的自由。要是连想去镇上就去不成，那不如干脆别活了。”
“再说阿尔伯特也要相思成疾了。”林顿补充道。
---
“喂，两位年轻先生，”镇上的船夫见二人上门，招呼道，“有什么可以效劳？”
“我知道这事听着蹊跷，”邓斯特布尔说，“可我们要雇一条船。我们是‘受压迫英国学生争取自由暨确保到手同盟’。您有船吗？”
船夫说，他记得自己是有条船的。确实，这么一想，他仿佛还觉得有一两条。他随即去备了一条，两位为事业献身的殉道士登船入座。

===Original===
Dunstable settled himself in the stern, and collected the rudder-lines.
“Hullo,” said Linton, “aren’t you going to row?”
“It may be only my foolish fancy,” replied Dunstable, “but I rather think you’re going to do that. I’ll steer.”
“Beastly slacker,” said Linton. “Anyhow, how far are we going? I’m not going to pull all night.”
“If you row for about half an hour without exerting yourself﻿—and I can trust you not to do that﻿—and then look to your left, you’ll see a certain hostelry, if it hasn’t moved since I was last there. It’s called the ‘Blue Boar.’ We will have tea there, and then I’ll pull gently back, and that will end the programme.”
“Except being caught in the town by half the masters,” said Linton. “Still, I’m not grumbling. This had to be done. Ready?”
===Chinese===
邓斯特布尔在船尾安顿下来，把舵绳收拢在手。
“喂，”林顿说，“你不划船吗？”
“兴许只是我的痴心妄想，”邓斯特布尔答道，“不过我倒觉得这活儿归你。我掌舵。”
“可恶的懒鬼。”林顿说，“话说回来，咱们去多远？我可不想划一宿。”
“你要是不费力气地划上半个钟头——我信得过你，你绝不至于费力气——然后往左边一望，就会瞧见一家客栈，前提是打我上次去后它没挪窝。那店名叫‘蓝野猪’。咱们在那儿喝个茶，然后我缓缓地划回来，节目单到此为止。”
“外加在镇上被半个学校的老师逮个正着。”林顿说，“不过，我没有怨言。这一趟非走不可。准备好了？”

===Original===
“Not just yet,” said Dunstable, looking past Linton and up the landing-stage. “Wait just one second. Here are some friends of ours.”
Linton looked over his shoulder.
“Albert!” he cried.
“And the blighter in the bowler who struck me divers blows in sundry places. Ah, they’ve sighted us.”
“What are you going to do? We can’t have another scrap with them.”
“Far from it,” said Dunstable gently. “Hullo, Albert. *And* my friend in the moth-eaten bowler! This is well met.”
===Chinese===
“且慢。”邓斯特布尔的目光越过林顿，朝码头栈桥上望去，“稍等片刻。来了几位咱们的朋友。”
林顿回头看去。
“阿尔伯特！”他叫道。
“还有那个戴圆顶礼帽、在我浑身上下各处捶了我好几下的混球。啊，他们瞅见咱们了。”
“你打算怎么办？跟他们再干一架可使不得。”
“差得远呢。”邓斯特布尔温文尔雅地说，“喂，阿尔伯特。*还有*我那位戴虫蛀礼帽的朋友！幸会幸会。”

===Original===
“You come out here,” said Albert, pausing on the brink.
“Why?” asked Dunstable.
“You see what you’ll get.”
“But we don’t want to see what we’ll get. You’ve got such a narrow mind, Albert﻿—may I call you Bertie? You seem to think that nobody has any pleasures except vulgar brawls. We are going to row up river, and think beautiful thoughts.”
Albert was measuring with his eye the distance between the boat and landing-stage. It was not far. A sudden spring.﻿ ﻿…
“If you want a fight, go up to the school and ask for Mr. Drummond. He’s the gentleman who sent you to hospital last time. Any time you’re passing, I’m sure he’d﻿—”
===Chinese===
“你们俩给我出来。”阿尔伯特在岸边顿住脚说。
“为什么呀？”邓斯特布尔问。
“让你们尝尝厉害。”
“可我们不想尝那份厉害。你的心胸太狭窄了，阿尔伯特——我能叫你伯蒂（Bertie）吗？你好像认定，除了粗鄙的斗殴，世上便无人有别的乐趣。我们这是要泛舟河上，想些美妙的心事。”
阿尔伯特正用眼睛丈量小船与栈桥之间的距离。不算远。只待猛然一扑……
“要是你想打架，就上学校去找德拉蒙德先生。上次把阁下送进医院的，正是那位君子。你哪天路过，他肯定乐意——”

===Original===
Albert leaped.
But Linton had had him under observation, and, as he sprung, pushed vigorously with his oar. The gap between boat and shore widened in an instant, and Albert, failing to obtain a foothold on the boat, fell back, with a splash that sent a cascade over his friend and the boatman, into three feet of muddy water. By the time he had scrambled out, his enemies were moving pensively upstream.
The boatman was annoyed.
“Makin’ me wet and spoilin’ my paint﻿—what yer mean by it?”
“Me and my friend here we want a boat,” said Albert, ignoring the main issue.
“Want a boat! Then you’ll not get a boat. Spoil my cushions, too, would you? What next, I wonder! You go to Smith and ask *him* for a boat. Perhaps he ain’t so particular about having his cushions﻿—”
===Chinese===
阿尔伯特纵身一跃。
可林顿早把他盯在眼里，趁他跃起，猛力一推桨。船与岸的间隙霎时拉开，阿尔伯特在船上没踩到落脚处，仰面栽了回去，扑通一声，溅得他朋友和船夫一身水花，跌进三英尺深的泥汤里。等他连滚带爬地上了岸，他的仇人们已然悠悠然溯流而上了。
船夫恼了。
“弄得我湿淋淋，还毁了我的漆——你们这是啥意思？”
“我和我这位朋友想雇条船。”阿尔伯特说，对正题置之不理。
“想雇船！那你们就雇不着。还想糟蹋我的坐垫，是吧？我看还有什么花样！你们找史密斯（Smith）去，问*他*雇船。说不定他对他的坐垫不那么讲究——”

===Original===
“Orl right,” said Albert, “*orl* right.”
Mr. Smith proved more complaisant, and a quarter of an hour after Dunstable and Linton had disappeared, Albert and his friend were on the water. Moist outside, Albert burned with a desire for Revenge. He meant to follow his men till he found them. It almost seemed as if there would be a repetition of the naval battle which had caused the town to be put out of bounds. Albert was a quick-tempered youth, and he had swallowed fully a pint of Severn water.
---
Dunstable and Linton sat for some time in the oak parlour of the “Blue Boar.” It was late when they went out. As they reached the water’s edge Linton uttered a cry of consternation.
“What’s up?” asked Dunstable. “I wish you wouldn’t do that so suddenly. It gives me a start. Do you feel bad?”
“Great Scott! it’s gone.”
===Chinese===
“行喽，”阿尔伯特说，“行喽。”
史密斯先生果然好说话些。邓斯特布尔和林顿消失后一刻钟，阿尔伯特和他那位朋友也下了水。外头湿淋淋，心里火腾腾，阿尔伯特满腔复仇的渴望。他打定主意，一路跟到底，直到把那两个家伙找出来。眼看着，那场导致全镇被划为禁地的水上大战，几乎又要重演一遍。阿尔伯特是个火爆性子的少年，何况他还足足咽下了一品脱塞文河（Severn）的水。
---
邓斯特布尔和林顿在“蓝野猪”的橡木厅里坐了许久。出门时天色已晚。到得水边，林顿失声惊叫。
“怎么了？”邓斯特布尔问，“你别这么突然行不行。吓我一跳。不舒服吗？”
“天哪！它没了。”

===Original===
“The pain?”
“Our boat. I tied it up to this post.”
“You can’t have done. What’s that boat over there! That looks like ours.”
“No, it isn’t. That was there when we came. I noticed it. I tied ours up here, to this post.”
“This is a shade awkward,” said Dunstable thoughtfully. “You must have tied it up jolly rottenly. It must have slipped away and gone downstream. This is where we find ourselves in the cart. Right among the ribstons, by Jove. I feel like that Frenchman in the story, who lost his glasses just as he got to the top of the mountain, and missed the view. Altogezzer I do not vish I ’ad kom.”
“I’m certain I tied it up all right. And﻿—why, look! here’s the rope still on the pole, just as I left it.”
===Chinese===
“什么没了？疼吗？”
“咱们的船。我明明把它拴在这根桩上了。”
“不可能吧。那边那条是什么船！看着像咱们的。”
“不，不是。咱们来的时候它就在那儿，我留意到了。咱们那条我拴在这儿，这根桩上。”
“这可有点儿尴尬了。”邓斯特布尔若有所思地说，“你八成拴得糟透了。它准是脱了缆，往下游漂走了。这下咱们可栽进坑里了。说真的，麻烦大发了，天哪。我觉得自己活像故事里那个法国人：好不容易爬上山顶，眼镜偏偏丢了，风景白白错过。『总滴来说，我一点儿也不希望来过。』”
“我敢肯定拴得好好的。而且——咦，瞧！绳子还在桩上呢，就跟我留下来时一模一样。”

===Original===
For the first time Dunstable seemed interested.
“This is getting mysterious. Did we hire a rowing-boat or a submarine? There’s something on the end of this rope. Give it a tug, and see. There, didn’t you feel it?”
“I do believe,” said Linton in an awed voice, “the thing’s sunk.”
They pulled at the rope together. The waters heaved and broke, and up came the nose of the boat, to sink back with a splash as they loosened their hold.
“There are more things in Heaven and Earth﻿—” said Dunstable, wiping his hands. “If you ask me, I should say an enemy hath done this. A boat doesn’t sink of its own accord.”
“Albert!” said Linton. “The blackguard must have followed us up and done it while we were at tea.”
===Chinese===
邓斯特布尔头一回来了兴致。
“越来越邪门了。咱们雇的是划艇还是潜水艇？这绳子头上坠着东西呢。拽一把试试。瞧，觉出来没有？”
“我说，”林顿的声音里透着敬畏，“这船是沉了。”
两人一齐拉绳。水面翻涌绽开，船头破水而出，待他们一松手，又扑通一声沉了回去。
“天地之间的事情，原比哲学里梦想到的多——（莎士比亚语）”邓斯特布尔擦着手说，“要我说，必是仇敌下的手（语出《圣经》）。船是不会自己沉的。”
“阿尔伯特！”林顿说，“准是那无赖一路跟上来，趁咱们喝茶动的手。”

===Original===
“That’s about it,” said Dunstable. “And now﻿—how about getting home?”
“I suppose we’d better walk. We shall be hours late for lockup.”
“You,” said Dunstable, “may walk if you are fond of exercise and aren’t in a hurry. Personally, I’m going back by river.”
“But﻿—”
“That looks a good enough boat over there. Anyhow, we must make it do. One mustn’t be particular for once.”
“But it belongs﻿—what will the other fellow do?”
===Chinese===
“八九不离十。”邓斯特布尔说，“那么眼下——怎么回家？”
“我看咱们最好走着回去。赶锁门是赶不上了，得迟到好几个钟头。”
“你嘛，”邓斯特布尔说，“要是爱好运动又不赶时间，尽可以走回去。至于本人，我要坐船走水路。”
“可是——”
“那边那条船看着蛮不错。不管怎样，凑合用吧。偶尔一回，人不能太讲究。”
“可那是别人的——船主人怎么办？”

===Original===
“I can’t help *his* troubles,” said Dunstable mildly, “having enough of my own. Coming?”
---
It was about ten minutes later that Sheen, approaching the waterside in quest of his boat, found no boat there. The time was a quarter to six, and lockup was at six-thirty.
===Chinese===
“*他*的难处我管不着，”邓斯特布尔温和地说，“我自己的麻烦已经够多了。走不走？”
---
约莫十分钟后，希恩来到水边找他的船，却发现船已不在。此时是差一刻六点，锁门时间是六点半。

## I Expert Opinions / 一 行家高见

===Original===
“With apologies to gent opposite,” said Clowes, “I must say I don’t think much of the team.”
“Don’t apologise to *me*,” said Allardyce disgustedly, as he filled the teapot, “I think they’re rotten.”
“They ought to have got into form by now, too,” said Trevor. “It’s not as if this was the first game of the term.”
“First game!” Allardyce laughed shortly. “Why, we’ve only got a couple of club matches and the return match with Ripton to end the season. It is about time they got into form, as you say.”
Clowes stared pensively into the fire.
===Chinese===
“先向对面这位先生致歉，”克洛斯（Clowes）说，“恕我直言，我觉得这支队伍不怎么样。”
“别跟*我*道歉，”阿拉代斯（Allardyce）一边往茶壶里续水，一边满脸嫌弃地说，“我觉得他们糟透了。”
“他们也早该进入状态了，”特雷弗（Trevor）说，“这又不是本学期的头一场比赛。”
“头一场！”阿拉代斯短促地笑了一声。“嘿，这个赛季就剩两场俱乐部赛和一场对里普顿的回旋赛了。正如你所说，他们真到该进入状态的时候了。”
克洛斯若有所思地凝视着炉火。

===Original===
“They struck me,” he said, “as the sort of team who’d get into form somewhere in the middle of the cricket season.”
“That’s about it,” said Allardyce. “Try those biscuits, Trevor. They’re about the only good thing left in the place.”
“School isn’t what it was?” inquired Trevor, plunging a hand into the tin that stood on the floor beside him.
“No,” said Allardyce, “not only in footer but in everything. The place seems absolutely rotten. It’s bad enough losing all our matches, or nearly all. Did you hear that Ripton took thirty-seven points off us last term? And we only just managed to beat Greenburgh by a try to nil.”
“We got thirty points last year,” he went on. “Thirty-three, and forty-two the year before. Why, we’ve always simply walked them. It’s an understood thing that we smash them. And this year they held us all the time, and it was only a fluke that we scored at all. Their back miskicked, and let Barry in.”
===Chinese===
“依我看，”他说，“他们这帮人，怕是要等板球赛季进行到一半才找得着状态。”
“说得差不离，”阿拉代斯说，“尝尝那些饼干，特雷弗。这地方大概也就剩这一样好东西了。”
“学校不比从前了？”特雷弗问着，一只手探进身旁地板上的饼干筒。
“是啊，”阿拉代斯说，“不光足球，样样都不行了。这地方简直是烂透了。比赛场场都输——或者差不多场场都输，这就够受的了。你听说上学期里普顿从我们身上拿了三十七分吗？我们对格林伯格（Greenburgh），也只靠一次达阵对零分勉强取胜。”
“去年我们拿了三十分，”他继续说，“前年三十三分，大前年四十二分。嘿，从前我们一直是轻取他们，压着他们打，这是公认的事。可今年他们从头到尾跟我们咬着，我们能得分纯属侥幸——他们的后卫踢失一脚，才放巴里（Barry）突了进去。”

===Original===
“Barry struck me as the best of the outsides today,” said Clowes. “He’s heavier than he was, and faster.”
“He’s all right,” agreed Allardyce. “If only the centres would feed him, we might do something occasionally. But did you ever see such a pair of rotters?”
“The man who was marking me certainly didn’t seem particularly brilliant. I don’t even know his name. He didn’t do anything at footer in my time,” said Trevor.
“He’s a chap called Attell. He wasn’t here with you. He came after the summer holidays. I believe he was sacked from somewhere. He’s no good, but there’s nobody else. Colours have been simply a gift this year to anyone who can do a thing. Only Barry and myself left from last year’s team. I never saw such a clearance as there was after the summer term.”
“Where are the boys of the Old Brigade?” sighed Clowes.
===Chinese===
“今天巴里给我留下的印象，是外线队员里最好的一个，”克洛斯说，“他比从前壮了，也更快了。”
“他是不错，”阿拉代斯表示同意，“只要中锋肯给他喂球，我们偶尔还能有所作为。可你见过这么一对废物吗？”
“盯我那个人，确实瞧不出有什么高明。我连他名字都不知道。我上学那会儿，他在足球上毫无作为。”特雷弗说。
“他是个叫阿特尔（Attell）的家伙。你们在的时候他还不在这儿，是暑假以后来的。我听说他是从什么地方被撵出来的。他不行，可又没有别人。今年只要谁还像点样，校色袖标简直白送。去年一队里如今只剩巴里和我两个。暑假后那样一场大清洗，我从来没见过。”
“旧日的众好汉都到哪里去了？”克洛斯叹道。

===Original===
“I don’t know. I wish they were here,” said Allardyce.
Trevor and Clowes had come down, after the Easter term had been in progress for a fortnight, to play for an Oxford A team against the school. The match had resulted in an absurdly easy victory for the visitors by over forty points. Clowes had scored five tries off his own bat, and Trevor, if he had not fed his wing so conscientiously, would probably have scored an equal number. As it was, he had got through twice, and also dropped a goal. The two were now having a late tea with Allardyce in his study. Allardyce had succeeded Trevor as Captain of Football at Wrykyn, and had found the post anything but a sinecure.
For Wrykyn had fallen for the time being on evil days. It was experiencing the reaction which so often takes place in a school in the year following a season of exceptional athletic prosperity. With Trevor as captain of football, both the Ripton matches had been won, and also three out of the four other school matches. In cricket the eleven had had an even finer record, winning all their school matches, and likewise beating the M.C.C. and Old Wrykinians. It was too early to prophesy concerning the fortunes of next term’s cricket team, but, if they were going to resemble the fifteen, Wrykyn was doomed to the worst athletic year it had experienced for a decade.
“It’s a bit of a comedown after last season, isn’t it?” resumed Allardyce, returning to his sorrows. It was a relief to him to discuss his painful case without restraint.
“We were a fine team last year,” agreed Clowes, “and especially strong on the left wing. By the way, I see you’ve moved Barry across.”
===Chinese===
“我不知道。真希望他们还在。”阿拉代斯说。
复活节学期开学两周后，特雷弗和克洛斯回校来，代表一支牛津A队同学校打了一场。结果客队以四十几分的优势荒唐地轻松取胜。克洛斯凭一己之力独得五次达阵；特雷弗若不是那么恪尽职守地喂球给边锋，得分多半也不相上下。即便如此，他还是两度突破得分，又踢进一记落踢球。此刻，两人正在阿拉代斯的书房里同他一道吃晚茶。阿拉代斯继特雷弗之后接任雷金学院的足球队长，发现这差事绝非闲职。
原来雷金学院一时气数不济。学校正经历一种常见的反弹——一个大获全胜的体育年之后，第二年往往如此。特雷弗任队长时，两场对里普顿的比赛全部拿下，另外四场校际赛也赢了三场。板球队的战绩更佳，校际赛全胜，还击败了马里波恩板球俱乐部（M.C.C.）和老雷金人队（Old Wrykinians）。预言下学期板球队的命运还为时过早，可他们要是跟一队一个德行，雷金就注定要迎来十年来最惨淡的体育年。
“比起上个赛季，这落差可不小，是吧？”阿拉代斯回到他的伤心事上。能毫无顾忌地痛陈自己的难处，对他倒是一种宽解。
“去年我们是支强队，”克洛斯附和道，“左路尤其厉害。对了，我注意到你把巴里调到那边去了。”

===Original===
“Yes. Attell can’t pass much, but he passes better from right to left than from left to right; so, Barry being our scoring man, I shifted him across. The chap on the other wing, Stanning, isn’t bad at times. Do you remember him? He’s in Appleby’s. Then Drummond’s useful at half.”
“Jolly useful,” said Trevor. “I thought he would be. I recommended you last year to keep your eye on him.”
“Decent chap, Drummond,” said Clowes.
“About the only one there is left in the place,” observed Allardyce gloomily.
“Our genial host,” said Clowes, sawing at the cake, “appears to have that tired feeling. He seems to have lost that joie de vivre of his, what?”
===Chinese===
“是啊。阿特尔传球不行，但从右往左传总比从左往右强些；巴里既是我们的得分主力，我就把他调了过去。另一侧边锋那个叫斯坦宁的，有时倒也不赖。你记得他吗？他是阿普尔比宿舍的。还有德拉蒙德，打前卫挺顶用。”
“相当顶用，”特雷弗说，“我早料到了。去年我还建议你多留心他来着。”
“德拉蒙德是个好小子。”克洛斯说。
“差不多是这地方硕果仅存的一个了。”阿拉代斯阴郁地说。
“咱们这位和蔼的主人，”克洛斯一边切着蛋糕一边说，“似乎染上了那种倦怠症。他那股人生兴味（joie de vivre）好像不见了，嗯？”

===Original===
“It must be pretty sickening,” said Trevor sympathetically. “I’m glad I wasn’t captain in a bad year.”
“The rummy thing is that the worse they are, the more side they stick on. You see chaps who wouldn’t have been in the third in a good year walking about in first fifteen blazers, and first fifteen scarves, and first fifteen stockings, and sweaters with first fifteen colours round the edges. I wonder they don’t tattoo their faces with first fifteen colours.”
“It would improve some of them,” said Clowes.
Allardyce resumed his melancholy remarks. “But, as I was saying, it’s not only that the footer’s rotten. That you can’t help, I suppose. It’s the general beastliness of things that I bar. Rows with the town, for instance. We’ve been having them on and off ever since you left. And it’ll be worse now, because there’s an election coming off soon. Are you fellows stopping for the night in the town? If so, I should advise you to look out for yourselves.”
“Thanks,” said Clowes. “I shouldn’t like to see Trevor sandbagged. Nor indeed, should I﻿—for choice﻿—care to be sandbagged myself. But, as it happens, the good Donaldson is putting us up, so we escape the perils of the town.
===Chinese===
“那滋味一定够呛，”特雷弗同情地说，“幸亏我当队长没赶上荒年。”
“怪就怪在人越臭，架子越大。你会看见一些家伙——搁在好年景连三队都进不去——穿着一队的运动外套、一队的围巾、一队的长袜满街晃，毛衣边上还滚着一队的校色。我就纳闷，他们怎么不干脆拿一队校色往脸上刺花。”
“那倒能给其中几位增色。”克洛斯说。
阿拉代斯继续发他的牢骚：“不过，正如我方才所说，问题不光是足球烂——这个嘛，也算没法子。我受不了的是样样都烂。比如说，跟镇上打架。自打你们走后，这种事就没断过。往后还会更糟，因为竞选马上就要开始了。你们两位今晚要在镇上过夜吗？要是在，我劝你们自己当心点。”
“多谢，”克洛斯说，“我可不愿看见特雷弗吃闷棍。说实在的，要依我挑，我自己也不乐意吃闷棍。不过赶巧好心的唐纳森（Donaldson）先生留我们住，镇上的种种凶险便与我们无缘了。

===Original===
“Everybody seems so beastly slack now,” continued Allardyce. “It’s considered the thing. You’re looked on as an awful blood if you say you haven’t done a stroke of work for a week. I shouldn’t mind that so much if they were some good at anything. But they can’t do a thing. The footer’s rotten, the gymnasium six is made up of kids an inch high﻿—we shall probably be about ninetieth at the Public Schools’ Competition﻿—and there isn’t anyone who can play racquets for nuts. The only thing that Wrykyn’ll do this year is to get the Lightweights at Aldershot. Drummond ought to manage that. He won the Feathers last time. He’s nearly a stone heavier now, and awfully good. But he’s the only man we shall send up, I expect. Now that O’Hara and Moriarty are both gone, he’s the only chap we have who’s up to Aldershot form. And nobody else’ll take the trouble to practice. They’re all too slack.”
“In fact,” said Clowes, getting up, “as was only to be expected, the school started going to the dogs directly I left. We shall have to be pushing on now, Allardyce. We promised to look in on Seymour before we went to bed. Friend let us away.”
“Good night,” said Allardyce.
“What you want,” said Clowes solemnly, “is a liver pill. You are looking on life too gloomily. Take a pill. Let there be no stint. Take two. Then we shall hear your merry laugh ringing through the old cloisters once more. Buck up and be a bright and happy lad, Allardyce.”
“Take more than a pill to make me that,” growled that soured footballer.
===Chinese===
“如今人人都是一副要死不活的懒散相，”阿拉代斯接着说，“大家还引以为时髦。你要是说一个星期没摸过一下书本，人家就当你是个了不起的大人物。倘若他们哪样真拿得起来，我倒也不计较。可他们样样不行。足球烂，体操六人队全是些一寸来高的小豆丁——公学锦标赛上我们多半要排到第九十名开外——打墙网球更是没一个像样的。雷金今年唯一能拿得出手的，就是在奥尔德肖特（Aldershot）拿下轻量级。德拉蒙德该能办到，上一届他就是羽量级冠军。如今他重了近一英石，拳又好得很。不过我估计，我们也就只能送他一个人去。如今奥哈拉（O'Hara）和莫里亚蒂（Moriarty）都走了，够得上奥尔德肖特水准的只剩他一个。旁人谁也不肯下功夫练，全都懒透了。”
“由此可见，”克洛斯起身说道，“不出所料，我一离校，学校就开始每况愈下。我们这就得动身了，阿拉代斯。我们答应过睡觉前去西摩先生那儿露个面。朋友，放我们上路吧。”
“晚安。”阿拉代斯说。
“你需要的是，”克洛斯一本正经地说，“一剂肝药丸。你把人生看得太灰暗了。吃一丸吧，别省着。吃两丸。到那时，我们就又能听见你那爽朗的笑声回荡在古老的回廊之间了。振作起来，做个快活明亮的小伙子，阿拉代斯。”
“要变成那样，一丸药可不够。”这位满腹怨气的足球队长咕哝道。

===Original===
Mr. Seymour’s views on the school resembled those of Allardyce. Wrykyn, in his opinion, was suffering from a reaction.
“It’s always the same,” he said, “after a very good year. Boys leave, and it’s hard to fill their places. I must say I did not expect quite such a clearing out after the summer. We have had bad luck in that way. Maurice, for instance, and Robinson both ought to have had another year at school. It was quite unexpected, their leaving. They would have made all the difference to the forwards. You must have somebody to lead the pack who has had a little experience of first fifteen matches.”
“But even then,” said Clowes, “they oughtn’t to be so rank as they were this afternoon. They seemed such slackers.”
“I’m afraid that’s the failing of the school just now,” agreed Mr. Seymour. “They don’t play themselves out. They don’t put just that last ounce into their work which makes all the difference.”
Clowes thought of saying that, to judge by appearances, they did not put in even the first ounce; but refrained. However low an opinion a games’ master may have﻿—and even express﻿—of his team, he does not like people to agree too cordially with his criticisms.
===Chinese===
西摩先生对学校的看法与阿拉代斯如出一辙。依他看，雷金正害着一场盛极之后的疲软。
“大年之后总是这样，”他说，“老的走了，空缺难补。说实话，我没想到暑假后会走那么一大批。这方面我们运气不好。比如莫里斯（Maurice）和鲁滨逊（Robinson），两人本该再读一年。他们走得实在太突然。有他们在，前锋线会大不一样。统领前锋群的，总得是打过几场一队比赛、有点经验的人。”
“可即便如此，”克洛斯说，“他们也不至于差到今天下午那个地步。一个个活像懒骨头。”
“恐怕这正是眼下学校的通病，”西摩先生表示同意，“他们不肯倾尽全力。就差最后那一把劲——恰恰是那把劲决定高下——他们就是不使出来。”
克洛斯本想说，照今天的样子看，他们连头一把劲都没使出来，话到嘴边又咽了回去。一位执掌运动的教师，无论对自己球队的评价多低——甚至说出口——也不喜欢别人太过热烈地附和他的批评。

===Original===
“Allardyce seems rather sick about it,” said Trevor.
“I am sorry for Allardyce. It is always unpleasant to be the only survivor of an exceptionally good team. He can’t forget last year’s matches, and suffers continual disappointments because the present team does not play up to the same form.”
“He was saying something about rows with the town,” said Trevor, after a pause.
“Yes, there has certainly been some unpleasantness lately. It is the penalty we pay for being on the outskirts of a town. Four years out of five nothing happens. But in the fifth, when the school has got a little out of hand﻿—”
“Oh, then it really *has* got out of hand?” asked Clowes.
===Chinese===
“阿拉代斯好像挺为此苦恼。”特雷弗说。
“我替阿拉代斯难过。作为一支特别优秀的队伍里硕果仅存的人，滋味总是不好受。他忘不掉去年的那些比赛，如今球队一拿不出当年的水准，他便失望个不停。”
“他刚说到同镇上的冲突。”特雷弗顿了顿说。
“是的，近来确实有些不愉快的事。这是我们学校地处镇子边缘所付的代价。五年里有四年风平浪静，可到了第五年，学校稍稍脱缰的时候——”
“哦，这么说它*确实*脱缰了？”克洛斯问。

===Original===
“Between ourselves, yes,” admitted Mr. Seymour.
“What sort of rows?” asked Trevor.
Mr. Seymour couldn’t explain exactly. Nothing, as it were, definite﻿—as yet. No actual complaints so far. But still﻿—well, trouble﻿—yes, trouble.
“For instance,” he said, “a boy in my house, Linton﻿—you remember him?﻿—is moving in society at this moment with a swollen lip and minus a front tooth. Of course, I know nothing about it, but I fancy he got into trouble in the town.
